# Blister Winter Buyer's Guide 2025-2026 (11th Edition) — Notes

Source: txt/2025-2026.clean (15,127 lines). Spec format: measured dims (length) | stated radius | measured weight/ski | tip/camber/tail profile | measured length | mount point (from true center / from tail) | lengths | core.

## 1. Structure, TOC, editorial framing (src lines 1-290)

- Cover: Paul Forward skiing the J Skis Best Friend, Chugach, AK (photo Charlie Renfro). Most people pictured are Blister reviewers; some photos from Blister Summit 2025.
- Ads: Elevation Hotel (Mt. Crested Butte); Blister Summit 2026 (April 13-17, 2026; test next year's gear, ride with reviewers, panels); BLISTER+ membership (12-month $0-deductible $25k/injury insurance across many sports, 1-on-1 gear recs, deals, Buyer's Guide, Community/Flash reviews, Deep Dive comparisons, new Resort Guides, Summit discount); Powder7 (Golden CO, used+new gear, boot fitting).
- "How to use this guide" (Jonathan Ellsworth): guide = digestible shortcut to 1500-3000+ word online reviews. Blister Spectrums rank skis within a class relative to each other (close = similar; several spots apart = meaningful). Clickable READ MORE links, GEAR 101 links, glossary. No advertising money from manufacturers reviewed. Multiple days in varied conditions on each product. "Think before you buy": older gear still good, many Best Of picks unchanged for years, environmental impact — keep using gear you're happy with. Skis listed narrowest to widest. No MSRP listed (misleading).
- "How to interpret our specs" (example K2 Mindbender 99Ti: 133.3-98.3-119.3 @184; 19.6 m; 2085/2120 g; 60/2.5/26 mm; -10.6 cm / 81.4 cm from tail; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass). Measured dims = widest shovel, narrowest waist, widest tail. Weight = both skis. Profile = tip splay / camber / tail splay when decambered (see Rocker 101). Measured length = straight tape tip-to-tail. Mount point relative to true center (measured length/2) and from tail; measure from tail since topsheets shift.
- TABLE OF CONTENTS: Articles — 6 About This Guide; 12 Letter from the Editor; 202 Ski-Quiver Selections; 278 Best Of Awards; 287 Index of Skis. Product reviews — 14 50/50 Backcountry & Resort; 28 Backcountry Touring; 44 Women's All-Mtn More Stable; 60 Women's All-Mtn More Forgiving; 75 Women's Powder; 84 Frontside; 104 All-Mtn More Stable; 128 All-Mtn More Forgiving; 148 All-Mtn Chargers; 155 All-Mtn Freestyle; 170 Park; 178 Powder More Directional; 189 Powder More Playful; 208 Ski Boots; 231 Touring Bindings & Skins; 244 Snowboards & Splitboards; 264 Apparel; 271 Helmets & Goggles.
- Letter from the Editor, "New Things" (Ellsworth): 11th edition; tens of thousands of hours testing. Theme: presence, openness, embracing ignorance. He'll snowboard for the first time this season, try flat pedals on MTB, did his first gravel race (Rebecca's Private Idaho). Argues being a beginner — setting aside ego, being bad at something — keeps egos in check, opens new joy and empathy; challenges readers to try something new.

## 2. Products

### 50/50 Skis: Backcountry & Resort (src ~286-)
Class overview: for one setup used for lift-accessed and touring. Dedicated setups still better (durability, stability, weight, safety), but AT gear advances make it the best time ever for a single setup. Weight is the main tradeoff: lighter = harder in typical inbounds conditions, easier uphill. These skis perform "fairly well" to "quite well" in resort conditions — heavier than dedicated touring skis, lighter than All-Mountain/Powder skis (but usually less damp/stable). Decide your compromises. BLISTER+ members can get personalized recs.

- **Völkl Blaze 94** — 132.5-94-118 (179); radii 20/40/17/22 m; 1613/1631 g; 59/2.5/25; 178.7 cm; -13.3 / 76.1 from tail; 151-186; poplar/paulownia/beech + recycled PET stringer + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Touring-weight but shines on corduroy; eager carver, very impressive edge hold; less easy to release/pivot in tight/grabby terrain; nervous in crud at speed but energetic. For light setup that rewards on firm smooth terrain, e.g., resort uphilling.
- **Norse Enduro 100** — 125.1-99.4-113 (188); 24 m; 1933/1943 g; 65/4.5/24; 187.1; -8.1 / 85.5; 164-188; ash/poplar/balsa + 2 layers aluminum + fiberglass. One of most capable for aggressive skiers wanting off-piste precision in firm/shallow snow; shines in moguls and tight trees (deep rocker, long radius, low swing weight), predictable in weird BC snow. Not forgiving backseat; needs speed/effort to lay over on groomers but strong edge hold. Less experienced → Bent 100; carvers → Blaze 104.
- **Atomic Bent 100** — 129-99.5-119.5 (186); 19.5 m; 1886/1925 g; 56.5/11.5/33.5; 182.9; -7.5 / 84; 158-186; poplar + fiberglass. A "33/33/33" ski: light all-mountain, downhill touring, even park. Drive shovels or ski upright; surprisingly solid on edge for weight. Most precise/directional of the Bent series, but more balanced/freestyle-friendly than most here. Very adaptable.
- **Elan Ripstick 102 Black Edition** — 137.5-101.5-118 (182); 18.8 m; 1776/1817 g; 51.5/6.5/17; 181.1; -7.8 / 82.8; 161-189; poplar/paulownia + 4 carbon rods + carbon strips + carbon weave + flax tip layer + fiberglass. Light, agile, precise; not eager for tight slow carves vs Blaze 104 but exceptional edge hold once on edge; rewards dynamic hop-turn style over smearing; not smooth/planted in crud; very nimble. Bent 100/Summit 106 more forgiving backseat. Standard Ripsticks for more forgiving ride.
- **J Skis Escalator** — 131.5-102-123 (182); 18.6 m; 1750/1761 g; 73/5/36; 180.2; -7 / 83; 162-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Accessible flex, light, do-everything shape; not great in harsh snow but soft surfy ends make controlled slow turns easy; nice suspension for weight, more predictable in variable snow than stiffer light skis (Blaze 94/Enduro). For directional or playful skiers wanting light, forgiving, maneuverable. More float → Slacker; more edge hold/stiffer → Bent 100.
- **Völkl Blaze 104** — 142-103.5-127.5 (186); radii 21/45/19/24 m; 1806/1864 g; 57.5/4/26.5; 186.2; -12.1 / 81; 158-186; same core as Blaze 94. Sharp piste-oriented personality; rewards aggressive edge angles on consistent snow; knocked around in crud; easy to flick (low swing weight) but needs directional stance/shovel pressure to release tails; floats well in pow, slarvy in soft snow. Few as exciting carving corduroy/corn. For soft/deep, Blaze 114 is more maneuverable.
- **Ferreol Pionnier 104** — 136.2-104.8-126.7 (188); 17 m; 1900/1917 g; 57/9.5/34; 188.1; -9.8 / 84.3; 164-188; poplar + cherry underfoot + carbon & fiberglass. Predictable, nimble across conditions; short radius + tapered shovels for carving and slashing, stiff midsection and less-tapered tail = secure edge even in bigger turns; nice suspension for weight. Size down if touring a lot. Alternative to Blaze 104 / Ripstick 102 Black for off-piste and suspension. More forgiving → Summit 106/Slacker; more soft-snow maneuverability → Ferreol Surfeur 112.
- **Wagner Summit 106** — 140.9-105.1-126.9 (186); 19.9 m; 2008/2065 g; 72/1.5/31.5; 184.4; -10.6 / 81.6; 165-186 (custom avail.); aspen/maple + carbon fiber torsion box + fiberglass. Standout of Wagner's semi-custom "Factory" line; intuitive immediately; deep low rocker, minimal taper = loose in tight spaces yet great carver for width. Vs Voss Z'N: more damp, forgiving, better carver, slightly less stable in soft snow. Not lightest but hard to unsettle. Full custom available.
- **DPS Kaizen 105** — 135.4-105.2-119.1 (184); 18 m; 1867/1908 g; 52/7/26; 183.5; -9.7 / 82; 155-189; poplar/ash + carbon laminate. DPS's do-it-all series; impressive damping-to-weight; less harsh on firm than light competitors; tapered ends/moderate rocker = maneuverable; easier to bend than old Pagodas without less stability. Like a looser/lighter Summit 106 or stiffer/more directional Escalator. For directional skiers prioritizing maneuverability over precision/stability.
- **Åsnes Voss Z'N** — 132.7-105.7-119.2 (187); 22 m; 1897/1917 g; 65/4/12; 185.7; mount "Boot Center" -9.6 / 83.3, "All-Mountain" -12 / 80.9; 167, 176, 187; poplar + carbon. Low swing weight + powerful directional flex; easy to pivot, stiff flat tail gives edge hold/support at speed, security on steep firm; great flotation; not engaging initiating carves at moderate speeds; not forgiving (see Escalator/Slacker). Like Majesty Havoc 110 Carbon (Voss more maneuverable in deep snow, less stable in chop).
- **Meier Crosscut BC** — 141.9-105.8-125.5 (185); 19 m; 1919/1928 g; 53/8/9; 184.8; -9.5 / 82.5; 178, 185; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + innegra & fiberglass. Surfy tapered rockered tips + much less rockered tail/mostly cambered — directional like Voss Z'N but less punishing backseat. Tighter radius = engaging on piste (though Summit 106/Blaze 104 carve better). Excels in soft snow/tight trees; good suspension. Less directional/forgiving → Summit 106.
- **Cardiff Goat 108 Enduro** — 128.5-107.5-121 (186); 24 m; 1852/1883 g; 76.5/5.5/52; 183.9; -4.6 / 87.4; 174-186; poplar/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Freeride/freestyle blend; deep rocker = easy slashes/drifts from forward or centered stance; surprisingly strong grip; for playful skiers more than suspension seekers; stiff midsection makes it more capable at speed than Bent 110. Newcomer brand, well-rounded.
- **Renoun Citadel 107** — 137-107.5-124.5 (184); 19.5 m; 1672/1740 g; 69.5/5/27.5; 182.2; -10.3 / 80.8; 163-191; aspen + titanal binding reinforcement + VibeStop (non-Newtonian) + carbon. Feels softer at low speeds, stiffer/damper under high forces; directional, floats well for width, turns easily in soft snow; on firm snow more damp than weight suggests; struggles vs heavy skis in deep chop; not best groomer carver. One of the lightest Blister would still use lift-served in softer snow.
- **J Skis Slacker** — 135-109.2-127.1 (188); 21.2 m; 1905/1919 g; 80/2/39; 185.4; -7.7 / 85; 169-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Easygoing, forgiving flex, deep rocker, progressive mount, instantly intuitive; retains J's predictability/playfulness in lighter package; floats well, not punishing backseat, easy on edge on firm. Stiffer/bigger turns → Havoc 110 Carbon etc.; freestyle → Bent 110. Broad appeal.
- **Majesty Havoc 110 Carbon** — 142.8-109.3-126.5 (186); radii 18/20/23/19; 1820/1821 g; 70/5/18; 184.9; -9.9 / 82.6; 166-191; paulownia/poplar + carbon/aramid stringers + carbon & fiberglass. One of more stable/directional light freeride options; composed at moderate speed in crud and high speed in consistent snow; not forgiving; impressive flotation; powerful carves on corn/soft groomers with grip and rebound. For strong directional skiers; also a Ti variant (continued).
  The Havoc 110 Ti variant shares the design but adds metal for better suspension/high-speed stability in rough snow.
- **Norse Freeride 110** — 132-109.4-122.1 (192); 26 m; 1973/2047 g; 67/3/33; 191; -7.2 / 88.3; 176, 184, 192; ash/poplar/balsa + 2 aluminum layers + fiberglass. Powerful yet maneuverable for steep technical terrain, light enough to tour; planes in low-density pow, not out of place on firm; reasonably damp in chop with light swing weight. Less directional alternative to Havoc 110 Carbon, or stiffer/more precise 4FRNT Hoji. Not for carving priority.
- **Atomic Bent 110** — 133.2-109.5-124.6 (188); 19 m; 1863/1872 g; 68/5/64; 186.3; -3 / 90.1; 164-188; poplar + fiberglass. One of the most playful here; more a narrower Bent Chetler 120 than a wider Bent 100; very surfy/nimble; nearly centered mount — not for directional skiers; good edge hold for class but knocked around in crud. More directional/stable → Line Vision 114.
- **Folsom Giver** (50/50 build) — 137.5-109.5-128.5 (186); 25 m; 1947/1960 g; 69.5/9/25.5; 184.4; -9.9 / 82.3; 184-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + 70/30 fiberglass/carbon. Giver shape minimizes deflection; rocker/camber/rocker version adds bite and pop on firm vs reverse-camber Givers (which are better in grabby snow). Good if Katana V-Werks/Hoji appeal but you dislike fully rockered looseness. Stable in soft-ish snow, agile in tight terrain. Folsom can build lighter; see Chargers section Giver for ultra-stable resort build.
- **Ferreol Surfeur 112** — 140-111.5-129.5 (184); 17 m; 1859/1864 g; 64/4/37; 183.4; -7.7 / 84; 176, 184; poplar + titanal binding plate + carbon stringers + flax laminate (no fiberglass; see GEAR:30 Ep.248). Impressively damp for weight; tapered and deeply rockered = maneuverable/playful; still predictable/stable in soft-ish snow from pow to spring. Not for big fast open turns, but more engaging than most at moderate speed; great in soft snow in tight terrain with broad appeal.
- **4FRNT Hoji** — 128.2-111.6-119.3 (184); 30 m (all lengths); 2060/2075 g; 73/0/26; 182.5; -6.6 / 84.7; 170-191; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Reverse camber, long radius, forward-ish mount: rapid pivots/slashes yet happy making big turns from centered/forward stance; flat-ish middle = reliable edge hold on firm; not an exciting carver but predictable. Most adaptable of Hoji's collection in and out of resort; 4FRNT Raven is lighter/narrower analog.
- **Völkl Katana V-Werks** — 142.5-112-132 (184); 23.5 m; 1959/1975 g; 68/0/18; 182.2; -13.9 / 77.2; 177, 184, 191; poplar + ash underfoot + carbon. Over a decade in production; go-to for lightweight strong yet maneuverable in technical terrain; surprisingly damp for carbon; long radius, set-back mount, no taper = little deflection; great resort pow ski and powerful carver (not quickest edge to edge); needs forward stance; excellent stability for weight. More easygoing → Blaze 114.
- **Line Vision 114** — 138.5-113-131 (183); 23 m; 1802/1819 g; 63/2.5/55.5; 181.9; -5.2 / 85.8; 159-189; aspen/paulownia + flax, carbon & fiberglass. Lighter backcountry remix of Optic 114: floats well, easy to slash/slarve, forward or centered stance; much less composed in tracked chop than Optic. For those between very directional and very freestyle skis, maneuverability over firm-snow precision.
- **Dynafit Tigard 114** — 140.5-113.5-129.5 (188); 23.5 m; 2011/2023 g; 65.5/4.5/31.5; 186.7; -7.8 / 85.6; 180, 188; poplar + carbon stringers. Dynafit's biggest/boldest: floats well, easy to drift; surprisingly stiff/precise on firmer snow — good for driving through layered BC snow, but not forgiving backseat and harsh on really firm. Stable for weight from centered or forward stance; comfortable in the air. Easygoing alternatives: Surfeur 112, Blaze 114.
- **Völkl Blaze 114** — 153.4-114-136 (184); radii 39/18/29 m; 1938/2008 g; 68/3/22; 183.5; -11.8 / 80; 166-192; poplar/beech + recycled PET stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Wide soft deeply rockered tips plane easily (rearward mount); intuitive in soft snow without specific stance; can carve clean on piste; not a charger but respectable suspension for weight. More intuitive than most for directional skiers; not for air-oriented balanced skiers. Broadly enjoyed on fresh-snow days.
- **Salomon S/Lab QST X** — 140-116-127 (184); 25 m; 1973/1997 g; 66.5/6/26; 183; -5.3 / 86.2; 178, 184, 192; karuba/poplar + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + flax/carbon + fiberglass. Extremely surfy; most maneuverable/least directional QST; easy slashes, long carves from centered stance in pow; chop → dynamic approach; on firm less in element (stiff midsection, short effective edge, long radius encourage skidding). BG 118 Tour more stable/better for forward stance; QST X one of best here for big slarvy arcs + quick slashes in deep snow.
- **ON3P Billy Goat 118 Tour** — 145.5-118.5-128 (191); 29.6 m; 1949/1956 g; 79/5/35; 190.8; -10.2 / 85.2; 181, 186, 191; bamboo/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Light version of long-standing Billy Goat; standard BG 118 better for blowing through chop, but Tour handles it very well for weight; directional yet drifts/slashes like freestyle skis; prefers taking it easy when pow runs out. Impressive flotation/maneuverability/stability combo.
- **Atomic Bent Chetler 120** — 143.5-119.5-134.5 (192); 20 m; 1941/1989 g; 62.5/8.5/57.5; 190.2; -2.6 / 92.5; 176, 184, 192; poplar + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. A reference playful pow ski: very surfy, butters, balanced/agile in air, stiff midsection supports landings; prefers centered stance; not for big fast turns in variable; nervous/chattery on firm. One of best bets for maneuverability/freestyle pow.

**Blister Spectrums — 50/50** (top → bottom):
- Best in Variable → Worst: Katana V-Werks; Folsom Giver; Wagner Summit 106; Majesty Havoc 110 Carbon; ON3P Billy Goat 118 Tour; Völkl Blaze 114; Norse Freeride 110; 4FRNT Hoji; Atomic Bent 100; Ferreol Pionnier 104; Salomon QST X; J Slacker; Åsnes Voss Z'N; Norse Enduro 100; Meier Crosscut BC; DPS Kaizen 105; Renoun Citadel 107; Dynafit Tigard 114; Cardiff Goat 108 Enduro; J Escalator; Elan Ripstick 102 Black; Line Vision 114; Ferreol Surfeur 112; Bent Chetler 120; Blaze 104; Bent 110; Blaze 94.
- Most Inbounds-Oriented → Most Touring-Oriented: Katana V-Werks; Summit 106; Bent 100; Blaze 104; Blaze 94; Ripstick 102 Black; Escalator; Enduro 100; Blaze 114; Giver; Freeride 110; Crosscut BC; Kaizen 105; Pionnier 104; BG 118 Tour; Bent Chetler 120; Surfeur 112; QST X; Bent 110; Slacker; Citadel 107; Hoji; Goat 108; Tigard 114; Havoc 110 Carbon; Vision 114; Voss Z'N.
- Most Playful → Least Playful: Bent Chetler 120; Bent 110; Surfeur 112; Vision 114; QST X; Slacker; Escalator; BG 118 Tour; Goat 108 Enduro; Bent 100; Hoji; Citadel 107; Freeride 110; Enduro 100; Kaizen 105; Pionnier 104; Tigard 114; Summit 106; Blaze 114; Crosscut BC; Giver; Ripstick 102 Black; Blaze 104; Blaze 94; Voss Z'N; Havoc 110 Carbon; Katana V-Werks.
(Column assignment reconstructed from interleaved 3-column table; rows read left/middle/right.)

### Backcountry Touring Skis (src ~1034-)
Class overview (Paul Forward photo): it's about the uphill/downhill balance for your objectives; personal preference. Don't fixate on ski weight — consider total weight on feet (skis+bindings+boots+skins). If not doing long tours/racing, heavier gear generally gives better stability, suspension/damping, and safety features on the descent.

- **Majesty Superwolf** — 129.9-90.6-110.1 (178); 18 m; stated 1540 g; 62/4/14; 176.3; -9.9 / 78.3; 154-184; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. One of the most predictable light touring skis; less harsh on firm and more stable at speed than expected; tapered subtly rockered tip intuitive in soft/variable; handled steep icy lines with little drama. Corn arcs to steep hop turns; reviewers happily carry ~200 g more than lighter options (incl. Superwolf Carbon) for its downhill reliability.
- **La Sportiva Salto** — 120-91.5-107 (178); 18 m; 1384/1434 g; 63.5/7/15.5; 177.2; -7.9 / 80.7; 164, 178, 188; poplar/paulownia + aluminum stringers + carbon. Tapered ends, lots of tip rocker, low-rise tail = maneuverable for width with solid edge hold; ~1400 g sweet spot for bigger objectives; tips can get twitchy in big variable turns; more maneuverable in crust/sastrugi than Zero G 96/Crux 93. Prefers suspension/maneuverability over min weight/max precision. Fresh-snow → Tempo, Capo.
- **Head Crux 93 Pro** — 130-92.5-112.5 (177); 18.6 m; 1124/1260 g; 62.5/6.5/17; 175.5; -10.4 / 77.3; 163-184; karuba + graphene/carbon/fiberglass. "Scalpel" — light, precise, strong traditional directional; good stability-to-weight in consistent snow (groomers, windbuff, corn) with support/rebound; in rough snow needs attentive, conservative pilot. Lighter than most here; Superwolf/Salto more predictable for a couple hundred grams.
- **Majesty Superpatrol** — 129-94.1-111 (177); 19 m; 1630/1635 g; 61/8/9; 175.8; -14.5 / 73.4; 161-185; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. Long effective edge, very set-back mount ("cut-off tail technology") so you can size down; driving shovels = impressive stability/flotation for size, short tails snap around; less intuitive with centered stance. Stiffer, more stable than Superwolf; needs more speed. For directional skiers liking lots of ski in front.
- **Blizzard Zero G 96** (redesigned) — 126-94.5-110 (178); 20 m; 1289/1329 g; 63/4/13; 175.3; -9.6 / 78; 164-185; paulownia/poplar + ash binding insert + carbon & fiberglass. Light, traditional, directional; not damp or maneuverable in variable but can be pushed hard in consistent snow; a bit more accessible/forgiving than Zero G 95. Best carving smooth snow — corn, light pow, near-ice. Like a more versatile Crux 99 Pro or lighter/less damp MTN 96 Carbon.
- **Salomon MTN 96 Carbon** — 130-95-116 (182); 19 m; 1451/1502 g; 68/6/13.5; 180.8; -11 / 79.3; 166-188; karuba + titanal binding reinforcement + cork tip inserts + carbon & basalt. Reliable edge hold, good suspension in firm/shallow; much like all-time favorite MTN Explore 95 but lighter/more maneuverable (deeper tail rocker, softer flex). Superpatrol more composed at speed in variable; Zero G 96 for icy couloirs. Predictable generalist.
- **Line Vision 96** — 131.5-95-120 (184); 17 m; 1634/1658 g; 64.5/6/31.5; 182.6; -6.3 / 85; 160-184; aspen/paulownia + flax/carbon/fiberglass. Outlier: freeride-influenced (like Optic 96 but light core); much easier to pivot/slash than light touring skis; good for neutral/centered stance; smooths harsh snow better than lighter options; not max edge hold. For those finding touring skis too directional/serious; bigger drifty arcs → 4FRNT Raven.
- **Head Crux 99 Pro** — 132-98.5-116.5 (184); 22.7 m; 1254/1327 g; 60.5/7.5/17; 182.6; -11.5 / 79.8; 163-184; karuba + graphene/carbon/fiberglass. Uphill efficiency + downhill precision; one of the lighter in class; shallow rocker + strong flex = edge hold/power over playfulness; nervous in rough snow at speed (long radius reduces twitchiness). Rare precision/power at low weight. Smoother → MTN 96 Carbon.
- **ZAG Slap 104 Lite** — 136-103-125 (182); 19 m; 1741/1753 g; 70.5/7/47; 180.2; mounts Centered -3.7/86.3, Progressive -5.5/84.6, Recommended -7.2/82.8; 170-188; poplar/paulownia + rubber inserts + flax/carbon/fiberglass. Light version of playful adaptable Slap 104; maneuverable end of touring skis, surprisingly fun carver, works driving shovels, neutral, or switch; not exceptionally light but nice suspension in crust/refreeze; fun at low pitch/speed yet composed.
- **4FRNT Raven** — 120.5-103.1-111.7 (184); 29 m (all lengths); 1736/1767 g; 64/0/25.5; 182.2; -5.7 / 85.4; 170-190; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Unconventional, long-time reviewer favorite; can be pushed hard yet one of easiest to maneuver (beginners to experts); no camber = easy smears in weird snow; long radius avoids twitchiness/hookiness; excellent 4-Lock skin attachment. Not for tight carves; camber bite → Slap 104 Lite, QST Echo 106. Primary reference ski for variable BC snow.
- **Fauna Pioneer** — 123.4-103.2-118.3 (184); 22 m; 1787/1793 g; 63.5/10/51; 184; -7.1 / 84.9; 175, 184; paulownia/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Freestyle shape (tapered, progressive mount, twin tail) with stout construction (lots of camber, very stiff underfoot); extremely easy to pivot in soft snow, slices chop/pow; decent edge hold with slightly centered stance; good suspension for weight but less composed than Raven in firm crud. For playful center-mount resort skiers; directional → Locator 104, Wagner Summit 105.
- **Elan Ripstick Tour 104** — 129.4-103.4-121.9 (187); 25.4 m; 1576/1592 g; 52.5/5.5/20.5; 184.3; -6.3 / 85.9; 173, 180, 187; paulownia/poplar + carbon rod + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Purpose-built touring Ripstick: lighter, softer, longer radius, more centered mount; energetic and maneuverable; adapts to stances, releases in grabby snow; less quick/snappy carving (see Slap 104 Lite). For pow-focused touring blending directional/playful traits.
- **Armada Locator 104** — 130-103.5-121.5 (178); 22 m; 1442/1474 g; 49/6/19; 177; -10 / 78.7; 162-186; caruba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Very good in fresh snow for width; stable/versatile for low weight but needs attentive pilot in rough snow; easy at slow speed; big fast turns or slashes in pow/corn; good firm edge hold; rearward mount/soft shovels = good flotation; not freestyle (unlike ARV/ARW). Directional but surfy; see wider Locator 112 for steep/big terrain.
- **Line Vision 104** — 131.5-103-123 (185); 19 m; 1703/1709 g; 61.5/3.5/41; 183.9; -5.2 / 86.6; 165-185; aspen/paulownia + flax/carbon/fiberglass. Freestyle DNA, surfy/playful, comes alive at low speed but OK at speed; big sweet spot; less composed than Raven at high speed; not for precise stiff directional skiers. Like the heavier Optic 104, balances playfulness and composure; good for those finding touring skis boring in mellow terrain.
- **La Sportiva Tempo** (overhauled, big weight reduction) — 131.5-103.5-121.5 (190); 19 m; 1558/1589 g; 74.5/6.5/19.5; 187.8; -8.5 / 85.4; 164, 179, 190; paulownia/PVC foam + aluminum stringers + fiberglass ... (continued). Playful design (tapered, deep rocker, moderately progressive mount); not great in firm/rough, floats and turns quickly in pow, corn, windbuff; neither super directional nor needing centered stance. Alternative to Vision 104 / Weston Summit 105 ...
  (Tempo cont.: core ends "+ fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon laminate"; good alternative for those with a more directional stance who prioritize maneuverability and low weight over max suspension/stability.)
- **Wagner Summit 105** — 134.7-104.4-122.6 (185); 23.8 m; 1705/1708 g; 72/5/31; 183.2; -9.8 / 81.8; 164-185 (custom avail.); paulownia + carbon & fiberglass. Not bespoke but a carefully crafted all-rounder; deep rocker = easy to slide, minimal taper = fun carving with some speed; big sweet spot but prefers forward stance. For directional skiers who like the Raven idea but want camber/tighter radius, or Agent 3 fans wanting more forgiveness. Standout: does everything a generalist touring ski should.
- **Head Crux 105 Pro** — 132.5-104.5-121.5 (184); 25.6 m; 1357/1380 g; 57/10/24.5; 182.7; -11.5 / 79.8; 170, 177, 184; karuba + graphene/carbon/fiberglass. Minimalist pow touring ski; traditional directional, long radius and slight taper boost soft-snow maneuverability vs narrower Crux; needs forward stance and good technique, knocked around in variable. More playful at similar weight → Alp Tracks 106; more suspension → Agent 3. Great for long pow-hunting missions.
- **Weston Summit Carbon 105** — 133.8-105.4-123.8 (186); 16.5 m; 1671/1675 g; 71/2/48; 184.2; -7.8 / 84.3; 156-186; paulownia + aluminum stringers + carbon. Fun without steep/fast terrain; soft, rockered, tapered ends — easy tight turns, forgiving, slashy; nice suspension for weight. Not for huge fast turns in difficult snow but not beginner-only (reviewers use it for most touring). Excellent for less experienced BC skiers and mellow/tree laps in high avy danger.
- **Faction Agent 3** — 133.2-105.5-123.5 (183); 21 m; 1725/1774 g; 69.5/5/24; 181.6; mounts Newschool -9.6/81.2, Progressive -11.1/79.7, Classic -12.6/78.2; 164-188; karuba + carbon weave + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. "Shape of a charger, weight of a climber"; same shape as metal Dancer 3 but lighter/more accessible; big sweet spot; best for traditional forward stance — maneuverable, holds edge really well, damp for weight, lively; doesn't punish sloppiness too quickly. Surfier → La Machine 3. For precision/stability/energy.
- **Movement Alp Tracks 106** — stated 134-106-122 (186); 22.5 m; stated 1460 g; 61/4.5/15.5; 184.2; -8.8 / 83.3; 170, 178, 186; paulownia + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon. Ultralight for uphill-efficient pow chasers; easy slarves, soft tips plane; in firm/rough needs attentive proficient skier. Fantastic for max fun in good snow with min effort; slightly calmer → Locator 104, La Machine 3.
- **Salomon S/Lab QST Echo 106** — 138.8-105.7-125.9 (181); 19 m; 1820/1824 g; 54/4/26; 180; mounts "MTN Binding" -8/82, "Alpine + Shift" -9/81; 157-189; karuba/poplar + cork tips/tails + basalt & fiberglass. Excellent do-everything for downhill-oriented BC skiers; calm and composed in unpredictable snow without demanding speed/technique; highly maneuverable, stance-adaptable; capable in rough snow. Great if Raven intrigues but unsure about reverse camber. 100-300 g heavier than many but worth it for adaptability from deep pow to corn to crusts.
- **Moment Wildcat Tour 108** — 134.5-107.7-127.1 (184); 24 m; 1692/1715 g; 72/5.5/65; 180.5; -7.1 / 83.2; 174-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Blurs freestyle playfulness and directional stability; can drive shovels on firm, stable for weight, but also centered and balanced in air; composed at speed vs playful peers; needs some speed to engage (tweaked flex helps); surfy, reliable edge on smooth firm. Aggressive forward stance → Agent 3/Ronin 108 (Ronin easier at slow speed). For creative descents.
- **ON3P Woodsman 108 Tour** — 138-108-127.5 (186); 21.0 m; 1766/1767 g; 67/4.5/40.5; 186; -8.1 / 84.9; 176, 181, 186; bamboo/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Lighter Woodsman 108: maneuverable + stable, not stance-picky; less damp than heavy version but stands out among light skis, not easily unsettled. QST Echo 106 more precise carver; Wildcat Tour 108 more playful. Rarely out of its element.
- **Ronin 108 LTE** — 140.8-108.1-124 (185); 20.5 m (all lengths); 1637/1647 g; 59/6/22; 185.4; -11.3 / 81.4; 160-192; balsa/bamboo + carbon & flax. Dependable well-rounded directional tourer; big shovels float, more precise than many similar widths; low weight with good suspension and pop; predictable with forward stance; near Agent 3 in support/stability/carving but more accessible. Ronin also makes heavier "MFD" variants.
- **Faction La Machine 3** — 131.7-108.7-123.8 (184); 21 m; 1560/1594 g; 68/3/40; 181.9; mounts Newschool -8/83, Progressive -9.5/81.5, Classic -11/80; 165-190; paulownia + carbon & fiberglass. Looser/more playful than Agent 3 for directional skiers; impressively light yet predictable in BC conditions for weight; impressive pow ski for width. QST Echo 106 better for high-speed rough composure. For those curious about reverse camber (Raven) but wanting lighter / keeping camber.
- **Atomic Backland 109** (and women's Backland 108 W) — 134.5-109-123.5 (184); 19 m; 1526/1569 g; 56.5/10.5/24; 182.8; -6.6 / 84.8; 176, 184, 189; poplar/karuba + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Mix of Atomic touring and Bent lines; vs Backland 107: less rearward mount, more tail rocker, softer — far more accessible, stance-versatile; not precise on firm or stable in rough. Better suspension → Woodsman 108 Tour, QST Echo 106. Efficient up, fun down in soft snow.
- **Moment Deathwish Tour 112** — 136-111-128 (184); 25 m; 1660/1680 g; 66/3/64; 183.4; -6 / 85.7; 174-190; paulownia/beech + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Playful freestyle shape + strong flex + "Triple Camber"; unconventional but intuitive; knocked around more than non-Tour Deathwish (great 50/50 option) but damp/stable for weight; dual personality: excellent edge hold driving tips, easy slash/spin from neutral stance. Not for fans of rearward-mounted minimally rockered skis.
- **Armada Locator 112** — 135.7-111.6-127.8 (187); 26 m; 1608/1661 g; 58/6/23; 186; -10.4 / 83; 166-187; karuba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Surprising downhill performance; even 120+ mm fans found it an excellent all-round BC pow ski; balanced in drifts despite -10 cm mount; very good stability-to-weight; feels big on firm, longer radius favors big turns. Regularly deep blower → go wider.
- **Armada ARV 116 JJ UL** — 139-116-134 (185); 18 m; 1820/1835 g; 71/6/64; 183.5; -5 / 87; 165-192; karuba + fiberglass. Even more playful than regular ARV 116 JJ and lighter; very rockered, tapered, soft ends = forgiving, butters, slashes, floats; great for BC booter sessions; easy in fresh snow regardless of angle; struggles/deflects in firm/variable. Less freestyle-biased → Wildcat Tour 118.
- **Faction La Machine 4** — 139-116.5-130.5 (185); 22 m; 1772/1788 g; 72.5/6.5/34; 183.1; mounts Newschool -9.8/81.7, Progressive -11.3/80.2, Classic -12.8/78.7; 171-191; paulownia + carbon & fiberglass. 117 mm version of La Machine 5; now with significant camber and more rearward directional mount — less intuitive drifting in steep deep snow than the original reverse-camber La Machine 5, but still worthwhile light pow ski. Best with stiffer freeride-touring boot; ideal range broader than La Sportiva Capo.
- **La Sportiva Capo** — 141.5-117-131 (189); 21 m; 1603/1611 g; 66.5/6/20.5; 187; -11.6 / 81.9; 166, 178, 189; paulownia/PVC foam + aluminum stringers + fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon. Very light for size; directional mount, early tip taper, lots of camber; knocked around in variable; decent flotation; best in moderate-angle terrain; harder to break into drifts (like La Machine). Balanced feel → Wildcat Tour 118, Heritage Lab BC120. Carves well on firm. For a light fat ski with traditional descent, not steep lines.
- **Moment Wildcat Tour 118** — 142.9-117.8-134.4 (190); 27 m; 1885/1914 g; 73/6.5/65; 186.5; -6.8 / 86.4; 174-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Loyal following (incl. reviewers): playful pow ski that doesn't wilt fast/choppy; fun surfy ride, adequate flotation, excellent stability for weight in variable; long radius predictable in big turns, easy to throw sideways; balanced for playful approach. One of best wide skis here — uncommonly adaptable.
- **Whitedot Ragnarok Carbonlite** (118) — 143.1-118.1-130.1 (190); 30 m; 1854/1903 g; 81/2/42; 187.7; -8 / 85.9; 190 only; poplar/ash + flax/carbon/kevlar/fiberglass. Heritage from burly big-mountain comp skis; powerful/stable in long turns, easy to slash in trees with aggressive forward stance; heavy for touring but composure in variable and landing support unmatched by most; strong on firm but needs speed. Centered style → Wildcat Tour 118 or Ragnarok ASYM. For steep lines, big drops.
- **Heritage Lab BC120** (Swallowtail) — 144-121-139 (193); 27.2 m; 1852/1879 g; 60/0/37; 193; -8 / 88.5; 180, 187, 193; paulownia/poplar + carbon. Exceptional BC pow ski — Paul Forward's likely current favorite on the market; same shape/near rocker as R120ST but stiffer and much lighter; very intuitive in big terrain, long carves in soft snow, easy big drifts; excellent suspension-to-weight; composed at speed on bumpy runouts. Swallowtail may need skin tail mods. Not best in tight trees.
- **Moonlight Cruiser Carbon All-Terrain** — 145-121-135 (186); 26 m; 1631/1662 g; profile n/a; 184.5; -11.2 / 81; 177, 186; poplar/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Two constructions: Carbon Superlight (~1300 g @186, maybe lightest relative to size ever reviewed; twitchy/chattery outside untouched pow) and All-Terrain (still extremely light, very good suspension for weight in almost all conditions, composed in pow, drivable with <1200 g 2-buckle boots). One of most impressive lightweight pow-touring skis tested.
- **Heritage Lab UT122** ("Ultra Taper") — 136-124-130 (190); 34 m; 1836/1845 g; 75/0/20; 190; -7 / 88; 183, 190; paulownia/poplar + carbon. Unapologetically pow-specific reverse-camber reverse-sidecut; looser, more stance-adaptable and better in grabby crust/wet snow than Cruiser AT; ludicrously easy to pivot in deep pow; light yet damp. Not versatile — very short effective edge, long radius, no camber feel weird on firm. Specialist for deep fresh.

**Blister Spectrums — Backcountry Touring** (top → bottom):
- Best for Steep, Firm Terrain → Worst: Majesty Superwolf; Salomon MTN 96 Carbon; Blizzard Zero G 96; Majesty Superpatrol; Faction Agent 3; Head Crux 93 Pro; Crux 99 Pro; La Sportiva Salto; Crux 105 Pro; Ronin 108 LTE; Armada Locator 104; QST Echo 106; Wagner Summit 105; Elan Ripstick Tour 104; La Machine 3; ZAG Slap 104 Lite; La Sportiva Tempo; Line Vision 96; 4FRNT Raven; Deathwish Tour 112; Wildcat Tour 108; Woodsman 108 Tour; La Machine 4; Atomic Backland 109; Line Vision 104; Weston Summit Carbon 105; Movement Alp Tracks 106; Wildcat Tour 118; Fauna Pioneer; Whitedot Ragnarok CL; Heritage Lab BC120; Moonlight Cruiser AT; Armada Locator 112; La Sportiva Capo; Armada ARV 116 JJ UL; Heritage Lab UT122.
- Best in Crusty/Punchy Snow → Worst: Wildcat Tour 118; Ragnarok CL; BC120; Raven; Cruiser AT; Deathwish Tour 112; ARV 116 JJ UL; Locator 112; UT122; La Machine 4; Wildcat Tour 108; Woodsman 108 Tour; La Machine 3; QST Echo 106; Capo; Fauna Pioneer; Wagner Summit 105; Vision 104; Slap 104 Lite; Weston Summit Carbon 105; Locator 104; Ronin 108 LTE; Backland 109; Tempo; Vision 96; Ripstick Tour 104; Agent 3; Alp Tracks 106; MTN 96 Carbon; Superpatrol; Crux 105; Superwolf; Zero G 96; Salto; Crux 99; Crux 93.
- Best in Heavy, Wet Snow → Worst: UT122; BC120; Wildcat Tour 118; Ragnarok CL; ARV 116 JJ UL; Cruiser AT; Locator 112; Raven; La Machine 4; La Machine 3; Deathwish Tour 112; Wildcat Tour 108; Woodsman 108 Tour; QST Echo 106; Capo; Vision 104; Slap 104 Lite; Backland 109; Locator 104; Weston Summit Carbon 105; Wagner Summit 105; Fauna Pioneer; Ronin 108 LTE; Tempo; Agent 3; Vision 96; Ripstick Tour 104; Alp Tracks 106; Crux 105; MTN 96 Carbon; Superpatrol; Superwolf; Salto; Crux 99; Zero G 96; Crux 93.

### Women's All-Mountain Skis — More Stable (src ~2040-)
Class overview (Kristin Sinnott photo): Brands vary in women's/men's/unisex distinctions; some skis "women-specific", others not, but all tested by female reviewers. Women's all-mountain split into More Forgiving / More Stable plus Women's Powder. More Stable = generally stiffer, less tolerant of mistakes (esp. backseat), but more damp and stable at speed through tracked snow, variable, rough groomers; often heavier. Good edge hold, powerful turn finish; good mogul skis for strong skiers but punishing for less experienced; less appealing to playful/loose fans or those wanting easy slow-speed initiation. Read caveats — some are fairly easy.

- **Blizzard Black Pearl 84** — 124-83.5-107 (170); 14 m; 1690/1700 g; 70/5/18.5; 168.4; -10.9 / 73.3; 146-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Walks line between user-friendly and room to grow; excels on piste with easy intuitive initiation without perfect technique or speed; off piste needs shovel pressure to release tails (vs More Forgiving skis), not piste-only; aggressive skiers may find it lacking on firm/rough. (Text says "Black Pearl 88" makes a case for less experienced skiers improving or advanced skiers on moderate groomers — likely meant 84.) More support → Mantra 84 W.
- **Völkl Mantra 84 W** (replaces Yumi 84; Völkl rebranded all men's/women's all-mountain skis as "Mantra") — 129-83.5-109.5 (170); radii 26/15/23 m; 1721/1734 g; 45.5/4/11.5; 170.2; -11.9 / 73.3; 149-170; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Excellent precision, rewarding on piste; stiff and stable, not easy at slow speeds/firm moguls; lively rebound when pushed; viable off piste for proficient skiers. For advanced/experts: as precise/lively as dedicated carvers but OK in occasional bumps/trees.
- **Ferreol Explo 86** — 123.5-86.5-111.5 (174); 15 m; 1393/1419 g; 54/10.5/18; 173.9; -8.4 / 78.5; 156-174; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Punches above weight: stable, edge hold, versatile; design like wider all-mountain skis; uncommonly damp for weight, secure on edge; initiates carves less readily than BP 84 but some reviewers more confident on firm/cut-up snow; low swing weight, easy off piste; good for occasional resort uphill. Light finesse-focused daily resort ski.
- **Head Kore 87 Ti W** (new Kore Ti collection — big departure; much more damp, accessible, maneuverable than prior Kore) — 126.5-86.5-110 (170); 14.6 m; 1664/1671 g; 53/10/20; 169.1; -9 / 75.5; 149-170; poplar/PET + 2 titanal layers + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Narrow ski capable all over; not a standout carver but easy to skid on ice and carve in softer snow; not ultra-energetic; easy to flick off piste, less carving-focused than Mantra 84 W. For intermediate-advanced; wider Kore Ti for fresh/crud.
- **Blizzard Black Pearl 88** — 127-87.5-109 (170); 15 m; 1773/1785 g; 73.5/4.5/20.5; 168.3; -10.4 / 73.8; 146-176; (continued)... Long-time staple; versatile, very intuitive for firmer/shallow conditions; aggressive skiers may want more stability in rough; easy to get on edge for ex-racers and beginners; off piste can be hooky in variable, must stay over shovels...
  (BP 88 cont.) ...less demanding there than K2 Mindbender 89Ti W. Max suspension → Santa Ana 92 / Black Pearl 94. Core: poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Reliable for ease on groomers with off-trail versatility.
- **K2 Mindbender 89Ti W** — 129.5-88.5-113.5 (170); 14.2 m; 1762/1779 g; 49.5/4.5/13.5; 169.8; -10.1 / 74.9; 146-176; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Stability, precision, high-speed confidence; tuned for firm, carving-oriented, less playful than 99Ti W; snappy, encourages tight high-angle turns; best driving shovels, punishing backseat. BP 88/Ranger 90/Stance 88 more forgiving in big moguls; 89Ti more stable than last two, quicker than BP 88. For advanced-expert hard carvers.
- **Fischer Ranger 90** — 123.9-89.5-113.4 (170); 16 m; 1764/1794 g; 63/3/20; 168.6; -7.7 / 76.6; 156-184; poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Capable at speed without bulldozer technique; for experienced skiers wanting suspension but prioritizing maneuverability over max precision; as stable as Stance 88/MB 89Ti; softer shovels = easier initiation (vague on very firm); looser off piste; strong tail unforgiving for beginners in moguls. Less forgiving but more predictable in variable than BP 88.
- **Majesty Havoc 90 Ti** — 122.5-89.5-107.5 (178); radii 17/20/21/18; 1711/1712 g; 71/7/17.5; 176.8; -9.6 / 78.8; 164-184; poplar/ash + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Playful twist on directional; very rockered tapered tips — low swing weight, easy to smear in trees/moguls — at cost of firm edge hold and high-speed stability (Stance 88, Ranger 90, MB 89Ti better there); floats better, more nimble; strong tail needs forward stance; shines off groomers even in chalky/soft snow. More forgiving → Explo 86.
- **Salomon Stance W Pro 88** (renamed, same build) — 126.2-90-108 (174); 18 m; stated 1660 g; 55/6/12; 174.1; -9.6 / 77.5; 154-174; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + C/FX inserts + fiberglass. Middle ground of power and approachability; respectable suspension/high-speed composure but less demanding than peers; not for backseat steering. Ranger 90/Havoc 90 Ti easier to pivot; Stance more secure on edge yet easier in moguls than MB 89Ti. Balanced blend.
- **Shaggy's Belle 90** — 136-90-116 (174); 16.25-17.9 m; 1760/1771 g; 52/5/16; 173.7; -8.5 / 78; 156-174; ash/poplar + magnesium sheets + carbon & fiberglass. Carving-focused with more width/versatility than piste skis; quick initiation, precise; stiff minimally rockered tail finishes powerfully but hard to release/skid; more composed on cut-up groomers than narrower carvers. Santa Ana 92/Reliance 92 Ti more intuitive in tight off-piste. One of best ~90 mm for edge grip.
- **Armada Reliance 92 Ti** — 130-92-116 (172); 17 m; 1822/1843 g; 52.5/9/20; 171; -9.3 / 76.2; 156-180; poplar + titanal + "elastic compound" + fiberglass. Impressive high-speed composure via different recipe; some shovel rocker/taper = more comfortable in variable; needs more input/speed to get on edge but then solid and stable on rough groomers; OK centered stance, not forgiving backseat. More lively/maneuverable → Stance 88. If SA 92/BP 94 too demanding, consider this.
- **Head Kore 93 Ti W** (new) — 129-92.5-114 (170); 14.7 m; 1686/1719 g; 60/11/20; 169; -9 / 75.5; 149-170; poplar/PET + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Well-rounded, can be pushed but not demanding; more stable/predictable off piste than 87 Ti; Kore Ti skis not super eager to hook up but very predictable, fun on not-scraped snow; can punish backseat in tight moguls but more agile/forgiving than SA 92/BP 94. For intermediate-expert wanting a trusted setup.
- **Nordica Santa Ana 92** — 128-93.5-116 (179); 17.5 m; 2045/2070 g; 65/5.5/19.5; 177.6; -7 / 81.8; 150-179; poplar/beech + 1 partial titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Standout for stability, precision, smooth suspension; few more composed in crud; energetic when pushed — rewarding for aggressive skiers; not forgiving/nimble. Top pick for stability/precision; outlier among "women-specific" skis — refreshing for those let down by women's skis at speed.
- **Blizzard Black Pearl 94** — 132-93.5-114.5 (176); 17 m; 1933/1953 g; 76/6/20.5; 174.2; -10.4 / 76.7; 152-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Excellent high-speed composure plus approachable versatility; big step up from narrower BPs in stability and demands; lively, not hard to bend, a bit more forgiving than peers; one of most predictable in rough snow; more accessible than SA 92, more precise but less playful than MB 99Ti.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 92 W** — 127.5-93.5-117.5 (178); 17 m; 1762/1781 g; 71.5/5.5/23.5; 177.1; -8.6 / 80; 146-178; poplar/PU + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Substantial flex + modern shape; more you put in the more energetic; heavier skis calmer in rough, but M-Pro 92 agile, maneuverable for active style; not most precise on very firm; happy to pivot/slash/hop off piste (more than SA 92/BP 94). M-Pro 98 better at speed. Stands out for agility among <95 mm skis.
- (Ad: BLISTER+ testimonial — member's ACL surgery/PT covered.)
- **ZAG Harfang 96** — 131.4-95.3-115.7 (170); 17.5 m; 1788/1793 g; 50.5/5.5/11.5; 169.1; -10.9 / 73.6; 170-188; poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Classic directional, well executed; responds to forward stance; surprisingly agile in tight spots (short tails), stable in open terrain; less pop than Dancer 2/M-Pro 92 (less likely to kick you backseat). Vs Dancer 2: looser, slightly damper; Dancer livelier, lower swing weight. No clear weaknesses — safe bet.
- **Faction Dancer 2** — 126.7-95.6-116.6 (171); 17 m; 1787/1798 g; 71/4.5/23.5; 169; mounts Newschool -7.2/77.3, Progressive -8.7/75.8, Classic -10.2/74.3; 155-187; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Debunks metal stereotypes: less directional, more playful than BP 94 and M7 Mantra W; more agile, maneuverable, energetic in tight terrain; best forward but can ski centered on Newschool line. Strong carver not locked-in or sluggish. Smoother → Harfang 96; more off-piste → MB 99Ti.
- **Ferreol Explo 96** — 128.7-96-118.5 (172); 15 m; 1533/1557 g; 75/9/34; 170; -7.5 / 77.5; 156-182; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass & carbon. Also viable for touring; "3P camber" (extra camber sections fore/aft of binding) = strong firm grip (takes getting used to); tip/tail rocker, not-rearward mount, low swing weight = agile, playful off piste. Explo 86 more intuitive on firm; 96 more predictable/stable in soft. Lighter resort setup tradeoffs.
- **Völkl M7 Mantra W** (formerly Secret 96, same design) — 138.5-96-121 (170); radii 24/38/15/20; 1883/1925 g; 42/4.5/11.5; 170.6; -12.1 / 73.2; 149-177; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Top-tier precision/stability; one of best ~95 mm carvers tested; easy to get on edge, encourages high angles intuitively; off piste avoid backseat (MB 99Ti W/Dancer 2 more maneuverable/forgiving). For advanced/experts; livelier alternative to BP 94/Santa Ana. More off-piste → Mantra 102 W.
- **K2 Mindbender 99Ti W** — 113.5(?)-98.5-119.6 (172) [as printed]; 15.4 m (166); 1928/1945 g; 58.5/3.5/24; 171.8; -10 / 75.9; 154-178; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Strong case across wide range of conditions and skier types: high-speed composure/edge hold for aggressive skiers, intuitive for progressing intermediates; one of most well-rounded here. Vs 4FRNT MSP CC: easier to slash, more forgiving, floats better. Vs Mantra/BP 94: less demanding, more forgiving, less precision/max-speed stability. Exceptionally versatile, not boring.
- **Head Kore 99 Ti W** (all-new) — 131-98.5-120 (170); 15.6 m; 1803/1843 g; 55/7/18; 168.1; -8.7 / 75.4; 156-177; poplar/PET + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Between precise narrower Kore Ti and surfier 104; adaptable for middle-of-bell-curve conditions; not as quick as old light Kores but smoother/more predictable; less exhilarating on firm than some ~100s but more maneuverable/forgiving off piste; nice suspension in chop; punishes backseat more than More Forgiving skis. Good if others here seem too stiff/heavy/carving-focused.
- **4FRNT MSP CC** — 132.5-98.4-121.1 (171); 16.75 m; 1957/1974 g; 61.5/5.5/12.5; 169.9; -7.8 / 77.2; 159, 165, 171; aspen + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Do-everything with emphasis on stability/precision; supportive, heavy enough to push hard; a bit more forgiving than BP 94/Mantra 102 W; dislikes hesitant backseat in tight terrain; more engaging on piste than MB 99Ti W. For those liking M7 Mantra/BP 94 but wanting less rearward mount and easier skidding.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 98 W** — 133.5-99.5-123.5 (178); 17 m; 1817/1818 g; 67/8/23; 177.2; -8.5 / 80.1; 154-178; poplar/PU + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Directional freeride line with some M-Free DNA; more stable than maneuverable but manageable in moguls/tight terrain if not tail-gunning; stance-versatile vs -10 cm skis; quicker to pivot than M7 Mantra/BP 94, more planted/precise than Havoc 100 Ti; vs MB 99Ti W: MB more forgiving, M-Pro quicker/livelier. A charger that "lightens up".
- **Majesty Havoc 100 Ti** — 129.5-99.5-114.5 (176); radii 17/19/22/18; 1815/1824 g; 66/6.5/17.5; 174.9; -9.8 / 77.7; 164-188; poplar/ash + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Low swing weight, nimble even in grabby snow; stable/precise when driven; tapered rockered tips don't engage carves on firm; floats well for width; stay off stiff tail; tips twitchy in rough crud. Similar to Maven 103 CTI (Maven much better carver; Havoc smoother suspension). For stiff directional fans who find such skis sluggish/locked-in.
- **Völkl Revolt 101** (new) — 132.5-100.5-114.5 (175); radii 22/17/20; 1979/2010 g; 63/4/28; 174.9; -7.2 / 80.3; 168-189; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Defies categories: stiff and damp like most stable skis but deeper rocker and more centered mount; drive shovels or ski centered, just not backseat; aggressive in all snow yet playful/surfy. Several reviewers loved it; for those split between stable directional and playful.
- **Völkl Mantra 102 W** (formerly Secret 102, same construction) — 141-101.7-123.8 (170); radii 26/16/23; 2010/2063 g; 59/3/17; 170.5; -12 / 74; 156-177; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Benchmark for high-speed stability/suspension in women's all-mountain; crud-erasing; manageable in tight terrain for those liking hefty skis. M7 Mantra W livelier on piste; 102 more versatile off piste. For those who find lighter skis imprecise/chattery.
- **Atomic Maven 103 CTI** — 132-103.5-121 (178); 18 m; 1744/1921 g (as printed); 57/6.5/21.5; 177.1; -9.7 / 78.9; 156-178; poplar/ash + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Actually "rips groomers and floats in pow"; best with good technique/forward stance; much lower swing weight than peers; lively/agile, not muting rough snow; one of more precise carvers yet surfy in pow with above-average float. Better suspension → next skis.
- **Moment Siren 104** (new; mirrors unisex Countach 104) — 137.5-103.5-124 (176); 16.5 m; 1840/1848 g; 51.5/6.5/28; 174.6; -8 / 79.3; 170, 176; poplar/pine + carbon & fiberglass. Middle ground directional/freestyle; surfy maneuverable in soft snow, precise and powerful when driving shovels; stance-tolerant but not backseat (esp. firm moguls). Closest comparison Revolt 101 — Siren more nimble/lively; Revolt damper/surfier.
- **Nordica Santa Ana 102** — 135-103.8-125.4 (179); 18.5 m; 2125/2139 g; 67/5/36; 177.4; -7.1 / 81.6; 155-179; poplar/beech + 1 partial titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Stout; helps aggressive skiers push; needs good technique; nearly unmatched composure at speed in imperfect snow; energetic when bent. Vs Mantra 102 W: harder to pivot, livelier. One of best stiff, damp, stable all-rounders.
- **Head Kore 104 Ti W** (brand-new, replaces Kore 103) — 132.5-104.5-124.5 (177); 16.7 m; 1921/1930 g; 65.5/7/28; 176; -8 / 80; 156-177; poplar/PET + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Surprisingly playful/maneuverable, excels in soft-ish snow, far more predictable in crud than Kore 103; easy to slide/slash — one of more accessible off-piste options here; nice suspension; more effort in tight moguls than Kore 93; clean turns when not icy (not for very firm). Accessible like More Forgiving skis but smoother.
- **Folsom Cash 106** — 138-104.6-124 (176); 18 m; 1884/1894 g; 61/5/36.5; 174.4; -6.7 / 80.5; 164-189 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Customizable; mid build balances maneuverability and stability; pushable but less sluggish/demanding than Mantra 102 and Dancer 3; lighter/softer build → forgiving; heavier/stiffer → among most stable here. Intuitive (not exhilarating) on piste, respectable float. Lighter/quicker non-custom → Kaizen 105, Siren 104.
- **DPS Kaizen 105** (women's listing) — 134-105-118.2 (179); 18 m; 1762/1830 g; 49/6/22; 178; -10 / 79; 155-189; ash/poplar (horizontally laminated) + carbon. For precise, dynamic skiing rather than charging; not exceptionally damp but impressive suspension for weight; heavier skis calmer in chop; better for more turns/creative lines; less effort to bend than older stiffer DPS carbon skis. Max maneuverability → DPS Wailers (More Forgiving).
- **Faction Dancer 3** — 133.7-105.6-123.5 (172); 18 m; 1958/1960 g; 66.5/4/26.5; 170.4; mounts Newschool -7.6/77.6, Progressive -9.1/76.1, Classic -10.6/74.6; 164-188; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Not a pow specialist — one of best here for fast chopped pow, strong/stable but lively and quick for size; floats well; holds edge on very firm. Mantra 102/SA 102 more planted; Dancer 3 more energetic, playful. Daily driver in snowy places or wider stable/surfy ski.

**Blister Spectrums — Women's All-Mountain More Stable** (top → bottom):
- Best on Ice → Worst: Völkl Mantra 84 W; M7 Mantra W; Santa Ana 92; Black Pearl 94; Black Pearl 88; Mindbender 89Ti W; Mantra 102 W; Black Pearl 84; Shaggy's Belle 90; 4FRNT MSP CC; Armada Reliance 92 Ti; Salomon Stance W Pro 88; Head Kore 93 Ti W; Kore 87 Ti W; Fischer Ranger 90; Ferreol Explo 86; Faction Dancer 2; Mindbender 99Ti W; Dynastar M-Pro 92 W; Kore 99 Ti W; Ferreol Explo 96; Santa Ana 102; Atomic Maven 103 CTI; Majesty Havoc 90 Ti; ZAG Harfang 96; M-Pro 98 W; Moment Siren 104; Kore 104 Ti W; Havoc 100 Ti; Völkl Revolt 101; Faction Dancer 3; DPS Kaizen 105; Folsom Cash 106.
- Best Suspension/Damping → Worst: Mantra 102 W; Black Pearl 94; Santa Ana 102; Santa Ana 92; MSP CC; Mindbender 99Ti W; Kore 104 Ti W; Revolt 101; M7 Mantra W; M-Pro 98 W; Dancer 3; Harfang 96; Cash 106; Kore 99 Ti W; M-Pro 92 W; Havoc 100 Ti; Dancer 2; Mantra 84 W; Siren 104; Belle 90; Mindbender 89Ti W; Reliance 92 Ti; Ranger 90; Stance 88; Kore 93 Ti W; Havoc 90 Ti; Black Pearl 88; Kore 87 Ti W; Black Pearl 84; Maven 103 CTI; Kaizen 105; Explo 96; Explo 86.
- Best in Powder → Worst: Kaizen 105; Maven 103 CTI; Siren 104; Cash 106; Dancer 3; Havoc 100 Ti; Kore 104 Ti W; Santa Ana 102; Mindbender 99Ti W; Revolt 101; Mantra 102 W; M-Pro 98 W; Harfang 96; Dancer 2; Kore 99 Ti W; MSP CC; Explo 96; Reliance 92 Ti; M-Pro 92 W; M7 Mantra W; Havoc 90 Ti; Black Pearl 94; Ranger 90; Kore 93 Ti W; Santa Ana 92; Mindbender 89Ti W; Belle 90; Explo 86; Kore 87 Ti W; Stance 88; Black Pearl 88; Black Pearl 84; Mantra 84 W.

### Women's All-Mountain Skis — More Forgiving (src ~2922-)
Class overview (Sascha Anastas photo): same women's framing; "forgiving" vs "stable" are broad with exceptions. More Forgiving skis generally (1) work well at slower speeds, (2) don't immediately punish backseat, (3) need less input/power. More Stable skis work best fast/aggressive. Many here suit beginner/intermediate everyday skiers, but also aggressive skiing in soft/consistent snow, and finesse-oriented advanced/experts wanting lighter, nimbler skis.

- **Coalition Snow Rebel** — stated 124-85-106 (168); 17 m; stated 1678 g; subtle rocker/camber/rocker; stated mount -8 cm; 162, 168, 175; birch + fiberglass. Playful, easygoing narrow all-mountain; snappy/lively on groomers; not best for icy carving but predictable on firm; one of more forgiving <90 mm skis; good for narrow ski used off piste. Firm edge hold → BP 88; variable snow → Lil' Nasty.
- **Renoun Earhart 88** — 126.4-86.6-110.6 (170); 14.5 m; 1364/1392 g; 70/5/10; 166.5; -9 / 74.2; 149, 156, 163; maple + 2 partial titanal + VibeStop + carbon & fiberglass. Easy at slow speeds yet rewards aggression — VibeStop stiffens with pressure; may feel chattery for skiers who don't drive it; surprisingly smooth at speed for one of lightest here (heavier skis more stable in rough). Versatile on/off piste, easy pivot, forgiving.
- **DPS Wailer 90** — 127-90.5-111 (184); 15 m (all lengths); 1917/1961 g; 78.5/8.5/22.5; 181.6; -12.1 / 78.7; 152-184; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Prioritizes off-piste maneuverability; extremely agile in tight terrain; rearward mount/strong flex encourage forward stance but not quick to punish; good in soft/variable; weaker initiation/edge hold on very firm (Quickdraw 92W, Ripstick 94 W, Blaze 94 better). For directional skiers avoiding ice.
- **Meier Quickdraw 92W** — 133.2-92-124.4 (173); 21 m; 1840/1850 g; 55/5/42; 171.5; -9 / 77; 147-173; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Approachable yet composed on edge; one of more damp/stable here; easy to skid despite little rocker/taper; more playful/accessible than many More Stable skis. Ripstick 94 W more nimble/energetic; Wailer 90 easier in soft/grabby. Damp do-everything with traditional feel, low learning curve.
- **Coalition Snow Lil' Nasty** (full name "Lil' Honey Yum Yum Baby Cakes Nasty Snack"; newest) — 121-93-115 (173); 18 m; 1694/1746 g; 70.5/3.5/42; 171; -4.5 / 81; 166, 173; birch + fiberglass. Narrow version of SOS/Rafiki: centered mount, symmetrical, prefers centered stance; substantial build — damper/more stable than Earhart 88 / Ripstick 94 W; deep rocker, easy to skid/slash; swing weight lighter than actual; strong middle. Initial take: rare playfulness+stability in narrow class (more time needed).
- **Elan Ripstick 94 W** — 129.5-93.5-109 (168); 15.8 m; 1550/1603 g; 50/3/20; 167.2; -7 / 76.6; 147-175; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods + carbon strips + flax tip + fiberglass. Very approachable, nimble, lively; easy initiation; stance-tolerant; great for less experienced heading off piste and for advanced skiers prioritizing quickness over suspension. More suspension → QST 94; ultra-precise light → Blaze 94.
- **Salomon QST 94** (brand-new; QST line overhauled and now fully unisex-labeled, two graphics) — 134-94-120 (180); 16.5 m; 1862/1931 g; 63/2.5/31.5; 178.7; -9.6 / 79.8; 156-188; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + basalt + cork tip/tail inserts + fiberglass. Narrowest new QST; more versatile than outgoing QST Lux 92; stands out for maneuverability/playfulness; carves better than predecessor; shines in trees, moguls, steeps (firm or soft); smoother/more predictable than equally maneuverable skis. Forgiving, not chattery.
- **Völkl Blaze 94** — (same specs as 50/50 listing: 132.5-94-118 @179; 1613/1631 g; -13.3 / 76.1). Sharp carving + nimble lively build; edge hold like heavier Völkl metal skis; not stable/planted; works as touring, 50/50, or groomer all-mountain. Vs Ripstick 94 W: stiffer, better edge hold, less maneuverable off piste; not for big moguls/damp seekers. Rare precision/energy/agility.
- **Blizzard Sheeva 9** — 129-96-118 (174); 16 m; 1751/1752 g; 79/4/41; 174.3; -7.7 / 78; 150-174; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Playful alternative to Black Pearl retaining some precision/power; versatile easygoing yet can be skied hard; suits progressing skiers and confident ones wanting light/undemanding; vs Blaze 94/Ripstick 94 W: damper, more stable, easier to pivot, less lively/forgiving backseat. Most precise/least stable Sheeva.
- **RMU Valhalla 97** (redesigned) — 125.5-96.5-120 (172); 17 m; 1598/1607 g; 25/5/15.5 (as printed); 170.3; mounts FR -4.3/80.9, TR -6.3/78.9; 162, 172; poplar + beech underfoot + fiberglass. One of quickest/most playful ~100 mm all-mountain skis tested; skis short, very maneuverable; tips knocked around in crud vs QST 94/Unleashed 98; not for firm carving; prefers centered (mount back 1 cm for forward stance). For playful advanced or less experienced wanting easy steering.
- **Renoun Endurance 98** — 133.2-96.8-118.4 (184); 18 m; 1901/1902 g; 68/4/29; 182.2; -10.1 / 81; 163-184; aspen + 6 VibeStop channels + 2 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Rare balance: calm/supportive at speed (one of more stable here) yet softer/accessible slow; versatile, loose off piste. Unleashed 98 better edge on very firm; Slap 98 for freestyle.
- **Nordica Unleashed 98** — 132.4-97.5-121.3 (174); 17.4 m; 1741/1747 g; 70.5/3/54.5; 171.8; -7.2 / 78.7; 156-186; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. For freestyle roots or playful directional skiers; quick in tight terrain/moguls (far more than Santa Ana), more planted at speed than lighter alternatives; lively and carves very, very well; stance-accepting. Looser → Slap 98, QST 100; more suspension → Santa Ana.
- **ZAG Slap 98** — 130-97.5-116.5 (180); 20 m; 1830/1860 g; 58.5/7.5/35; 178.5; -7.3 / 82; 160-187; paulownia/poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + rubber strip + fiberglass. Blurs freestyle/directional; well-rounded, no obvious weakness; surfy/forgiving off piste, carves fairly well, OK in fresh and old snow; skis switch; directional reviewers found it intuitive. Not exciting on piste. Wider Slap 104 better for mixed snow/off piste.
- **Line Pandora 99** — 130.5-98.5-120 (170); 16 m; 1711/1748 g; 57/3.5/39; 169.3; -4.6 / 80.1; 149-184; aspen + fiberglass. Solid all-mountain with freestyle sprinkle; agile/maneuverable more than damp; good suspension for weight; mount closer to center but works forward. Completo 100/Wailer 100/Maiden 102 easier to pivot; Pandora more precise on piste (maybe detune edges). Heavier metal versions = Line Optic.
- **Folsom Completo 100** — 129.3-98.9-119.3 (174); 16 m; 1666/1668 g; 70/3/43; 171.6; -5.3 / 80.5; 156-186 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. Exceptionally nimble; highly tapered, deep rocker; centered-ish mount for upright or forward; supportive middle, soft ends; downside vague firm initiation, less composure than Pandora 99. Not too light/soft; full custom available.
- **Rossignol Sender Free 100** (new) — 130.5-99.5-122.5 (178); 18 m; 1901/1923 g; 54/9.5/53; 175.9; -4 / 84; 162-190; poplar + rubber layers + "Air Tip" insert + fiberglass. Inspired by wider Sender Free; prefers centered upright stance (learning curve for set-back skiers); lively, balanced, capable; lighter swing weight than weight suggests; handles speed/bigger airs better than lighter options; carves well, looser in soft snow. Forward stance → Unleashed 98.
- **Salomon QST 100** (new middle QST; replaces QST 98) — 134-99.5-121.5 (180); 17.5 m; 1944/1995 g; 66/5/39; 178.5; -8.7 / 80.6; 148-188; same QST construction. Most playful sibling with impressive suspension; vs QST 98 more maneuverable, forgiving, stance-accepting without losing damping/stability; rarely out of place (trees, corduroy, park); not for icy groomers. Firm precision → Pandora 99. Suits aggressive and newcomers.
- **Elan Ripstick 100 W** — 136-100.5-116.5 (175); 17.8 m; 1680/1693 g; 46/5/16; 174.2; -6.9 / 80.2; 154-175; Ripstick carbon construction. Light, easygoing, agile at low speed; less eager carver than 94 W but good edge hold underfoot; easy to flick but not loose/surfy (QST 100, Wailer 100 are); tails can feel long; not damp/stable — moderate speeds. Precise more than ultra-maneuverable.
- **DPS Wailer 100** — 133.5-100.5-118.5 (184); 15 m (all); 1916/1963 g; 95/9/27; 181.4; -12.3 / 78.4; 153-189; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Tight radius, deep rocker, lots of taper — quick precise turns, off-piste agility; easy/loose for directional skiers; respectably damp when driving shovels in open terrain (Ranger 102, Maiden 102 more stable); floats very well; not for very firm high-angle carving.
- **Fischer Ranger 102** — 136.3-101.2-126.1 (176); 18 m; 1880/1916 g; 66.5/3.5/35; 174.4; -10.1 / 77.1; 155-190; beech/poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Soft front, strong back — fits both Forgiving and Stable; easy initiation, good float, respectable speed stability; stiff tails powerful but punishing in moguls if centered/backseat; loose/surfy off piste; firm edge hold concentrated underfoot. For directional skiers wanting playful/loose with more power.
- **Blizzard Sheeva 10** — 134-102-123 (180); 17.5 m; 1851/1856 g; 75/4/39; 178; -7 / 81.5; 156-180; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Reactive, adaptable, broad appeal (intermediate-expert); not forgiving backseat; agile, easy to slide/carve/hop; supportive, precise on edge for maneuverability. Vs Unleashed 98: more demanding in tight terrain, floats/turns better in fresh; vs Ranger 102: more secure on edge, less punishing. Middle-of-spectrum balance.
- **Icelantic Maiden 102** (overhauled last season) — stated 137-102-129 (176); 18 m; 1830/1835 g; 69/5.5/57.5; 173.1; mounts PC -4.8/81.8, BC -6.8/79.8; 155-176; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Most well-rounded Maiden yet; blend of stability and playfulness between directional and freestyle; deep rocker/soft ends playful (more than Sheeva 10), minimal taper/moderate weight = more stable than similarly maneuverable skis. Like a more forgiving Sheeva 10, less directional Ranger 102, or less freestyle ARV 106.
- **ZAG Slap 104** — 133.7-102.4-122.5 (176); 18.5 m; 1761/1778 g; 67.5/6/49.5; 174.1; -7.3 / 79.8; 164-188; paulownia/poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + rubber strip + fiberglass. Marketed BC freestyle but more than a soft-snow toy; easy pivot/slash/slarve; middle flex/mount for varied stances; on-piste predictable not exciting; shines in dynamic off-piste turns in soft snow; handles speed/rough OK. Freestyle → ARV 106, Sender Free 100; Slap better for directional skiers.
- **Line Optic 104** — 131.8-103.5-123.2 (178); 19 m; 2074/2088 g; 56.5/4/44.5; 177.6; -6.7 / 82.1; 166-190; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Uncommon recipe, reviewer favorite; heaviest in section, smooth/damp in rough, yet one of most playful (switch, balanced in air, loose, poppy); stiff midsection edge hold, soft ends forgiving. Aggressive directional may find shovels soft. Lighter, no metal → Pandora 106.
- **Coalition Snow SOS** — 125.2-104.3-119.5 (173); radii 40/25/44; 1955/1990 g; 64/4/46.5; 171.8; -2.9 / 83; 157-180; birch + fiberglass. Center-mounted symmetrical but damp/stable (bucks light-and-loose trend); prefers upright centered stance; smooth planted. ARV 106 livelier on piste (SOS needs speed to edge), SOS looser with lower swing weight. Deeper days → Coalition Rafiki (Women's Powder).
- **Armada ARV 106** (formerly ARW 106, now unisex) — 131.5-105.5-124.5 (180); 20 m; 2010/2031 g; 53.5/8/50.5; 178.4; mounts Freestyle -2.6/86.6, Factory -5.1/84.1; 164-188; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Freestyle roots, very capable all-mountain; one of loosest/most playful here; good for natural features/park; directional reviewers found it intuitive and stable enough at fairly high speeds. On-piste precision → Pandora 106, MB 106C W.
- **Line Pandora 106** — 133.5-105.5-125 (176); 16.7 m; 1912/1931 g; 58/3/41; 175.3; -4.9 / 82.8; 155-189; aspen + fiberglass. Wants to do everything and mostly succeeds; damp/stable enough to ski hard, quick/lively; Optic 104-influenced stance versatility; resists deflection better than lighter skis; floats well, carves firm better than Slap 104/ARV 106. Solid 1-ski quiver in snowy areas. Directional → MB 106C W; smoother → Optic 104.
- **K2 Mindbender 106C W** — 137-105.6-125.5 (176); 17.3 m; 1961/1985 g; 57/3/32; 175.7; -8.5 / 79; 155-176; aspen + "carbon spectral braid" + flax & fiberglass. Wide ski that doesn't make you dread fast turns/firm groomers; directional, forward stance (Summit 106/Pandora 106 more forgiving); super maneuverable, excellent float, predictable on firm, carves surprisingly well; more supportive than Summit 106, more stable than Pandora 106/ARV 106.
- **Wagner Summit 106** (women's listing) — 140-106-126.9 (172); 17.4 m; 1847/1854 g; 70.5/1.5/29.5; 170.4; -10 / 75.2; 165-186 (custom). One of most intuitive skis ever tested; carves as easily as skids; predictable everywhere; "forget we're on it" (compliment). Suspension/on-piste like MB 106C W but more forgiving flex, lower swing weight; alternative if Pandora 106's centered mount is unappealing.
- **Icelantic Maiden 108** — stated 143-108-135 (176); 18 m; 1821/1835 g; 66/2.5/59.5; 173.2; mounts PC -4.4/82.2, BC -6.4/80.2; 162, 169, 176; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Also in Women's Powder; soft ends forgiving and take edge off crud; easy to slip/slide; decent carver on soft slopes for size; Maiden 102 best all-rounder, 108 adds float and stability... (cont.)
  (Maiden 108 cont.) ...accessible and capable at higher speeds when snow is soft.

**Blister Spectrums — Women's All-Mountain More Forgiving** (top → bottom):
- Best in Deep Snow → Worst: K2 Mindbender 106C W; Icelantic Maiden 108; Wagner Summit 106; Line Pandora 106; DPS Wailer 100; Armada ARV 106; Maiden 102; Blizzard Sheeva 10; Fischer Ranger 102; Line Optic 104; Folsom Completo 100; ZAG Slap 104; Salomon QST 100; Pandora 99; RMU Valhalla 97; Slap 98; Coalition SOS; Nordica Unleashed 98; Wailer 90; Renoun Endurance 98; Elan Ripstick 100 W; QST 94; Rossignol Sender Free 100; Sheeva 9; Völkl Blaze 94; Coalition Lil' Nasty; Ripstick 94 W; Renoun Earhart 88; Meier Quickdraw 92W; Coalition Rebel.
- Most Stable in Crud → Least: Optic 104; Endurance 98; Summit 106; Maiden 108; Ranger 102; Mindbender 106C W; ARV 106; Pandora 106; QST 100; Sheeva 10; Maiden 102; Sender Free 100; QST 94; Sheeva 9; Unleashed 98; Pandora 99; Slap 104; Slap 98; Quickdraw 92W; Lil' Nasty; Wailer 100; Completo 100; Wailer 90; SOS; Valhalla 97; Ripstick 100 W; Earhart 88; Rebel; Blaze 94; Ripstick 94 W.
- Easiest in Tight Terrain → Most Difficult: Completo 100; Earhart 88; Wailer 90; Ripstick 100 W; Ripstick 94 W; Valhalla 97; Maiden 102; Wailer 100; Slap 104; ARV 106; QST 100; Slap 98; Summit 106; QST 94; Pandora 106; Maiden 108; Pandora 99; Optic 104; Sheeva 10; Sender Free 100; Mindbender 106C W; SOS; Lil' Nasty; Endurance 98; Unleashed 98; Sheeva 9; Blaze 94; Quickdraw 92W; Ranger 102; Rebel.

### Women's Powder Skis (src ~3747-)
Class overview (Kara Williard, Hokkaido photo): Same women's framing. Skis for deep soft snow: wider, more tip/tail rocker than all-mountain; range directional to playful. Since resort pow days include chop/crud, Blister also describes tracked-out performance. Some overlap with other sections; several viable as all-mountain in snowy areas.

- **Folsom Cash 106** (same specs as above). Standout in both pow and all-mountain; enough weight to avoid bounce in cut-up pow, quick/agile; as loose/surfy as ARV 106/Valhalla 107 but more stable/precise on edge — appreciated on tired legs. Folsom's wider shapes for truly deep (More Directional pow section). More composure → Dancer 3, Maiden 108.
- **Prior Flute** — 132.2-104.9-119.8 (163); 17 m; 1500/1504 g; 73/2/26; 162.3; -9.2 / 72; 163, 169, 175 (custom); yellow cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Made in Whistler with customization; tested "Quad-Glass" build light/nimble (carbon touring build lighter). Turns easier in deep snow than some wider skis; holds edge with some speed; impressive damping for weight; prefers forward stance but swing weight like playful skis. Heavier skis better in deep chop at speed; one of quickest; great for hybrid binding / BC+resort.
- **Faction Dancer 3** (same specs). Blurs hard-charging all-mountain and dependable pow tool for aggressive skiers; biased to big fast turns but manageable in tight ones; not loose/surfy; one of best in chop, crud, firm. For directional stability-first skiers; mount forward for centered stance. Max playfulness → Line, Armada, Coalition models.
- **Line Pandora 106** (same specs). Does everything with style; more forgiving/freestyle-friendly than set-back skis (Dancer 3, MB 106C W); respectable suspension in cut-up snow; adaptable for directional and playful skiers. ARV 106/Valhalla 107 looser; Maiden 108/Unleashed 108 float better and less knocked around in deep chop. Middle ground.
- **Armada ARV 106** (same specs). Just a dusting is enough; ARV 112 for consistently deep untracked; 106 more versatile in shallow/chopped resort snow; surfy, balanced; directional reviewers impressed by composure in heavy snow/chop. Valhalla 107 quicker; ARV 106 more versatile at speed. Less freestyle → Unleashed 108, Maiden 108.
- **K2 Mindbender 106C W** (same specs). Strong versatile directional; firm-snow precision plus excellent float — one of best ~106 mm skis for flotation; looser/surfier in pow than firm; can ski aggressively in soft chop. For directional skiers driving fronts (allows centered in pow). Pandora 106 more forgiving/lively; MB more planted. Deep-snow focus → MB 116C W.
- **Wagner Summit 106** (same specs). Dependable, capable anywhere; floats well enough for most storm days; less loose than ARV 106/Valhalla 107 but carves much better on firm; more forgiving than MB 106C; suits wide range of abilities, not demanding. More suspension → heavier Summit 107; custom for pow specialist.
- **RMU Valhalla 107** — 134-106.5-123.5 (172); 18.5 m; 1783/1815 g; 59/3/37; 170.9; -6.5 / 79; 162, 172; poplar + beech mounting zone + fiberglass. Punches above width in deep snow and tight turns; short effective edge, deep rocker = easy release/pivot/slash in pow, slush, firm; most intuitive for centered upright stance; flex tweaked this year (smoother transitions = more support). Can be all-mountain. Carving/bigger turns → ARV 106, Unleashed 108. Good suspension (not excessively light); great for storm days and stash-hunting.
- **DPS Wailer 107** — 139.5-107-125 (184); 15 m (all); 2055/2056 g; 88.5/6.5/30; 182.1; -9.6 / 81.4; 155-189; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Wailer tradition of easy turning in fresh snow plus versatility; highly tapered deep-rocker shovels, tight radius, directional mount — floats exceptionally, turns easily even slow; encourages forward stance but very maneuverable/playful; nervous in big fast turns in variable but nice suspension for controlled turns. Few as easy in tricky off-piste terrain.
- **Nordica Unleashed 108** — 140.6-107.5-129.5 (180); 19 m; 2035/2083 g; 73/3/56; 178; -7.5 / 81; 162-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Among more playful here: energy, flotation, edge hold, all-round composure; adaptable to centered or forward stance; for directional skiers wanting quickness/pop and freestyle skiers wanting more stiffness/precision. Closest comparison Sheeva 11 (Unleashed more practical daily driver; Sheeva slightly better in soft chop); Maiden 108 more forgiving/playful, less composed in dense chop. Broad range.
- **Icelantic Maiden 108** (same specs). Easygoing maneuverability + surprising composure at speed; centered or forward; middle of stable-damp vs quick-playful; deeply rockered ends plane up and stay loose; blasts through chop respectably. Aggressive directional → Sheeva 11; max deep maneuverability → wider tapered skis. For intermediates easing off piste to experienced playful skiers.
- **Blizzard Sheeva 11** — 139.5-111.5-129 (180); 19 m; 1959/1986 g; 77/3/42; 179; -7.6 / 81.3; 168, 174, 180; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. More versatile than most similarly wide skis; easy slashing in fresh/chop; one of better ~112 mm carvers; doesn't match others' float/maneuverability in pure untracked; wider skis easier in tight low-angle terrain; surfier/forgiving → Maiden 108. Shines in resort pow-day soft chop — drive strong minimally tapered tips through piles; excels in chaotic mixed-condition runs.
- **Armada ARV 112** — 134.5-111.5-129.5 (185); 21 m; 1998/2026 g; 55/7/54; 183.4; mounts Freestyle -2.8/88.9, Factory -5.2/86.5; 165-192; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Freestyle roots; low swing weight, deep rocker = flick, slash, throw sideways; supportive/damp enough in heavy pow/chop; balanced but lets you drive shovels in dense snow; upright stance in light deep snow. High-speed chop composure → Rafiki, Anima Birdie. For playful skiers who want backbone on landings.
- **Salomon S/Lab QST Blank** (updated) — 138.5-112-127 (178); 18 m; 2000/2047 g; 69/5.5/36.5; 176.5; -7.9 / 80.6; 170-192; poplar + basalt + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Even more playful/maneuverable than original: quicker, more forgiving, easier to surf/slash; lost some suspension/high-speed stability but still respectable vs equally floaty peers. Alternative to ARV 112 for directional forward-stance skiers, or lighter/more agile than Rafiki. Doesn't carve like Sheeva 11 but better for on-the-fly deep-snow turns (trees, big faces).
- **Coalition Snow Rafiki** — 137-114-131.5 (180); 21.4 m; 2166/2178 g; 95/3/61; 178; -4.5 / 84.5; 157-180; poplar/birch + fiberglass. Rare mix of easy pivoting/deep-snow looseness and high-speed composure in soft chop; one of heaviest here — not bounced in cut-up pow; centered mount, freestyle-friendly, but directional reviewers found it intuitive. Sheeva 11/Unleashed 108 much better on piste and when it hasn't snowed; Rafiki one of favorites for resort storm ("chop") days.
- **Black Crows Anima Birdie** — 145-114.5-134 (182.1); 19 m (all); 2306/2332 g; 63/6/54.5; 180.3; -6.4 / 83.8; 176.6, 182.1; poplar + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. One of heaviest women-specific skis tested — excellent suspension; charges heavy pow, thick chop, crud damply; balanced by loose surfy feel; forward or centered. Shines in cut-up conditions. Vs Rafiki: more precise on firm, drive shovels harder (Rafiki surfier/balanced). Not easy to muscle at very slow speed.
- **K2 Mindbender 116C W** — 142.5-115-131 (177); 19.5 m; 1927/2009 g; 53/4/30; 175.9; -8.5 / 79.5; 163, 170, 177; aspen + carbon spectral braid + flax & fiberglass. Dedicated pow float/maneuverability with big-mountain backbone; works driving shovels but doesn't require it; fun without super-deep snow; heavy/stiff enough for soft chop; less practical in dust-on-crust but not niche. Anima Birdie calmer in chop; 116C better float, lower swing weight. Adaptable to turn shapes.
- **Salomon S/Lab QST X** (women's listing at 178 cm) — 138-116-125 (178); 24 m; 1826/1845 g; 61/4.5/28.5; 177.1; -5.6 / 83; 178, 184, 192; karuba/poplar + cork + titanal binding reinforcement + flax/carbon + fiberglass. One of most optimal for deep untracked pow: excellent float, very low swing weight, predictable slarves/drifts; short/twitchy in nasty snow; strong midsection for aggressive light-footed style with a few inches soft snow; excellent for skinning; best centered but directional reviewers liked it.

**Blister Spectrums — Women's Powder** (top → bottom; note printed: shorter lengths would rank lower / longer higher on flotation):
