- Most Stable in Chop → Least: Black Crows Anima Birdie; Coalition Rafiki; Blizzard Sheeva 11; K2 Mindbender 116C W; Salomon QST Blank; Armada ARV 112; QST X; Faction Dancer 3; Nordica Unleashed 108; ARV 106; Folsom Cash 106; Icelantic Maiden 108; Mindbender 106C W; DPS Wailer 107; Line Pandora 106; Wagner Summit 106; RMU Valhalla 107; Prior Flute.
- Most Surfy/Loose → Least: Rafiki; QST X; ARV 112; QST Blank; Valhalla 107; Wailer 107; ARV 106; Anima Birdie; Mindbender 116C W; Maiden 108; Pandora 106; Prior Flute; Mindbender 106C W; Unleashed 108; Summit 106; Sheeva 11; Cash 106; Dancer 3.
- Most Flotation → Least: QST X; Mindbender 116C W; QST Blank; Rafiki; Anima Birdie; Unleashed 108; Wailer 107; ARV 112; Maiden 108; Sheeva 11; Mindbender 106C W; Pandora 106; Summit 106; ARV 106; Valhalla 107; Cash 106; Dancer 3; Prior Flute.

### Frontside Skis (src ~4272-)
Class overview (Dylan Wood / Luke Koppa photos): "frontside" is nebulous; here it covers (1) dedicated groomer carvers, (2) skis that carve well and also work in moguls, (3) groomer/mogul skis that see some off-piste (steeps, trees). Many don't realize the best carvers (skinny, flat tail, tight radius) are often terrible in moguls/off piste, and off-piste traits compromise on-piste initiation, edge hold, power. Decide where you want them to shine.

- **Atomic Redster X9S Retro ARC 735 RS** (limited edition, retro graphics, tweaked construction) — 113.5-65-99 (175); 15.2 m; 2148/2165 g w/ plates; 39.5/7.5/5.5; 172.1; -10.8 / 75.3; 167, 175, 183; beech/poplar + 1 titanal + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Exceptionally precise on firm, powerful and lively but less demanding than next two; easy initiation on moderate slopes, shines in SL-GS turns on steeper pitches; fantastic edge hold and energy; less predictable as groomers soften/pile up. Smoother/higher speed limit → next two.
- **Fischer RC4 Noize** (new Noize collection centerpiece of Fischer race/piste line) — 111-65-99.5 (178); 15.5 m; 2335/2342 g w/ plates; 44.5/9/5.5; 176.6; -11.2 / 77.1; 168-183; beech + 2 titanal + printed metal pigment + fiberglass. Strong, damp, game-on; easier to edge than true GS ski but needs significant input to bend; excellent edge hold/rebound, smooth on very firm; excels fast and firm only. GS feel without full GS effort. Accessible → Fischer Curv GT series.
- **Fischer The Curv** — 120-69.8-102.5 (185); 17.5 m; 2432/2445 g w/ plates; 42/6/7; 185; -11.5 / 80; 157-185; beech/poplar + 2 titanal + carbon "bridge" underfoot + carbon & fiberglass. Stiff damp carver more accessible/adaptable than race-inspired skis; more top-end than X9S Retro; smooth suspension, high-speed composure, turn-shape versatility (prefers GS); needs effort, esp. 185; less punishing than race ski or RC4 Noize; slightly more predictable in variable piste than the other two. For experienced skiers wanting more power than recreational carvers.
- **Head Supershape e-Magnum** — 125.5-71.5-109.5 (170); 13.1 m; 2003/2011 g w/ plates; 46.5/10.5/5; 169; mount N/A (plate); 149-177; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + "Energy Management Circuits" + carbon & fiberglass. Firm-snow, tighter-turn Supershape; stiff and damp for recreational carver; can make GS-ish turns if not driven hard; precise, agile, sharp high-angle turns on moderate slopes. Forza 70° Ti / Dancer 79 easier to edge; e-Magnum better high-speed composure/edge hold. For aggressive short turns on firm snow.
- **Völkl Peregrine 72** (new) — 122-71.5-102.5 (173); radii 21/15/17 m; 2180/2193 g w/ plates; 34/6/9; 173.1; -12.6 / 74; 158-178; beech/poplar + titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Peregrine 82's well-rounded performance in slimmer firm-snow package; less demanding than race-inspired skis (Disruption MTi), more stable/supportive than e-Magnum; very intuitive initiation, excellent edge hold at varied speeds; tight or open turns. Less planted than The Curv, less easy than Forza 70° Ti, but more versatile than both. Wider Peregrines better in mixed piste.
- **K2 Disruption MTi** — 119.6-73.3-102.9 (175); 18.1 m; 2225/2248 g w/ plates; 39/5/4; ... (cont.) Race-inspired, powerful and confidence-inspiring at speed; prefers bigger, faster turns but not an ultra-planted tank; livelier/lighter-feeling than The Curv with impressive stability/suspension; not as easy to initiate as The Curv...
  (Disruption MTi cont.) 175.2 cm; -10.6 / 77 from tail; 165-180; maple/aspen + titanal + "Dark Matter Damping" inserts + fiberglass. Needs speed/input to bend; ideal for big GS turns with lots of energy. Less aggressive / varied groomers → Peregrine 82, Supershape e-Rally. For those finding recreational carvers lacking on firm open runs.
- **Rossignol Forza 70° Ti** — 133.1-76.5-109.5 (173); 14 m; 1701/1706 g; 44/9/7; 172.5; -10 / 76; 163, 173, 181; poplar + ~1.5 titanal layers + carbon/basalt/fiberglass. Makes high-edge-angle carving accessible; most "expert" Forza ("70" = 70° edge angle) but one of most easygoing carvers here; fat tips/tight radius quick to initiate, easy to feather/release; damper at speed than expected but not a damp cruiser. Slalom-style turns at moderate pace. Stiffer/precise → Dancer 79.
- **Folsom Spar 78** — 122-77-106 (177); 17 m; 1675/1732 g; 51/6/11.5; 176.3; -9.1 / 79; 158-184; poplar/maple/bamboo + 90/10 fiberglass/carbon (custom options). Nimble, energetic on piste, OK off trail; vs Spar 88 (favorite ~90 mm all-mountain for moguls/trees): quicker to initiate, more precise/lively; needs precise technique off piste but more all-terrain viable than similar narrow skis; energy over suspension (custom damper builds possible).
- **Head Supershape e-Rally** — 127.5-77.5-111.5 (177); 15.3 m; 2199/2211 g w/ plates; 51/11/11; 175.7; mount N/A (plate); 156-184; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + Energy Management Circuits + carbon & fiberglass. Adaptable carver for aggressive skiers valuing suspension; strong, damp, precise; tight carves or predictable GS if you ease off shovels; proficient skiers can handle spaced moguls; needs force on low-angle firm. Longer arcs → e-Titan; more agile → e-Magnum.
- **Parlor Warbird** — 130.9-77.7-107.1 (178); 16 m; 1724/1735 g; 67/4/9; 176.6; -10.8 / 77.5; 155-192; maple/poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass (custom). "Precision instrument tuned for fun"; accessible flex (most narrow skis here very stiff); wide soft shovels, easy edging, quick across fall line; light, energetic, predictable in consistent snow; one of easier skis to carve on icy groomers. Custom stiffer/heavier for speed.
- **Faction Dancer 79** (new) — 118-78.5-106.5 (178); 17 m; 1687/1693 g; 52/11.5/18; 176.6; mounts Newschool -6.9/81.4, Progressive -8.4/79.9, Classic -9.9/78.4; 158-184; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Light metal Dancer build in piste shape; proper carver for tighter turns, energy/agility over suspension; easy to bend, strong edge hold in consistent snow; not hooky but twitchy in challenging snow (low mass). Peregrine 82/e-Titan better for GS+ turns.
- **Völkl Peregrine 80** — 126-79.5-109 (177); radii 27/15/23; 1941/1968 g; 35.5/6.5/10.5; 178.1; mount N/A (plate); 162-182; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Rewards finesse and power; easy initiation, cruisy big turns, locks into tight arcs when driven; carves ice, easy to feather. vs Peregrine 82: less damp/stable at very high speed. Safe bet intermediate-expert valuing forgiveness.
- (Ad: Carv 2 digital ski coach.)
- **Renoun Atlas 80** — 126.9-78.5-111.6 (177); 15 m; 1728/1750 g; 55/7/13; 173.1; -6.2 / 80.4; 163-184; maple + 2 titanal + VibeStop + carbon & fiberglass. Speed-sensitive flex: soft and easy at slow speed, stiffer/more stable when pushed; fun on mellow slopes, secure on edge; fights its tight sidecut in big fast turns (ski more centered). Less intuitive for some; off-piste → Renoun Endurance.
- **Völkl Peregrine 82** — 128-81.5-111 (177); radii 27/15/23; 2000/2016 g; 37/6/12; 178; N/A (plate); 162-182; same as 80. Reviewers' grab when groomer conditions unknown; for more advanced/aggressive than 80; better edge hold, damper at speed; edge hold more binary (hold or break free); far from race ski, varied turn shapes like Curv GT 85. Well-executed all-conditions carver for advanced/experts.
- **Kästle MX84** — 126.5-82.5-111.5 (176); 16.3 m; 1940/1949 g; 53/7.5/9; 174.1; -11.6 / 75.4; 155-183; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Shines slicing groomers; excellent ice grip, lots of energy; not most forgiving — less intuitive transitioning between skid and carve; more manageable off piste than e-Titan but MX88 far better in variable/tight moguls; agile for weight but suspension less than expected (Montero AR, Supershape smoother). Strong, lively, precise small-medium turns.
- **Stöckli Montero AR** — 127.5-83-113 (180); 17.4 m; 2058/2079 g; 47/9/11.5; 178.7; -12.3 / 77; 165-185; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Power, control, smooth ride on firm; easy-bending shovel that doesn't hook unpredictably; stiffer precise mid/tail, powerful finish; not eager on low-angle; in moguls must unweight wide stiff tails but more viable off piste than MX84/Octo. Tighter turns → Steadfast 85.
- **Black Crows Octo** — 130-83-115.5 (179.3); 14 m; 1724/1749 g; 38.5/10.5/8; 178.1; -7.5 / 81.6; 167.2-185.4; poplar + fiberglass. Inspired by Mirus Cor but more directional, less radical; excels at short high-angle turns; more stable at speed and more intuitive for directional skiers than Mirus Cor; fairly light, not smooth/planted; secure edge on most pistes; some off-piste if prioritizing quickness/precision.
- **Head Supershape e-Titan** — 128.5-83.5-112.5 (177); 17.2 m; 2121/2147 g w/ plates; 62/9/12; 175.4; N/A (plate); 156-184; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + EMC + carbon & fiberglass. Best Supershape for bigger turns and wide range of piste conditions; strong, precise, damp, not deflected; not snappy at slow speed or good off piste; excellent aggressive carving from icy to chewed groomers; slightly less damp than e-Rally, more GS-biased. Accessible alternatives with similar suspension → Steadfast 85, Curv GT 85.
- **Völkl Mantra 84** (replaces Kanjo 84) — 129-84-109.5 (177); radii 29/16/26; 1869/1886 g; 45/3.5/11.5; 177.9; -12.1 / 76.9; 163-184; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Family resemblance to Mantra 88/M7: stiff, precise, lively; most agile/piste-oriented, needs less speed to arc tight or long turns, least damp; advanced/experts; fun off piste in firm snow (not tight odd moguls). Close to Peregrine 82 precision with more versatility.
- **Fischer The Curv GT 85 Redefine** — 133.5-84.5-117 (175); 15 m; 1837/1854 g; 50/6.5/8; 174.3; -11.5 / 75.6; 161-182; poplar/beech + "Al4ever" titanal (2 layers) + flax & fiberglass. Same specs as Curv GT 85 but recycled/alternative materials — reportedly 36% less CO2e; both precise, stable, versatile carvers; less speed than e-Titan to edge, comfortable in GS; Redefine slightly damper/less energetic. For intermediates improving and experts wanting grip without demanding ride.
- **Nordica Steadfast 85 DC** — 136-84.5-115.5 (179); 15.8 m; 2096/2154 g; 51/7/11.5; 177.7; -7.3 / 81.6; 162-179; wood + 1 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Very smooth suspension, forgiving, composed; not quite Peregrine 82/Mantra 88 edge hold/speed composure but close when driven; suspension matches/beats them; easier to feather carve vs skid; far more viable off piste. Bridges piste-specific and 50/50 skis; calm, collected.
- **Majesty HNX Ti** (new) — 124.5-85-119.5 (178); radii 15.5/17/18/16; 1821/1827 g; 39/9/9; 177.4; -7.7 / 81; 166, 172, 178; poplar/ash + 1 titanal + triax & biax fiberglass. Unusual tail but not a gimmick or tight-turn specialist; precise, fairly powerful medium-radius carver in most conditions (icy to slushy); agility/pop over suspension; some off-piste potential (more off piste → MB 89Ti, Havoc). For GS-style carving fans who dislike heavy "dead" skis.
- **Black Crows Mirus Cor** — 133.2-86.7-122.8 (178); 13 m (all); 1796/1838 g; 50/2/40; 176.5; -3.9 / 84.4; 160.1-184.2; poplar + partial titanal plate + fiberglass. Seemingly random traits (87 waist, 13 m, centered mount, twin rocker, split tail) combine into one-of-a-kind blast; effortless tight turns, accessible symmetrical flex, easy release/slash, skis switch; centered stance takes adjustment; better off piste than most ~87s but hooky in variable. Favorite for low-angle groomers.
- **Rossignol Arcade 88** — 134.5-87-123.5 (178); 15 m; 2045/2057 g; 57.5/7/13.5; 175.7; -6.7 / 81.2; 154-186; poplar + ABS stringer + titanal + elastomer & AirTip inserts + fiberglass. Powerful all-conditions carver, different feel: not eager at slow speed, but front pressure → excellent grip/power transfer and launch into next turn; engaging on low-angle, supports high angles at speed; not for long turns; nice suspension without being dead; not off-piste for most. Softer snow → new Arcade 94.
- **Völkl Mantra 88** (replaces Kendo 88) — 132.5-87.5-113.5 (184); radii 31/17/27; 1848/1986 g (as printed); 43/4.5/10; 183; -12.1 / 79.4; 163-184; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Stiff, precise, rewards input; viable off piste if you stay out of backseat; like a longer-turn, more maneuverable Peregrine 82 (Peregrines quicker/snappier); off piste like M7 Mantra in firm/shallow. Anomaly 88 smoother; Mantra livelier.
- **Blizzard Anomaly 88** — 127.5-87.5-109 (182); 18 m; 2047/2056 g; 73/7/16; 180.4; -10.4 / 79.8; 164-188; poplar/beech + titanal (full-width & partial) + fiberglass. Composed, confidence-inspiring in firm and mixed; minimal taper/shallow rocker precise; not hooky off piste if on shovels; strong, not light — advanced/experts; fine in moguls/trees/chalky steeps if off tails; less lively than Mantra 88 but equal or better edge hold/suspension. Good for ~70/30 on/off piste directional skiers.
- **Dynastar M-Cross 88** — 134.5-87.5-116.5 (184); 15 m; 1906/1907 g; 48.5/10.5/15.5; 183.4; -12.7 / 79; 159-184; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Piste-focused all-mountain with freeride flair; snappy, dynamic, easy carve initiation despite tip rocker/taper; good firm edge hold, easy to feather; pop over suspension but smoother than lighter skis (MB 90C); not as maneuverable off piste as M-Pro/M-Free but not piste-specific. Tight moguls → M-Pro 92.
- **Kästle MX88** — 129-87.5-113.5 (181); 18.8 m; 1960/2004 g; 54.5/8/10; 179; -8.9 / 80.6; 160-188; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. True all-mountain version of MX84 power/precision: groomers, moguls, trees, variable — for skiers who stay off tails (backseat → Line Blade or More Forgiving section); slower initiation than MX84 but composed in GS turns, impressive edge hold. More agile/forgiving → Stance Pro 90.
- **Head Kore 88 Ti** (new) — 128-87.5-111.5 (177); 16 m; 1783/1801 g; 52.5/11/22; 176.2; -10.2 / 78; 156-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Overhauled Kore line: heavier, softer, more rockered → much better suspension, stability, all-mountain capability in poor snow; still fun to carve with accessible edge hold; less locked-in/stable than Mantra 88/Anomaly 88 but easier to pivot/skid/hop off piste; still agile/lively. Frontside ski that doesn't punish leaving groomers (not for ice).
- **Meier Quickdraw 88** — 137.5-88-113.5 (181); 19 m; 2025/2028 g; 55/11/25; 181; -11 / 79.8; 156-186; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Less one-dimensional than dimensions suggest; subdued initiation — less hooky in big variable turns but less engaging on low-angle; hops/pivots in moguls/trees if not backseat; nice suspension in rough (better than Zephyr 88); not for ice carving. Open cruising lines.
- **RMU Zephyr 88** — 134-88-117 (176); 16.5 m; 1794/1795 g; 58/10/15; 174.2; -8.3 / 78.8; 168, 176, 184; poplar + beech mounting plate + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. By far RMU's most piste-oriented: long effective edge, more camber than rocker; easy on edge, holds strong in corduroy or crud; soft shovels ease into turns (like Steadfast 85) — not exciting slow but predictable big turns; SL-GS for aggressive skiers; rebound over suspension; calm on steeps. Tighter turns on firm → Dancer 79.
- **K2 Mindbender 89Ti** (unisex) — 129.8-88.3-113.6 (182); 16.6 m; 1915/1937 g; 56/7/16; 181.7; -10.5 / 80.3; 164-188; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Narrow all-mountain capable on piste: easy tight/medium carves, very good firm edge hold, energetic; easier to release/slash in tight off-piste than most here; less damp/stable than Anomaly 88/Mantra 88 but more manageable/forgiving. Balanced on/off. Variable → MB 99Ti.
- **K2 Mindbender 90C** — 131.5-89.3-117 (178); 15.3 m; 1735/1754 g; 45/6/21; 178; -8 / 80; 160-178; aspen + carbon spectral braid + fiberglass. Carbon instead of metal — more nimble/lively; deeper rocker, low swing weight; "precise" not "surfy"; secure edge, aggressive carves; strong tail hard to release in firm moguls — more piste-oriented but viable off piste with precise dynamic technique. MB 89Ti better suspension and preferred off piste by many reviewers.
- **Salomon Stance Pro 90** — 128.3-90.2-109.6 (182); 19 m; 1854/1863 g; 56/8/9; 181; -10.5 / 80.5; 168-188; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/basalt inserts + fiberglass. Most influenced by wider all-mountain skis; less eager to pull into turns but far better in bumps, trees, variable; not for firm low-angle but secure with speed. Balance like MX88 (Stance quicker/livelier; MX88 more stable). Exciting on-piste alternative → MB 89Ti.
- **Shaggy's Brockway 90** — 136-90.3-116.1 (180); 17.5-19.25 m; 1833/1849 g; 60/6.5/15; 179.5; -8.8 / 81; 162-186; ash/poplar + nanoMAG magnesium stringers + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Composed/precise on firm yet calm in soft/rough; "camber pockets" fore/aft of bindings + minimal taper = excellent edge hold; magnesium boosts suspension; not quick edge-to-edge but shines in loose/variable groomers where narrow skis get hooky. All-mountain → Ahmeek 95. For advanced carvers wanting more edge hold than typical ~90s.
- **Wagner Summit 91** — 134-90.5-115 (182); 17.7 m; ... (cont.) Precise, confidence-inspiring all-mountain ski yet approachable; less maneuverable/forgiving in tight bumps than Stance Pro 90...
  (Summit 91 cont.) 2072/2094 g; 76/1/26; 179.8; -10 / 80; 168, 175, 182 (custom); aspen/beech + titanal layers + fiberglass. Wins on piste initiation and high-speed composure vs Stance Pro 90; like Summit 97 but more precise/exciting on piste, less easy to skid in variable; less punishing/demanding than MX88. Broad appeal for firm-snow all-mountain. Stiffer/livelier → MSP 91.
- **4FRNT MSP 91** — 129-91-116 (181); 16.5 m; 2047/2082 g; 55/6/15; 180; -8.5 / 81.5; 165-187; aspen + 2 titanal + neoprene tip insert + fiberglass. MSP blueprint tuned for piste; more firm-snow biased than damp MSP 99; surprisingly snappy/lively, quick across fall line, impressive edge hold, pop; capable off piste for precise skiers; easier in bumps/trees than Anomaly 88. For those finding heavy skis too damp/uninspiring. More forgiving → Summit 91.
- **Northland AM178** — 138.5-91-123 (178); 15 m; 1808/1834 g; 57/10/13; 177.7; -8.5 / 80.5; 158, 168, 178; hickory/ash/beetle-kill pine + kevlar/carbon/fiberglass. Northland skis "built to carve," unconventional; nearly full camber, tight radius, strong flex — deep trenches; rewards deliberate technique (learning curve); rare precision, energy, "direct" sensation, livelier than heavy metal carvers; handful in moguls/trees (no rocker, strong flat tail) but wider platform helps in variable. Classic feel for skilled frontside skiers.
- **Kästle Paragon 93** — 126.5-91.5-113.5 (177); 16.4 m; 1820/1867 g; 59.5/8/17.5; 174.8; -11.3 / 76; 163-184; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Much more agile yet precise alternative to MX: lighter, more rocker, tapered tip; secure on edge, excels in firm/shallow; less damp than MX88 but lower swing weight, easier to edge and pivot. Stability → MSP 91; more suspension/maneuverability → MB 89Ti.
- **Rossignol Arcade 94** (newest Arcade) — 137-93.5-126.5 (178); 16 m; 1988/2067 g; 61/9.5/11; 176.8; -6.7 / 81.7; 154-186; poplar + ABS stringer + 1 partial titanal + rubber layers + Air Tip + fiberglass. Excellent all-round medium-radius carver for its width; less quick than Arcade 88 but otherwise similar; a bit more maneuverable off piste but fat stiff flat tail — stay forward in bumps. Stance Pro 90/MB 89Ti more agile, but Arcade 94 beats them on edge hold/high-speed stability on rough groomers.
- **Folsom Spar Turbo** — stated 148-94-120 (182); 15 m; 2112/2116 g; 51/7/15; -7 / 81 from tail; 152-186 (custom); maple/poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. "Fun carver" niche with Mirus Cor/Line Blade — stiffest and most versatile off piste of those; very easy to tip up for small aggressive carves (only Mirus, Blade, Forza 70° Ti easier). Spar 88 better 1-ski quiver; Spar 78 better ice grip. Makes low-angle groomers interesting.
- **Line Blade** — 154-95.3-124 (181); 13.5 m (176); 1999/2060 g; 49.5/6/27; 178.8; -7 / 82.4; 153-181; aspen + titanal sheet w/ cutouts + fiberglass. Oddball: very tight radius, soft flex, progressive mount, slight twin; loved by several reviewers; anyone can bend into slalom turns with rebound; easy-to-release tail = surprisingly versatile off piste; enough mass not harsh. Not for pure ice; aggressive directional skiers must adapt to soft flex. Firmer conditions → Mirus Cor.

**Blister Spectrums — Frontside** (top → bottom):
- Most Versatile → Most Specialized: K2 Mindbender 89Ti; Wagner Summit 91; Völkl Mantra 88; Blizzard Anomaly 88; Head Kore 88 Ti; Folsom Spar Turbo; Salomon Stance Pro 90; Line Blade; 4FRNT MSP 91; Kästle Paragon 93; Dynastar M-Cross 88; Black Crows Mirus Cor; Kästle MX88; Völkl Mantra 84; Black Crows Octo; K2 Mindbender 90C; Shaggy's Brockway 90; Folsom Spar 78; Meier Quickdraw 88; Rossignol Forza 70° Ti; Nordica Steadfast 85 DC; Stöckli Montero AR; Rossignol Arcade 94; Northland AM178; Majesty HNX Ti; Völkl Peregrine 80; Faction Dancer 79; Kästle MX84; RMU Zephyr 88; Peregrine 82; Head e-Titan; Arcade 88; e-Rally; Fischer Curv GT 85 Redefine; Peregrine 72; Renoun Atlas 80; Parlor Warbird; e-Magnum; Fischer The Curv; Atomic X9S Retro; K2 Disruption MTi; Fischer RC4 Noize.
- Best in Moguls → Worst: Stance Pro 90; Blade; Spar Turbo; MB 89Ti; Summit 91; Kore 88 Ti; Anomaly 88; MSP 91; M-Cross 88; Paragon 93; Mantra 88; Mirus Cor; MB 90C; Mantra 84; Spar 78; Quickdraw 88; Brockway 90; Steadfast 85; Octo; Montero AR; MX88; Forza 70° Ti; AM178; HNX Ti; Arcade 94; Dancer 79; Zephyr 88; MX84; Arcade 88; e-Titan; e-Rally; Peregrine 80; Curv GT 85 Redefine; Peregrine 82; Atlas 80; Warbird; Peregrine 72; e-Magnum; Disruption MTi; X9S Retro; The Curv; RC4 Noize.
- Most Accessible → Most Demanding: Blade; Mirus Cor; Spar Turbo; Forza 70° Ti; Octo; MB 90C; Stance Pro 90; Spar 78; M-Cross 88; Quickdraw 88; Steadfast 85; Kore 88 Ti; MB 89Ti; Summit 91; Zephyr 88; MSP 91; Anomaly 88; Dancer 79; Paragon 93; e-Magnum; HNX Ti; Arcade 94; Montero AR; Peregrine 80; Mantra 84; Brockway 90; Mantra 88; MX88; Warbird; Atlas 80; MX84; Curv GT 85 Redefine; Arcade 88; e-Rally; AM178; Peregrine 72; Peregrine 82; e-Titan; X9S Retro; Disruption MTi; The Curv; RC4 Noize.

### All-Mountain Skis — More Stable (src ~5415-)
Class overview (Dylan Wood / Jonathan Ellsworth photos): stiffer, less tolerant of backseat than More Forgiving, but damper/more stable at speed in tracked, variable, rough snow. Formerly heavier on average, but not for the past few years — Blister "not thrilled" since mass still correlates strongly with stability, though brands have improved at strong, directional lighter skis that aren't harsh. Good edge hold, powerful turn finish; good mogul skis for strong skiers, punishing for less experienced; less appealing to playful/loose fans or those wanting easy slow-speed initiation. Some could fit More Forgiving.

- **Renoun Endurance 88** — 126.5-86.5-110.5 (184); 17 m; 1939/1959 g; 61/7.5/24; 180.4; -10 / 80.1; 156-184; maple + 2 titanal + VibeStop + fiberglass. Soft/easy slow, stiffer/stable fast; maneuverable in bumps/trees yet holds up in open terrain; tapered tips slower to initiate carves but good edge hold with force. Off-piste-biased narrow all-mountain. Livelier/more precise on piste → MB 89Ti.
- **Folsom Spar 88** — 131.9-87.3-115.8 (182); 17 m; 2008/2015 g; 63/2/13; 181.2; -10.6 / 80; 172-186 (custom); maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Well-rounded for narrow skis used all over; carves well, very easy to pivot, rarely hooky in soft snow; tested build heavier than standard "Signature Series"; more forgiving than Anomaly 94/Enforcer 94 with nice suspension; tip shape = slightly vague initiation. Tight piste turns → Spar Turbo; off-piste → Cash 93.
- **Head Kore 88 Ti** (same specs as Frontside). Adaptable, agile; low swing weight, energetic flex, less quickness-biased than Kore 87 — broader appeal; much better suspension than lighter predecessor and more forgiving in tight moguls; not class-leading edge hold but fun to carve. For strong directional fans not wanting sluggish/locked-in skis.
- **K2 Mindbender 89Ti** (same specs). Surprising off-piste capability plus exciting groomer ride; easy initiation, very good edge hold, energy; easier to release/slash than Frontside narrow skis; more stable than lighter alternatives (heavier here damper). Minimizes on/off piste compromise. More soft chop → MB 99Ti.
- **J Skis Fastforward** — 125.6-92-110.8 (181); 18.2 m; 2053/2059 g; 66/4/36; 178.9; -7.6 / 81.8; 160-187; maple/aspen + 2 titanal strips + fiberglass. Smooths rough conditions without beating you up; very good suspension/stability yet maneuverable/forgiving; notably smooth calm ride on firm rough snow; easy to pivot off piste, uncommon playfulness for class; other ~90s carve very firm groomers better. More accessible → J Masterblaster (More Forgiving).
- **Folsom Cash 93** — 128-92-118 (185); 19 m; 1952/1964 g; 65/5/18; 183.5; -8.8 / 83; 170-190 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Freeride twist; tested "mid-light" build easy to flick, better off piste than most narrow skis; tapered tips not hooky but less precise initiation (vs Spar 88); still carves firm groomers; mid-pack suspension. Max precision/suspension → Anomaly 94, Enforcer 94; piste/lively → Spar 78.
- **Black Crows Serpo** — 131.1-92.3-114.9 (180); 20 m (all); 1824/1835 g; 53/3/20; 179.1; -7.8 / 81.8; 152.3-186.3; poplar + 2 H-shaped titanal plates + fiberglass. Playful off piste, unexpectedly exciting on it; like narrower Justis: maneuverable, floats well, powerful carver; not plush but suspension good enough to ski hard; less punishing than Justis — middle ground between this section and More Forgiving. Rare maneuverability + edge hold combo. More rearward/planted → Stormrider 95. For hard-pushing dynamic skiers.
- **ON3P Woodsman 92** — 127.5-92.5-115 (181); 17.4 m; 1804/1819 g; 58/6.5/29.5; 181.3; -7.9 / 82.8; 171-186; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Narrowest ON3P freeride ski; off-piste maneuverability and predictability in variable like Woodsman 100/108; tighter radius and less-rockered "Ripper" profile = more engaging on firm groomers; not chattery despite light weight, more composed than some heavier skis in grabby snow. Not for railroad-track carving. For those finding narrow skis too twitchy/directional.
- **Blizzard Anomaly 94** — 133-93.5-114 (182); 18.5 m; 2138/2163 g; 65/6.5/15.5; 180.7; -10.5 / 79.8; 164-188; poplar/beech + titanal (full-width & partial) + fiberglass. Damp, precise, powerful in firm conditions; beats most rivals in suspension/high-speed composure; carves like Frontside skis; not nimble/forgiving or easy to skid in tight terrain. For directional skiers with good technique; calm predictable except deep snow. More maneuverable → Enforcer 94.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 94 Ti** — 127-93.5-117.5 (178); 17 m; 1762/1769 g; 70/8/21.5; 177.1; -8.6 / 80; 154-186; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Directional freeride with some M-Free DNA; best forward but maneuverable, agile, even playful; not damp but enough suspension on firm for dynamic skiers; blast in tight technical terrain; fun carving if not icy. Strong yet forgiving. Stiffer/stable → Kore 94 Ti.
- **Head Kore 94 Ti** (new) — 130.5-93.5-115.5 (177); 16.3 m; 1874/1877 g; 58/12/18.5; 176.3; -10.3 / 77.9; 156-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Middle-ground adaptable; not wildly light or stiff like Kore 93; respectable suspension, agile, forgiving flex. Vs M-Pro 94 Ti: M-Pro more maneuverable; Kore more composed at speed, better edge. Well-rounded safe bet.
- **Nordica Enforcer 94** — 128.5-93.5-116 (179); 17.5 m; 2024/2035 g; 63.5/7/17; 177.8; -6.9 / 82; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Aggressive on/off piste; stable yet energetic rebound; stands out for suspension, composure, precision; needs better technique than Kore 94 Ti/Stormrider 95 off piste but less demanding than similarly stiff heavy skis; quick initiation, good edge, pop. Less planted than Anomaly 94 but more energetic/quicker swing. More variable-snow stability → Enforcer 99/104.
- (Ad: resort "Eight Peaks... One Story" — likely Palisades/other; pure ad.)
- **Stöckli Stormrider 95** — 131-94-119 (182); 18.5 m; 1821/1838 g; 47/6.5/10.5; 180.4; -10.2 / 80; 170-188; poplar/beech/paulownia + titanal layers + "Freeride tip & tail" inserts + fiberglass. Fairly light for metal class but distinctly supple; knocked around more than heavy skis in crud but less harsh on firm than light skis; easy on edge, holds carves, varied turn shapes; nimble/lively off piste; big sweet spot liked by directional and playful reviewers. Rare blend: nimble, maneuverable, precise, supportive, forgiving.
- **ZAG Harfang 96** — 132.7-95.2-116.9 (182); 20 m; 2008/2043 g; 55/5/11.5; 182.1; -12 / 79; 170-188; poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Predictability-focused directional; nice suspension, skiable hard on firm; tapered shovels avoid hookiness; agile for proficient skiers; very rearward mount, minimal tail rocker — drive shovels; less engaging initiation on low-angle. Vs Dancer 2: smoother, looser; Dancer more centered-stance friendly, more rebound.
- **Völkl M7 Mantra** (all Völkl metal all-mountain now "Mantra"; returns unchanged) — 138.5-95.5-120 (184); radii 26/41/18/23; 2111/2144 g; 43/3.5/10.5; 183; -12.2 / 79.3; 163-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. For proficient skiers valuing precision, stability, support; exceptional carver for class, matches narrower skis' edge hold, energetic; not easy to pivot/skid in variable/big moguls. Off-piste → Mantra 102, Summit 97; piste → Mantra 84/88. Standout for experts who find others loose/soft/unsettled.
- **Faction Dancer 2** — 126.6-95.6-116.4 (182); 19 m; 1981/1991 g; 61/6/24.5; 180.5; mounts Newschool -7.8/82.5, Progressive -9.3/81, Classic -10.8/79.5; 155-187; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Metal-laminate edge hold/backbone + freestyle pop/agility; not super forgiving, loose, or planted; low swing weight, strong flex, precise; rewards aggressive yet dynamic style with airs; impressive initiation/edge hold on firm. Reviewer favorite.
- **Salomon Stance Pro 96** — 131.9-96-113.9 (182); 20 m; 1946/1968 g; 56/9/12; 181; -10.5 / 80.5; 168-188; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/flax inserts + fiberglass. Avoids extremes, works in most situations; fairly stiff, damp, light metal ski; less precise/eager than M7 Mantra/Anomaly 94 but respectable carver and more accessible in bumps/trees; less planted than heavier but quicker, less fatiguing. Livelier → Stormrider 95.
- **Wagner Summit 97** (factory model) — 134.2-96.3-118.8 (182); 19 m; 2165/2186 g; 79/3.5/30; 179.6; -9.9 / 79.9; 161-182 (custom); maple/ash + titanal + fiberglass (custom). Mutes rough snow on par with M7 Mantra/Enforcer 94 but considerably more forgiving/maneuverable; deep low-slung rocker, minimal taper; impressive carving; heavy swing weight but approachable; stiffer/softer flex options or full custom. Planted through chop without demanding perfect technique.
- **Fischer Ranger 96** — 128.5-96.6-119.6 (180); 18 m; 1935/1962 g; 66/3.5/21; 178.7; -7.8 / 81.6; 159-187; beech/poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Directional with off-piste maneuverability emphasis plus high-speed support; loose/surfy shovels, easy to flick; strong tail — avoid backseat; easy initiation, fairly good edge hold (very firm carving → Dancer 2, M7, MB 99Ti); balanced swing weight/suspension. More precise/stable → Stance Pro 96.
- **Shaggy's Mohawk 98** — 135.5-98-119.2 (186); 20.5-22.5 m; 2079/2089 g; 70/3/22; 185.5; -8.5 / 84.5 (as printed); 162-186; ash/aspen + nanoMAG magnesium strips + carbon & fiberglass. More bite than its loose look suggests; deep rocker = easy release/pivot, good pow for width; very directional flex (stiff tails) punishes centered/backseat; light-feeling swing weight; below-average crud composure. Similar to Black Crows Justis — Mohawk if valuing agility/surfiness over suspension/edge hold.
- **K2 Mindbender 99Ti** — 133.3-98.3-119.3 (184); 19.6 m; 2085/2120 g; 60/2.5/26; 184; -10.6 / 81.4; 166-190; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Embodies versatility: "well" to "excellent" across conditions, terrain, speeds, styles; not best-in-class suspension but more agile/forgiving than Mantra 102 yet heavy/stiff enough for speed; deep rocker manageable at slow speed; carves firm, lively. Easiest do-it-all recommendation; jack of all trades without being boring.
- **4FRNT MSP 99** — 134-98.5-122 (181); 18 m; 2114/2130 g; 63.5/6/14; 179.6; -8.3 / 81.5; 171-187; aspen + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Charger stability/power with enough freeride maneuverability; not forgiving backseat in tight moguls but more agile/accessible than Enforcer 99; snappy initiation, precise edge, lively; stay over shovels off piste; wide rockered shovels plane/blow through soft chop; good suspension/quickness combo. More forgiving → MB 99Ti; more crud composure → MSP 107.
- **Black Crows Justis** — 137.9-98.6-122.2 (183); 21 m; 2078/2138 g; 68/3/33; 181.7; -7.5 / 83.3; 171-189; poplar + 2 H-shaped titanal plates + fiberglass. Embraces extremes: deep rocker = one of looser in class; almost no taper = very good on-piste initiation/edge hold; downside: hooky/unpredictable in big fast variable turns (detuning helps slightly); stiff tails punish backseat. For advanced/experts wanting maneuverable yet drivable ski. Firm/tight → Serpo.
- **Nordica Enforcer 99** — 133.5-99-121.5 (185); 18.5 m; 2199/2229 g; 63.5/6/26.5; 183.6; -7.1 / 84.7; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Calm and composed in poor snow — one of most damp/stable ~100s; Mantra 102 comparable but more maneuverable; MSP 99 quicker but less damp. Not quick/surfy/forgiving off piste but OK for advanced skiers who prioritize suspension; carves really well, energetic rebound.
- **Kästle Paragon 101** — 134-99.5-120.5 (182); 18 m; 2032/2037 g; 59/9/17.5; 180; -11.5 / 78.5; 169-190; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + Hollowtech tip insert + fiberglass. Nimble yet precise; power over playfulness; like previous Kästle FX: directional, low swing weight, secure on edge; carves very well, lively; suspension on very firm not great; drive hard in soft snow, conservative in rough. More forgiving/surfy → M-Pro 100 Ti, Declivity X 102. For those finding light skis lacking support/excitement.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 100 Ti** — 133.5-99.5-123.5 (186); 18 m; 1967/1972 g; 69.5/8.5/23; 185; -8.9 / 83.6; 162-186; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Middle ground between stiff precise skis and loose M-Free; mid-pack suspension/stability, maneuverable, not punishing, allows centered stance; easy to edge (Enforcer 99/Mantra 102 better near-ice); easy release/slash/hop off piste. Stronger → Kore 100 Ti; max composure → M-Pro 108 Ti (Chargers).
- **Head Kore 100 Ti** (new) — 132.5-99.5-121.5 (177); 17.2 m; 1951/1991 g; 58/8.5/23.5; 176.1; -10 / 78.1; 156-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Average of 94 Ti and 106 Ti: less precise/nimble than 94, damper and easier to skid; more exciting on piste and forgiving than 106. Stands out for doing most things well; far better suspension than Kore 99 it replaced; quick, not needing speed. Looser → Kore Ti W models.
- **Liberty Radian 100** — 134.5-100-122.5 (179); 19 m; 1731/1750 g; 55.5/7/17.5; 179.1; -13.8 / 75.7; 167-185; poplar/ash + partial titanal + fiberglass & carbon. Nimble/surfy in soft snow, sharp/powerful on firm; very directional mount but tapered with deep rocker — easy to smear even in deep snow; light, agile over damp; easy in moguls if not steering from strong tails; energetic, secure, varied turn shapes. Wider Radian 106 similar.
- **Völkl Revolt 101** (unisex; specs 132.5-100.5-114.5 @182; radii 25/19/22; 2028/2111 g; 56.5/5/23.5; 182.2; -7 / 84.1; 168-189; poplar/beech + fiberglass). In freestyle collection but directional shape/rocker/mount; strong but deep rocker = one of looser here; for those between this section and All-Mountain Freestyle; carves well, nice suspension; not forgiving way backseat; fun for playful mogul bashers.
- **ON3P Woodsman 100** — 133.5-101-122.5 (186); 19.5 m; 2067/2076 g; 69/4.5/38; 186.3; -8 / 85.2; 171-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Middle ground between ON3P Jeffrey (freestyle) and Wrenegade (directional); strong, stable, damp yet maneuverable/playful; less planted/precise on firm than Mantra 102 but more agile, floats better, centered-stance OK; knocked around less than similarly pivoty skis. Not for firm smooth carving. Surfier → Jeffrey; deeper → Woodsman 108.
- **Armada Declivity X 102** — 134.5-101.5-124 (180); 18 m; 2026/2038 g; 55/7/21; 179.1; -8.5 / 81; 164-188; poplar + partial titanal + elastic compound + fiberglass. Reviewer favorite: directional with playful seasoning; strong, heavy enough to ski hard, allows centered stance, agile/lively; well-rounded on/off piste; carves well, surfy/loose in moguls/soft snow vs metal peers. Like M-Pro 100 Ti (quicker, less damp). Soft chop stability → Woodsman 100.
- **Völkl Mantra 102** — 141.3-101.8-124.1 (184); radii 30/19/27; 2166/2237 g; 60/2.5/21; 182.9; -11.3 / 80.1; 170-191; poplar/beech + titanal layers + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Top pick for stability and damp composure from bowls to trees; not agile/easygoing overall but surprisingly accessible for its class; remarkably planted at speed; excellent on piste for width, easier to release/pivot than M7. Narrower Mantras snappier; Romp Oso 105 better in tight terrain. Standout high-speed composure yet versatile.
- **Stöckli Stormrider 102** — 134.5-102-124.5 (182); 19.8 m; 1998/2010 g; 64.5/5/13; 180; -11.7 / 78.3; 173, 182, 191; poplar/beech/paulownia + titanal + Freeride tip/tail inserts + fiberglass. More off-piste capable than 95 with same ride quality; easy to pivot/slash with forward stance; less engaging/energetic on piste than 95 but more composed at speed in rough; light-ish, mutes small vibrations; floats well. Strongly prefers forward stance. Less directional mount → Declivity X 102.
- **Parlor Cardinal Pro 102** (custom-width version) — 141.1-102.5-129.1 (182); 18.5 m; 1976/2028 g; 66/4/16; 180.8; -12.8 / 78; 154-196 (custom); maple/poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Highly capable carver for width; handles mixed conditions well for a light quick ski; easy initiation, precise powerful finish; adaptable off piste with weight forward; impressive damping without excess weight. Stock 93 mm Cardinal Pro in Parlor "Factory" series for firmer snow.
- **Moment Countach 104** — 137-103.5-124 (188); 19.5 m; 2084/2123 g; 56.5/7.5/28.5; 186.7; -8 / 85.3; 170-188; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Fills gap between playful and directional; vs Countach 110: less rockered, stiffer tail, more precise on firm; brings soft-snow personality to firmer days; agile/playful end of ~105s but best for directional shovel-drivers. Revolt 101 similar with slightly better suspension.
- **Nordica Enforcer 104** — 136-104-126.5 (185); 19 m; 2273/2297 g; 60.5/6/27.5; 183.8; -7.4 / 84.6; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Smooth suspension across varied terrain; one of heavier in class, mutes rough snow; rebound/pop when pushed (more than old Enforcer 104 Free); more effort in tight terrain but less demanding than Chargers. Vs Mantra 102: Mantra easier to pivot, Enforcer easier to edge at slower speed. Reviewers prefer it to Enforcer 99 for forgiveness in moguls/trees.
- **Atomic Maverick 105 CTI** — 134-104-123 (185); 19 m; 2014/2038 g; 57/2.5/20; 184.2; -9.8 / 82.3; 170-191; ash/poplar + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Actually carves groomers as well as it floats in pow; buoyant, slashy (Bent inspiration?) yet engaging, lively, secure on firm; light — not plush in rough crud; directional, punishing backseat in tight moguls but more forgiving/playful than Paragon 101. For those who find pow skis boring on groomers.
- **Romp Oso 105** — 140.5-104.5-130.5 (188); 20 m; 2215/2231 g; 57.5/6/40.5; 186.4; -7.2 / 86; 172-188; ash/aspen + carbon stringer + fiberglass. Maneuverability + stability for technical terrain/variable; better suspension than lighter skis, moderate taper/rocker makes it easy to release and shed speed; less precise on low-angle groomers than Enforcer 104/Mantra 102 but more maneuverable in soft snow, centered-stance tolerant. Romp Zorro for quick/playful; custom options.
- **Folsom Cash 106** (unisex Signature-like version) — 138-105-124 (188); 21 m; 1989/2000 g; 73/4/32; 185.5; -8 / 84; 164-189 (custom); poplar/bamboo + fiberglass & carbon (custom). Highly adaptable; agile, maneuverable, skiable aggressively in soft/consistent snow; versatility defining trait; low swing weight, easy release without sacrificing too much stability/edge hold. Lighter, more forgiving Oso 105; heavier custom builds among most stable here.
- **ZAG Harfang 106** — 139.5-105.1-124.1 (186); 21.5 m; 2118/2128 g; 53/3/16.5; 185.1; -13 / 79.5; 178, 186, 192; poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Versatile traditional directional; not twitchy in chop; lower swing weight, less hooky than more stable alternatives (tapered rockered tips); very rearward mount, strong flat tail — for shovel-drivers wanting precise finish. Damper/more stable alternative to Paragon 107. Less extreme mount → Oso 105, Countach 104.
- **Head Kore 106 Ti** (replaces Kore 105) — 134-105.5-126 (184); 18.5 m; 2134/2142 g; 62.5/9/30; 183; -9.9 / 81.6; 163-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Very different from agile Kore 105: heavier, softer, much damper/more stable/forgiving in rough firm snow; still relatively quick vs MSP 107/Enforcer 104; good carver, easy to skid; less punishing backseat than Paragon 107/Harfang 106. For off-piste skiers unsure of priorities.
- **Liberty Radian 106** (new) — 138-105.5-126.5 (185); 21 m; 1854/1904 g; 61.5/8/17; 183.9; -8.6 / 83.4; 173, 179, 185; poplar/ash + partial titanal + fiberglass & carbon. Agility/precision of narrower ski with float/maneuverability in soft snow; less rearward mount than Radian 100 — more stance-versatile, more forgiving and stable; still very light, not planted. Lighter/more forgiving alternative to Maverick 105 CTI/Paragon 107. Soft-snow hard chargers → Woodsman 108, Countach 110.
- **4FRNT MSP 107** — 139-106-128 (187); 21 m; 2366/2370 g; 59/6/17.5; 185.4; -8.8 / 83.8; 171-187; aspen + 2 titanal + fiberglass. One of best here for hard/fast in mixed conditions; mass powers through chop/crud; more accessible than Chargers; conservative rocker hampers deep-snow float/maneuverability (vs MB 108Ti, Declivity X 108); carves well, energetic. "Charger core, generalist shape."
- **Wagner Summit 107** — 142.7-106.1-128 (186); 19.6 m; 2258/2303 g; 75/4/35; 183.8; -10.5 / 81.4; 165-186 (custom); aspen + titanal + fiberglass (custom). Phenomenal suspension with forgiving flex and versatile shape; carves impressively yet easier to release/pivot than many; for those who keep skis grounded. Vs MSP 107: MSP stiffer, more stable/precise on firm; Summit floats better, more forgiving, turns easier in tight terrain.
- **Kästle Paragon 107** — 139.5-107.5-128.5 (184); 19.2 m; 2066/2083 g; 62/8/18; 182.5; -11.3 / 80; 168-192; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Retains excellent edge hold without losing soft-snow maneuverability; precise on firm, initiation not snappiest on low-angle; looser in soft snow, low swing weight. Not a charger (heavier skis better) but pushable in soft snow. Lighter, more precise Harfang 106; better suspension → MSP 107; more forgiving → Countach 110.
- **K2 Mindbender 108Ti** — 135.9-107.4-124.5 (186); 22.8 m; 2220/2237 g; 64/4/19.5; 185.7; -9.5 / 83.3; 172-193; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Uncommon mix of floaty surfiness and calm composure in soft conditions; deep rocker = easy release, excellent deep pow performance for width; feels short on firm (MB 99Ti better all-rounder). Directional pow ski with burly all-mountain suspension; stiff tail not demanding in soft snow.
- **Armada Declivity X 108** — 129-107.5-120.5 (184); 24 m; 2161/2167 g; 55.5/5/30; 182.8; mounts Modern -5.7/85.7, Factory -8.7/82.7; 176, 184, 191; poplar + partial titanal + elastic compound + fiberglass. Freeride/pow-inspired; surfier and more stable in big turns than 102 but much less exciting/precise on piste; strong case as narrower pow setup; middle weight/flex balances agility/stability; prefers forward stance. More versatile → Summit 107, Countach 110; more stable soft snow → MB 108Ti. Most maneuverable of those in tight pow trees. More crud composure → Woodsman 108.
- **Black Crows Corvus** (latest, new-school version) — 136.5-109-119.5 (186.2); 25 m; 2008/2049 g; 53/3.5/34.5; 184.7; -6.1 / 86.2; 169.4-191.4; poplar/beech + partial titanal + fiberglass. Historically big-mountain charger, now modern: fairly centered mount, tapered soft tips/tails, symmetrical rocker — one of more agile/playful here; stiff cambered midsection and long radius allow big fast turns in soft snow; not exciting carver nor damp in crud. For centered, quick skiers who dislike soft tight-radius freestyle skis.
- **ON3P Woodsman 108** — 139.5-109-129 (186); 21.0 m; 2194/2200 g; 67.5/4.5/38.5; 186.3; -8.1 / 85.1; 171-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Like Woodsman 100 tuned for higher speeds and deeper variable snow; strong, heavy-ish, not easily unsettled; deep rocker, progressive mount — easy to release, centered OK, floats well. Less precise/planted than MSP 107/Summit 107, quicker and livelier; a bit more demanding than Countach 110 but calmer in dense chop.
- **Moment Countach 110** — 139.6-110-130 (188); 21 m; 2078/2131 g; 63/5/31; 186.7; -7.8 / 85.5; 176-194; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Looks niche but one of Moment's most versatile; originally between playful Wildcat and directional Commander (Commander discontinued this year); lighter/quicker than many here yet more supportive/damp than More Forgiving/Freestyle skis; best in soft/variable, holds edge fine, floats well, blast in cut-up pow. 104 more agile/precise; 110 damper, more forgiving and maneuverable in soft/mixed.
- **Dynastar M-Free 112** — 140.5-111-132.5 (190); 22 m; 2404/2405 g; 61/6.5/46; 189.6; -3.4 / 91.4; 176, 183, 190; poplar/PU + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. One of most surprising skis last season, many reviewers' favorite; wide, lots of rocker, very centered mount — also in Freestyle section; directional reviewers enjoyed it as soft-snow all-mountain; surfier than most here; swing weight lighter than its very heavy weight suggests (size up if between lengths); excellent suspension/high-speed stability in loose, playful, forgiving package; not exciting carver or quick. Rare suspension+stability+playfulness.

**Blister Spectrums — All-Mountain More Stable** (top → bottom):
- Best on Ice → Worst: Völkl M7 Mantra; Blizzard Anomaly 94; K2 Mindbender 89Ti; Nordica Enforcer 94; Black Crows Serpo; Head Kore 88 Ti; Faction Dancer 2; Enforcer 99; 4FRNT MSP 99; Völkl Mantra 102; Mindbender 99Ti; Kore 94 Ti; Stöckli Stormrider 95; Folsom Spar 88; Kästle Paragon 101; Liberty Radian 100; Enforcer 104; Wagner Summit 97; Renoun Endurance 88; Parlor Cardinal Pro 102; ZAG Harfang 96; Kore 100 Ti; Folsom Cash 93; Dynastar M-Pro 94 Ti; J Skis Fastforward; ON3P Woodsman 92; Black Crows Justis; MSP 107; Radian 106; Atomic Maverick 105 CTI; Kore 106 Ti; M-Pro 100 Ti; Salomon Stance Pro 96; Paragon 107; Armada Declivity X 102; Moment Countach 104; Stormrider 102; Fischer Ranger 96; Shaggy's Mohawk 98; Summit 107; Völkl Revolt 101; Romp Oso 105; Woodsman 100; Harfang 106; Folsom Cash 106; Black Crows Corvus; Countach 110; Dynastar M-Free 112; Woodsman 108; Declivity X 108; Mindbender 108Ti.
- Best Suspension/Damping → Worst: M-Free 112; Summit 107; MSP 107; Enforcer 104; Mantra 102; Enforcer 99; Woodsman 108; Summit 97; Anomaly 94; Enforcer 94; Mindbender 108Ti; Oso 105; Countach 110; MSP 99; Harfang 106; M7 Mantra; Endurance 88; Kore 106 Ti; Mindbender 99Ti; Stormrider 102; Woodsman 100; Fastforward; Declivity X 108; Kore 100 Ti; Revolt 101; Spar 88; Harfang 96; Declivity X 102; Stance Pro 96; M-Pro 100 Ti; Countach 104; Cash 106; Woodsman 92; Cardinal Pro 102; Maverick 105 CTI; Justis; Paragon 107; Stormrider 95; Corvus; Dancer 2; Kore 94 Ti; Ranger 96; Kore 88 Ti; M-Pro 94 Ti; Cash 93; Mindbender 89Ti; Serpo; Paragon 101; Mohawk 98; Radian 106; Radian 100.
- Best in Powder → Worst: Mindbender 108Ti; Declivity X 108; M-Free 112; Countach 110; Woodsman 108; Summit 107; Corvus; Maverick 105 CTI; Paragon 107; Radian 106; Cash 106; Oso 105; Countach 104; Harfang 106; Kore 106 Ti; Revolt 101; Justis; Enforcer 104; Mohawk 98; Woodsman 100; Mindbender 99Ti; Paragon 101; Radian 100; Stormrider 102; Declivity X 102; Mantra 102; MSP 107; M-Pro 100 Ti; Kore 100 Ti; Cardinal Pro 102; Summit 97; Ranger 96; MSP 99; Enforcer 99; Harfang 96; Dancer 2; M-Pro 94 Ti; Stormrider 95; Stance Pro 96; M7 Mantra; Woodsman 92; Serpo; Mindbender 89Ti; Cash 93; Kore 94 Ti; Enforcer 94; Endurance 88; Fastforward; Kore 88 Ti; Anomaly 94; Spar 88.

### All-Mountain Skis — More Forgiving (src ~6733-)
Class overview (Luke Koppa / Dylan Wood photos): diverse group; generally (1) work well at slower speeds, (2) don't immediately punish backseat, (3) need less input than Chargers/More Stable. Suit beginners/intermediates wanting a dependable everyday ski, aggressive skiing in soft/consistent snow, and finesse-oriented advanced/experts wanting lighter, nimbler skis.

- **Line Optic 88** — 121-87.5-110.5 (184); 17.9 m; 1933/1977 g; 56.5/3.5/37.5; 183.4; -7.4 / 84.3; 156-184; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Unusual blend: very maneuverable/playful for width yet composure like heavy More Stable skis; deep rocker, progressive mount — easy release/pivot; great to progress on and for experienced skiers wanting nimble surfy ski not knocked around in shallow crud. Not for icy carving or heavy backseat.
- **ZAG Mata Ti** — 141.5-90-118 (178); 14.5 m; 1980/1981 g; 43.5/9.5/19.5; 177.1; -9.4 / 79.2; 163-183; poplar + partial titanal + quadraxial fiberglass + rubber inserts. Concept-ski look but accessible; short radius, soft shovels = easy tight carves on moderate slopes; forgiving tails release in moguls/trees; predictable but not great on scraped groomers, blast on softer. Closest: Line Blade (Mata quicker, more energetic; Blade better edge hold, less knocked around).
- **DPS Wailer 90** (unisex listing, same specs as women's). Quickness, loose playful maneuverability; easy slashes and tight carves; doesn't encourage high angles on very firm; outperforms dimensions in 3D snow (slush, soft chop, pow); forward stance but easy; advanced skiers can push with dynamic style. More centered/stable → Optic 88, QST 94.
- **Meier Wrangler** — 135.5-93-118.3 (185); 17 m; 1947/1962 g; 57/3/18; 185; -10 / 83; 170-185; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Does a bit of everything; damper, more stable, precise than many here but less demanding than More Stable; minimal taper = reliable firm grip, versatile turn shapes; less easy in tight moguls/trees (pressure shovels). Softer/more maneuverable → QST 94.
- **Salomon QST 94** (unisex; same specs). Standout for several reviewers; very accessible/maneuverable, big sweet spot — experienced playful-directional skiers can push it; very nice suspension for weight; little effort to skid/hop; not for high angles on scraped groomers. Rustler 9 more precise; QST 100 more playful. One of most well-rounded here for quicker/surfier setup.
- **Shaggy's Ahmeek 95** — 131.5-94.9-116.1 (180); 20-22 m; 1808/1823 g; 57/1.5/45; 179.3; -6.6 / 83; 162-186; ash/poplar + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Pivots/smears and holds edge uncommonly well; "camber pockets" (like Ahmeek 105) for firm grip; deep rocker easy to slash; less damp than 105 but handles speed/big turns better than Ripstick 96 / MB 96C; stance-adaptable. Not for tight piste carves. Better suspension/forgiving → Optic 96.
- **Line Blade** (also here; same specs). Strange and addictive; tight carves with big minimally tapered tips; easy-to-release tail = forgiving, playful; heavy swing weight/big shovels cumbersome in tight moguls; fine in spaced bumps/trees; nice suspension, energetic. Reviewer favorite; not for high-speed GS or zipper-lining.
- **K2 Mindbender 96C** — 131-95.5-119 (178); 16.7 m; 1831/1844 g; 55/5/27; 178; -9 / 80.5; 166-184; aspen + carbon spectral braid + flax & fiberglass. Blurs More Stable/Forgiving; less suspension than 99Ti but notably quicker; directional, forward stance (others more backseat-forgiving); low swing weight, deep rocker = quick slashes; tips pull into carves, impressive edge hold. For proficient dynamic skiers. Damper/forgiving → Endurance 98.
- **Elan Ripstick 96** — 133-95.5-113.5 (182); 17.8 m; 1735/1741 g; 48/5/17.5; 181.3; -8.4 / 82.2; 161-189; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods/strips + flax tip + fiberglass. One of most agile/lively in class; less smooth in rough than heavier skis; easy to flick/slash/carve; forgiving flex, stance-tolerant; carves well when driven; more well-rounded than Wailer 90/Completo 100. Great to progress on; experts with active style. Variable snow → heavier next skis.
- (Ad: Taos Ski Valley, NM.)
- **RMU Apostle 96 Ti** — 134-95.5-119 (184); 18.5 m; 1894/1919 g; 65/5/26; 182.5; mounts FR -5/86.5, TR -7/84.5; 176, 184; poplar + beech mount plate + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Bridges surfy maneuverability and directional confidence; very easy to pivot/slash/flick on snow and in air; stance-adaptable, fairly forgiving backseat; Ripstick 96/Rustler 9 better edge on very firm, but impressive on piste vs equally maneuverable skis. Better suspension → Optic 96; soft snow → Apostle 106.
- **Line Optic 96** — 129.1-95.6-118.7 (184); 18 m; 2077/2096 g; 59/3/42; 183.8; -7 / 84.9; 156-184; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Freestyle shape + metal build; more stable at speed than nearly all previous skis here yet engaging slow; smooth but lively/poppy; loose and surfy off piste (like Apostle 96 Ti); not for lots of firm carving. Rare suspension+maneuverability+playfulness. Most versatile Optic; 88 stiffer/precise; 104 better suspension/surfier.
- **Renoun Endurance 98** (unisex; same specs as women's listing). Adapts seamlessly to speeds/inputs via VibeStop; forgiving when easy, damp/supportive when pushed; very versatile/intuitive; for intermediates to advanced. Looser → QST 100; more precise on very firm → Rustler 9.
- **Blizzard Rustler 9** — 131-97-121 (180); 17 m; 1880/1887 g; 78/3/41; 178; -8 / 81; 162-186; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Stiffer, more precise ride in a soft surfy class; not as loose in grabby snow; initiates/holds/finishes carves with more power than most here; still more maneuverable than More Stable skis (esp. detuned). Masterblaster/QST 100 smoother, surfier. Slightly damper/playful/forgiving → Apostle 96 Ti.
- **ZAG Slap 98** (unisex; same specs). Freestyle-minded but not freestyle-specific; accessible, maneuverable, easygoing; easy carve but stands out for release/pivot in moguls/trees/soft; intuitive with forward stance; practical swing-weight/suspension balance. Rustler 9 better edge/exciting on low-angle; Pandora 99 more composed at speed. Good entry to playful skis for directional skiers.
- **Scott SEA 98** — 133-97.5-121.5 (180); 17 m; 1901/1905 g; 46.5/7.5/36; 178.7; -7.9 / 81.5; 156-188; poplar + partial titanal reinforcement + fiberglass. Versatile for directional and playful; moderate design ("Ski Everywhere Anytime"); on piste neither sketchy nor exhilarating; off piste quick and slashy but more precise/supportive than Optic 96/Completo 100; middle mount/flex intuitive forward or centered. Suspension → Masterblaster; carving → Bent 100.
- **Line Pandora 99** (unisex; 130.5-98.5-120 @184; 19.5 m; 1976/2000 g; 61.5/3/38; 183.5; -5 / 86.8; 149-184; aspen + fiberglass). Optic-inspired, lighter/livelier; reminiscent of Optic 96; balanced, surfy, can be driven; quicker and less damp than Optic 96 but carves a bit better on firm. Versatile middle ground: damper Rustler 9 or more agile Masterblaster. No blatant weaknesses.
- **J Skis Masterblaster** — 132.5-98.6-117.9 (181); 18 m; 2112/2149 g; 71/4.5/33; 178.6; -7.6 / 81.7; 160-187; maple/aspen + 2 titanal strips + fiberglass. Smooths firm/choppy snow without being demanding; better suspension than most here; heavier swing weight but easy to throw sideways, comes alive at low speed; not energetic/precise on scraped groomers but more accessible/playful than equally damp skis. For intermediates wanting calm in harsh snow, or advanced wanting damp + accessible + playful; good intro to heavier skis.
- **Folsom Completo 100** (unisex) — 129.3-99-119.6 (186); 18 m; 1851/1868 g; 60/4/42; 186.3; -6.8 / 85; 156, 174, 186 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. Nearly effortless direction changes; very tapered/rockered soft ends; less firm precision and high-speed composure but impressively stable in soft-ish snow; balanced and intuitive centered (unlike similar Wailer 100). Custom lighter or heavier builds.
- **Romp Zorro 100** — 136.7-99.3-129.7 (183); 17 m; 1800/1824 g; 58/4/50; 181.4; -6 / 84.7; 155-190 (custom); poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Adaptable; easy ski rarely out of place; easy to edge, enough taper for tight terrain; freestyle-friendly for those comfortable with directional or center-mounted skis; stock build light, not damp, extremely poppy. Custom for more suspension.
- **Salomon QST 100** (unisex; same specs). Replaces QST 98 with more playful, easygoing personality; forgiving, surfy, but moderate flex/weight give broad appeal; beginners find it easy, advanced reviewers pushed it in steeps/moguls; not for very firm carving. Stiffer/more directional → QST 94. Rare: clearly maneuverable/easygoing without feeling chattery or limiting.
- **Atomic Bent 100** (same specs as 50/50). Hard to pin down: freestyle intent but directional; surfy in soft, precise and lively on piste; light enough to tour; no specific ideal user — directional skiers wanting agile/precise, or playful skiers wanting stronger setup. Softer/more rockered (Nomad 100) easier in tight terrain; heavier (Masterblaster) smoother on firm bumps. Cohesive blend.
- **Icelantic Nomad 100** — stated 135-100-127 (188); 20 m; 1893/1925 g; 65/7/61; 185.1; mounts PC -4.9/87.7, BC -6.9/85.7; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Very playful/maneuverable but not limited to short turns; minimal taper, very deep rocker, soft lively ends; accessible, surfy, agile, easygoing; holds up at speed surprisingly well; floats really well; fun to carve if not icy. Stiffer → Bent 100, Rustler 10.
- **DPS Wailer 100** (unisex; same specs). One of most well-rounded Wailers; dramatic taper, deep rocker, tight radius = extremely easy to slash; engaging on piste (not for very firm); damper construction than old carbon Wailers/RPs — more predictable in tough resort snow. For strong directional skiers in tight off-piste terrain, fun in fairly deep snow.
- **Ellis Sabr 100** — 131-101-120.5 (186); 20.2 m; 1945/1946 g; 63/4.5/55; 183.8; -7.1 / 84.8; 174, 180, 186; poplar + fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Newer brand; many things well, not boring; progressive mount, near-symmetrical rocker — playful, viable all-mountain freestyle, but precise/powerful when driven. Like a more maneuverable Bent 100 or more forgiving Rustler 10; agile but not harsh; lively flex. Happy medium between playful and directional.
- **Rossignol Sender Soul 102** — 134-102-124.5 (180); 17 m; 2097/2098 g; 57/7.5/27.5; 180.1; -7.1 / 83; 156-188; poplar + partial titanal + ABS stringer + rubber layers + Air Tip + fiberglass. Approachable, confidence-inspiring for beginners to experts; not quick to punish — ski forward, centered or fairly backseat; traditional shape (unlike Soul 7); easy to bend/slash, fun carver on soft-ish snow; big shovels not knocked around; very nice suspension, above-average composure within section. Like QST 100, suits early-journey and experienced skiers wanting soft, loose but damp.
- **Fischer Ranger 102** (unisex) — 137.5-102.2-127.8 (183); 19 m; 2057/2061 g; 70/4/34; 181.2; -10 / 80.6; 155-190; beech/poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Playful and powerful; soft shovels easy to initiate; decent firm grip underfoot; low swing weight, loose; more punishing than many here if backseat over stiff tails (backseat/new to off-piste → Sender Soul 102, QST 100). Impressive stability for nimbleness. More off-piste → Ranger 108; less directional → Fischer Nightstick 97.
- **Liberty Scope 104** — 133-103-123 (188); 22.5 m; 1778/1792 g; 57/8/47.5; 186.4; -6.7 / 86.5; 164-188; poplar/ash + phenol binding plate + fiberglass. Blends surfy looseness and carving seamlessly; very low swing weight, progressive mount = playful; carves surprisingly well; not damp but handles speed well for weight; stance-versatile; viable all-mountain freestyle yet intuitive for directional skiers. Lighter, better-carving Slap 104 or surfier, more forgiving Rustler 10. Off-piste stability → Nomad series.
- **ZAG Slap 104** (unisex 188 cm: 137.2-103.3-125.9; 20 m; 1997/2001 g; 69/4.5/47; 186.2; -8.3 / 84.8; 164-188). Multi-tool with dash of playfulness; light-ish, quick, better suspension than Scope 104; tapered/rockered — loose in tight terrain/grabby snow; predictable (not exciting) on firm; mount +2 cm for centered skiers. Rustler 10 more on-piste excitement; Atris similar but more precise/energetic.
- **Blizzard Rustler 10** — 136-103.5-126 (186); 18.5 m; 1956/1976 g; 78/3/41; 184; -7.8 / 84.2; 162-192; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Precision/support of directional Blizzards with playfulness; stance-versatile; more playful than More Stable skis; quick initiation, impressive edge hold; fun in soft snow, floats well; not smooth suspension for weight, less forgiving backseat. More maneuverable/forgiving → Atris.
- **Line Optic 104** (unisex 185: 131.6-103.7-123.2; 20 m; 2230/2290 g; 59.5/3.5/44.5; 184.4; -7 / 85.2; 166-190). One of heaviest here yet among most playful: smooths rough snow, skis switch, balanced in air, easy to bend, loose; energetic, not dead; stiff midsection edge hold; soft tips/tails butterable; aggressive directional may find shovels soft. For playful skiers tired of getting tossed on light skis, or directional skiers wanting forgiving surfy + suspension.
- **Black Crows Atris** — 137.2-103.9-121.8 (184); 20 m; 1946/1962 g; 59.5/3.5/41; 182.6; -7.9 / 83.4; 172-190; poplar + fiberglass. Long-time go-to playful all-mountain with directional precision/stability; low swing weight, deep rocker, lively; mount +2 cm for freestyle; drivable; fun carver. Sakana/Rustler 10 better tight piste carves; Apostle 106 surfier/forgiving; Atris more balanced on/off than Slap 104/Wailer 100. Safe middle-of-spectrum bet.
- **Line Sakana** — 149-104.4-137.2 (181); 16.3 m; 1869/1873 g; 56/3/18; 180.8; -10.9 / 79.5; 166, 174, 181; paulownia/maple + carbon/flax stringers + fiberglass. Unconventional: wide soft shovels, minimal rocker, snappy flex — blast for tight hard carves; like Blade, not limited to groomers: not hooky, easy to slash, floats well; knocked around in rough snow. Pandora 106 more versatile; Blade smoother. Refreshingly different.
- **RMU Apostle 106** — 139-105-127 (184); 20 m; 1999/2012 g; 62/4/30; 182.6; mounts FR -4.9/86.4, TR -6.9/84.4; 176, 184, 192; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Very surfy/flickable yet capable aggressively; quick, little effort to slash, forgiving; carves well for its surfiness; respectably damp/stable for agility (Optic 104 more planted). For directional and freestyle skiers seeking max maneuverability; reviewer favorite for aggressive-dynamic style.
- **Shaggy's Ahmeek 105** — 140.3-105.2-128.7 (186); 20-22.5 m; 2202/2209 g; 54/3/40; 185.4; -7 / 85.7; 162-186; poplar/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Contradicts tradeoffs: one of damper/stabler here yet playful; excellent firm/soft blend; camber pockets = above-average edge hold for width (long radius needs speed); deep rocker maneuverable; heavier swing weight, not forgiving backseat in tight moguls; balanced in air. Alternative to Optic 104 with better edge hold and stronger flex.
- **Line Pandora 106** (unisex 183: 133.5-105.5-125; 18.4 m; 2025/2090 g; 56/4/43; 182.3; -5.2 / 86; 155-189). Optic 104-inspired but more playful; stance-versatile, surfy; quicker/livelier than Optic 104, carves as well or better without metal; not wildly light — pushable. Optic 104 more planted in rough.
- **K2 Mindbender 106C** — 137.5-105.5-126.5 (183); 18.9 m; 2050/2084 g; 53/2.5/30; 182.9; -9 / 82.5; 169-189; aspen + carbon spectral braid + flax & fiberglass. Not just a metal-less 108Ti — different ski; both surfy in soft snow, but 106C trades high-speed stability for lower swing weight and surprising firm precision; carves better than most this width; damp for weight; strong tail dislikes backseat in moguls; excellent flotation. Middle ground between soft surfy and precise heavy directional.
- **Salomon QST 106** (revised) — 140-106-127 (189); 20.5 m; 2103/2255 g (as printed); 65/7/40; 187.6; -9.1 / 84.7; 157-189; poplar + basalt + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Same shape, subtly updated: lighter, looser, stiffer; even more impressive pow ski for width (floats/slashes like wider skis); skiable hard in soft chop; less damp/forgiving than predecessor (still above average); won't tolerate sloppy backseat in tight terrain. QST 100 more forgiving for firm/shallow. For directional skiers wanting surfy soft-snow ride with strong flex.
- **Icelantic Nomad 106** — stated 141-106-133 (188); 20 m; 2022/2029 g; 69/5.5/60; 185.2; mounts PC -5/87.6, BC -7/85.6; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Maneuverable with freestyle flair and more stability than expected; deep rocker, soft ends, easy to pivot; lively; composed at speed in soft-ish snow; carves soft groomers; freestyle-friendly yet intuitive for directional skiers prioritizing accessibility. Nomad 100 better on firm; 106 more support. Softer-tailed alternative to QST 106/Ranger 102.
- **Ellis Kanso 106** — 136-106.5-126.5 (186); 19.5 m; 2026/2054 g; 62.5/4.5/52; 184.7; -6.9 / 85.4; 174, 180, 186; poplar + poured PU tip/tail inserts + fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Sabr 100 personality in wider footprint for soft/mixed; lively, agile, playful, broad; floats/turns better, damper than Sabr. Between Apostle 106 (surfiest) and Atris (most precise). More suspension → Ahmeek 105, Optic 104.
- **DPS Wailer 107** (unisex; same specs). DPS pow-shape hallmarks in everyday-justifiable package; tapered rockered tips float better than most at width; practical without new snow; quick and surfy, a bit more planted than narrower Wailers in soft chop; knocked around in crud at speed; cuts clean arcs on soft slopes. Great tight-tree pow ski / all-mountain for tree skiers.
- **Wagner Summit 109** — 141.7-107.9-129.1 (186); 20.4 m; 2171/2191 g; 70/5/33; 184.2; -7.3 / 84.8; 186 (custom); beech/aspen + carbon fiber torsion box + fiberglass (custom). Float plus surprising precision/edge hold; minimal taper and shallow low rocker — very good initiation; looser in soft snow; feels lighter than weight; slightly better suspension than Ranger 108 with similar swing weight. Ranger 108/QST 106 surfier; Optic 104 smoother in crud. Hard to fault — stable, playful, versatile. Lighter → Summit 106.
- **Fischer Ranger 108** — 142.7-108.4-133.7 (185); 18 m; 2110/2117 g; 68/2.5/33; 183.4; -9.6 / 82.1; 171-192; beech/poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Ranger 102 recipe tuned for float and off-piste maneuverability; composed in soft chop, tails can punish backseat, but more forgiving/balanced flex than 102; floats very well — excellent pow ski, predictable on piste; energetic yet surfy enough for ground-bound pivoters. More well-rounded alternative to Wailer 107. Firm precision → MB 106C; more forgiving → Nomad 106.

**Blister Spectrums — All-Mountain More Forgiving** (top → bottom):
- Best in Deep Snow → Worst: Fischer Ranger 108; DPS Wailer 107; Salomon QST 106; Wagner Summit 109; K2 Mindbender 106C; Icelantic Nomad 106; RMU Apostle 106; Liberty Scope 104; Line Pandora 106; Line Optic 104; Ellis Kanso 106; Ranger 102; ZAG Slap 104; Black Crows Atris; Folsom Completo 100; Blizzard Rustler 10; Shaggy's Ahmeek 105; Nomad 100; Wailer 100; Line Sakana; Apostle 96 Ti; QST 100; Atomic Bent 100; Rossignol Sender Soul 102; Pandora 99; Ellis Sabr 100; Optic 96; Renoun Endurance 98; J Skis Masterblaster; Mindbender 96C; Romp Zorro 100; Slap 98; Scott SEA 98; Wailer 90; QST 94; Elan Ripstick 96; Rustler 9; Optic 88; Line Blade; ZAG Mata Ti; Meier Wrangler; Ahmeek 95.
- Most Stable in Crud → Least: Optic 104; Ahmeek 105; Masterblaster; Summit 109; Nomad 106; Mindbender 106C; Ranger 108; QST 106; Ranger 102; Apostle 106; Slap 104; Pandora 106; Wrangler; Sender Soul 102; Kanso 106; QST 100; QST 94; Endurance 98; Pandora 99; SEA 98; Optic 96; Rustler 10; Optic 88; Nomad 100; Wailer 107; Atris; Apostle 96 Ti; Sabr 100; Bent 100; Completo 100; Scope 104; Slap 98; Zorro 100; Ahmeek 95; Rustler 9; Wailer 100; Blade; Wailer 90; Mata Ti; Mindbender 96C; Ripstick 96; Sakana.
- Most Accessible → Most Demanding: Completo 100; QST 100; Nomad 100; Zorro 100; Ripstick 96; QST 94; Sakana; Optic 96; Wailer 90; Wailer 100; Sender Soul 102; Scope 104; Nomad 106; Apostle 106; Kanso 106; Wailer 107; Pandora 106; Optic 104; Apostle 96 Ti; Slap 98; Blade; Sabr 100; Pandora 99; Atris; Slap 104; Bent 100; SEA 98; Endurance 98; Masterblaster; Rustler 9; Ahmeek 95; Optic 88; Mata Ti; Mindbender 96C; Wrangler; Summit 109; Rustler 10; Ranger 108; Ranger 102; Mindbender 106C; Ahmeek 105; QST 106.

### All-Mountain Chargers (src ~7842-)
Class overview (Dylan Wood / Ellsworth photos): class has diversified — heavy & stiff, light & stiff, subtly or seriously rockered, very demanding or even fairly forgiving. After years of lightening, some brands acknowledge weight is valuable — especially here. Not for everyone but favorites of passionate skiers who prioritize high-speed stability over low-speed maneuverability.

- **Blizzard Anomaly 102** — 135.5-101.5-122.5 (188); 21 m; 2259/2278 g; 62/5/14; 186.6; -10 / 83.3; 176-192; poplar/beech + titanal (full-width & partial) + fiberglass. Widest/heaviest/most stable directional Blizzard all-mountain; more traditional precision than most chargers; more precise on firm than others here; slightly more compliant than discontinued Bonafide 97. For strong skiers wanting solid edge on groomers and through crud; bigger turns off piste.
- **Folsom Primary 104 ('Blister Pro' Edition)** — 136.4-103.4-122.5 (186); 21 m; 2325/2352 g; 75/4/27; 184.7; -9.6 / 82.7; 163-189 (custom); maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Stiff, substantial yet highly maneuverable for aggressive technical terrain; mutes rough snow; much easier to release/throw sideways than others; less precise initiation/edge hold on firm smooth snow. For those liking heavy skis' planted ride but struggling with locked-in feel in bumps/trees/steeps. Custom variants possible; wider → Folsom Giver.
- **4FRNT MSP 107** (charger listing; stated radius 20.8 m; same measured specs). Current version ramps up suspension enough for this section; smooths variable impacts; middle balance off-piste maneuverability vs on-piste precision; carves better than Primary 104, easier to release than Anomaly 102; vs Mantra 108: less stiff/precise/stable at extreme speed but more forgiving, less cumbersome. Excellent suspension in well-rounded shape.
- **Völkl Mantra 108** (formerly Katana 108) — 145.4-107.7-128.6 (184); radii 39/19/34; 2353/2360 g; 57.5/2.5/21; 183.1; -11.5 / 80; 170-191; poplar/beech + "titanal frame" + carbon tips + fiberglass. Mantra 102's stiff precise ride with higher-speed capability in mixed snow; big shovels heavy/awkward in tight terrain but releases more than Anomaly 102; not too demanding for strong skiers (tails not punishing); great carver for size; excellent through chop/crud. Most should pick Mantra 102; 108 for open faces.
- **Prior Husume** — 135.7-109-123.5 (188); 25 m; 2312/2386 g; 78/2/22; 187.5; -10.7 / 83; 175-193 (custom); yellow cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Impressive stability, smooth ride, surprisingly surfy shape; very stiff, excellent suspension but not as demanding as weight suggests; deep rocker, tapered shovel = loose for a directional ski (needs physical input); outstanding flotation — great first pow turns, shines in chop. Mantra 108 more engaging on groomers; Husume nearly matches its stability with better maneuverability in technical/soft terrain. Less directional → Giver.
- **Folsom Giver ('The Hammer' Edition)** — 138.3-109.2-129.3 (188); 25 m; 2371/2374 g; 63/0/42; 186.4; -7 / 86.1; 184-195 (custom); maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Very beefed-up Giver for inconsistent conditions; heavy, very stiff — for strong skiers; remarkable composure at speed; forward mount, reverse camber = loose like Primary 104/Husume; allows more centered stance. Lighter/softer builds available; softer/surfier analog → Dynastar M-Free 112 (190).
- **Dynastar M-Pro 108 Ti** — 138.5-109.8-128.7 (192); 22 m; 2448/2499 g; 68/6/26; 191.9; -13.3 / 82.7; 182, 192; beech/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. "Love letter" to old big-mountain comp skis (like Dynastar Pro Rider); very heavy, exceptional suspension; rearward mount, big swing weight; hard to find its speed limit; eliminates harsh vibration; needs speed/aggression but flex not punishing. One of best current skis for experienced directional skiers wanting top suspension and big fast turns.
- **Völkl Katana V-Werks** (charger listing; same specs). Very different take: early-Katana shape but drastically lighter; quickest/most agile here; excellent big-mountain pow ski; big sweet spot; one of dampest carbon skis tested — but least stable in section by sizable margin. Can be skied remarkably hard for weight in soft-ish snow; charger personality light enough for BC.

**Blister Spectrums — All-Mountain Chargers** (top → bottom):
- Best in Powder → Worst: Prior Husume; Völkl Katana V-Werks; Dynastar M-Pro 108 Ti; Völkl Mantra 108; Folsom Giver; 4FRNT MSP 107; Folsom Primary 104; Blizzard Anomaly 102.
- Easiest in Tight Terrain → Most Work: Katana V-Werks; Primary 104; Husume; Giver; MSP 107; Mantra 108; Anomaly 102; M-Pro 108 Ti.
- Least Demanding → Most Demanding: Katana V-Werks (needs precision, but less strength since light); Husume (stiff/heavy but taper & deep rocker make it easier); MSP 107; Primary 104; Mantra 108; Giver; Anomaly 102; M-Pro 108 Ti (due to size & weight; not ultra-stiff).

### All-Mountain Freestyle Skis (src ~8100-)
Class overview (Luke Koppa / Dylan Wood photos): more maneuverable, forgiving, suited to spinning/flipping/switch/playful approach — but no backflips required. Suit (a) experienced skiers wanting playful maneuverable ride, (b) feature hunters, (c) those taking a few park laps, (d) intermediates wanting forgiving easy-turning skis. Some narrow enough for park, some wide for pow. Most encourage centered upright stance; symmetrical consistent flex helps balance. Don't write off because of the word "freestyle."

- **Fischer Nightstick 97** — 132-96.5-123 (184); 18 m; 1944/1952 g; 56.5/8.5/51; 182.3; -3.4 / 87.7; 163-184; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Well-balanced do-everything freestyle ski; Ranger-like ride quality/stability in symmetrical playful design; between Unleashed 98 and M-Free 100 on precise vs surfy; easy to throw sideways with solid edge hold underfoot; mid swing weight/stability; directional skiers may prefer Unleashed 98.
- **J Skis Allplay** — 123.5-96.5-120.5 (186); 20.5 m; 2009/2015 g; 57/5.5/49.5; 185.5; -3.5 / 88.1; 158-186; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Overall playfulness, park or everywhere; all reviewers found it intuitive — easy pick for beginners/intermediates; experienced freestyle skiers use features creatively at lower speeds; soft flex easy butters/slashes; not max support/stability. Good for venturing off piste.
- **Season Kin** — 139.5-97.6-135.5 (181); 15 m; 1824/1843 g; 61/6/62; 179.2; -2.6 / 87; 163, 175, 181; karuba + fiberglass. Soft, agile, uniquely responsive; deep rocker, easy to bend/slash, tight radius + impressive edge hold — one of most exciting carvers in section without effort; easy in trees/bumps, predictable off piste; sketchy in big fast turns; very engaging at low speeds/small features. Variable-snow composure → Season Nexus.
- **Nordica Unleashed 98** (unisex 186: 134.3-97.6-123.4; 18.8 m; 1938/2003 g; 73/3.5/57.5; 183.6; -7.3 / 84.5; 156-186). One of most versatile here — directional precision + occasional park; minimal rocker/taper = excellent carver, not surfiest; firm steeps edge hold/energy but slippable (vs Enforcer); recommended line for directional; ~-5 cm for strong lively freestyle ski. Studio 2 more balanced/surfy strong ski; Kin/Reckoner 102 more engaging slow.
- **Rossignol Sender Free 100** (new; unisex 184: 130.5-100.5-122.5; 20 m; 1984/1986 g; 63.5/8/46; 181.6; -3.2 / 87.6; 162-190). Narrower, lighter, more agile Sender Free 110; 110 much damper in variable, 100 more park-practical, nimble, lively; stands out for edge hold and energy; easier to slash in soft snow; strong centered-stance preference (learning curve). Smoother/more off-piste maneuverable → M-Free 100.
- **Dynastar M-Free 100** (new; replaces M-Free 99; "Hybrid Core 2.0") — 130-100.5-123 (185); 18 m; 1951/2003 g; 72/8.5/51; 181.3; -7.7 / 83; 162-192; poplar/PU + fiberglass. Slightly stronger, more precise than M-Free 99 (whose edge hold was a weak point); still surfy for ~100s, excels in deeper pow/chop vs peers; good suspension/stability for weight; intuitive for directional skiers, freestyle skiers mount 1-2 cm forward; stiffer, not engaging at slow speed.
- **RMU Professor 101** (latest Professor) — 127.5-100.5-120.5 (186); 20.5 m; 1945/1972 g; 61.5/9/37; 184; -4.5 / 87.5; 168, 178, 186; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Wider Professors' nimble surfy ride on everyday platform; well-rounded; carves pretty well, easy slashes, middle flex; like RMU Apostles in suspension/swing weight but more balanced in air. M-Free 100/Jeffrey 100 more composed in heavy chop; Allplay more engaging slow.
- **ON3P Jeffrey 100** — 132.5-100.5-125.5 (186); 19.5 m; 1980/1995 g; 77.5/6/57; 186.6; -4.7 / 88.6; 161-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass.
