Jeffrey traits: strong flex, big rocker, composure in challenging snow; not for super firm carving; support/suspension to ski aggressively with balanced surfy platform; explosive pop once bent. Directional skiers → Woodsman; slow-speed engagement → Reckoner 102.
- **K2 Reckoner 102** — 133.5-101-126 (184); 19.4 m; 2040/2061 g; 61/7.5/56; 184.1; mounts Team -2.1/89.9, Midsole -4.2/87.8; 163-191; aspen + carbon stringers & spectral braid + fiberglass. Class-leading playfulness + versatility; soft flex, easy butters, forgiving; surprisingly surfy given moderate rocker; fun carver; not stiff/supportive (→ K2 Omen Team in Park), not lowest swing weight; doesn't fall apart faster/bigger. Variable → Reckoner 110, Reckoner KF.
- **Faction Studio 2** — 130.5-101.5-126.5 (183); 20 m; 1917/1934 g; 61.5/7/58.5; 180.7; mounts Newschool -3.7/86.7, Progressive -5.2/85.2, Classic -6.7/83.7; 166-188; poplar + carbon/rubber edge reinforcement + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Freestyle design tuned for fast aggressive skiing; stiff, lots of pop, light and balanced in air; agile, precise off piste (more maneuverable than Unleashed 98); carves very well, tighter than stated radius; not for low-speed jibs/butters; predictable at speed for light stiff ski; intuitive for directional skiers on rearward lines.
- **Völkl Revolt 104** — 131.4-103.6-121.6 (188); radii 27/21/25; 1973/1997 g; 68.5/3.5/43; 186.7; -1.7 / 91.6; 172, 180, 188; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Freestyle-first, familiar to park skiers; strong centered-stance preference (even mounted a few cm back); balanced quick ride on snow/air; shovels knocked around fast in variable; tail punishing in tight bumps (188); rare surfiness + landing support. Slow-speed playful → J Vacation, Reckoner 102; shovel-drivers → next skis or Revolt 101.
- **J Skis Vacation** — 126.3-103.7-122.3 (186); 20.1 m; 2010/2018 g; 70/3/53; 183.3; -3.7 / 87.9; 166-186; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Relaxed, fun-loving; 186 balances all-mountain and jibby playfulness; shorter lengths notably softer (more like Reckoner 102); 186 sits between Reckoner 102 and Deathwish 104 stiffness. Accessible flex, balanced. Firmer snow → narrower Allplay.
- **Moment Deathwish 104** — 132.7-103.8-124.1 (184); 22 m; 1970/1993 g; 67.5/4/66.5; 181.8; -5.8 / 85.1; 164-190; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Stiff + Triple Camber: power and playfulness; deep rocker surfy; camber pockets give excellent grip when driven on firm; low swing weight, very poppy; strong, not super damp; learning curve. New Deathwish 96 not yet tested (for low-snow locales).
- **Whitedot Altum 104** — 134.3-104.4-124.4 (187); 22 m; 2049/2053 g; 67/3.5/55; 185.5; -5.9 / 86.8; 171, 179, 187; poplar/ash + carbon/kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Seamless freestyle + directional versatility; forward or neutral, skis switch, stiff enough to push; minimal taper/low rocker = excellent carving for class, still pivots; not as forgiving as Vacation/Dirty Bear XL; high speed limit in soft snow without heavy weight. Like Wildcat 108 (Altum better carver, slightly less surfy/stable in chop). Easy recommendation as true all-rounder.
- **FullSend Max K 106** — 138.5-104.5-129 (187); 18 m; 2077/2083 g; 62/0/50 (reverse-camber version measured); 185.2; -5.1 / 87.6; 187; bamboo/poplar + fiberglass + carbon stringer. One of FullSend's few stock models (custom otherwise); choose cambered or reverse camber and optional small swallowtail — differences not drastic (camber more bite, reverse more surf). Maneuverable, floats well, respectable stability, adaptable; fairly stiff, stance-agnostic; like Wildcat 108/Altum 104; handmade in small US batches.
- **Fischer Nightstick 104** — 140-104.5-129.5 (188); 20 m; 2090/2122 g; 56.5/7.5/54; 186.1; -3.4 / 89.7; 172, 180, 188; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Widest Nightstick; burlier, more centered-biased than 97; composed in chop at speed but quicker than heaviest here; mid-spectrum carving/surfiness. Forward stance → ARV 106, Altum 104. Softer → Dirty Bear XL.
- **Majesty Dirty Bear XL** — 139.4-104.6-129.5 (186); radii 18/20/21/19; 1960/1962 g; 59/1.5/51; 184.5; -3.9 / 88.3; 162-186; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Doesn't fit a niche: impressive on-piste edge hold yet easy to slash in soft snow; supportive middle but easy to butter/press; surfier → Devastator. Park-ski initiation/grip with better soft-snow handling; lively, poppy off lips, low swing weight; more playful than previous few. Read full review.
- **Season Nexus** — 135.4-105.2-131.9 (183); 17.5 m; 2073/2074 g; 53/5/48; 181.5; -2.6 / 88.1; 158-189; karuba + fiberglass. Welcome variety; near-symmetrical ~106; blast to carve, surprisingly surfy, damp/supportive enough for fairly high speeds; like Line Bacon 108 (centered, engaging carver) but stronger with lower swing weight; more exciting on piste than stiff straight skis (Jeffrey 108) but not a soft jib stick; learning curve for directional skiers.
- **Armada ARV 106** (unisex 188: 131.5-105.5-124; 22 m; 2015/2170 g; 56/7.5/54.5; 186.2; Freestyle -2.5/90.6, Factory -5/88.1; 164-188). Closer than ever to do-everything freestyle; intuitive for many styles; does many things well — safe bet if unsure; alternative to stiffer directional (Unleashed 108, Altum 104) and softer freestyle-specific (Dirty Bear XL, Bacon 108).
- **4FRNT Devastator** — 135.5-107.2-130.6 (186); 21 m; 1947/2011 g; 60/0/55; 183.3; -4.7 / 86.9; 172, 179, 186; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Far from a bruiser — among most nimble/accessible; reverse camber, low weight — extremely surfy; predictable and fun carving on non-bulletproof firm; knocked around in firm crud but composed for weight; super low swing weight, easy last-second adjustments. Heavier damp surfy → M-Free 108, Jeffrey 108. Top pick for tight terrain slashes.
- **Nordica Unleashed 108** (unisex 186: 141.5-107.5-130.5; 19.7 m; 2077/2097 g; 77/3/59.5; 183.6; -7.7 / 84.1; 162-191). Specs not "freestyle" but room for creativity; carves impressively, better edge hold/precision on firm than similar widths; loose in pow (wide minimally tapered tips plane); middle ground in weight/flex; forward or neutral stance; mount a few cm forward for balance.
- **Dynastar M-Free 108** — 137-107.6-127.4 (192); 20 m; 2322/2322 g; 64/9.5/50.5; 189.6; -8.3 / 86.5; 162-192; poplar/PU + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Still one of best for particularly damp, stable, yet playful ski; strong flex, crud-eating suspension at speed; surfy but needs more physical effort; directional skiers fine, center-mount fans push bindings forward. Excels in challenging conditions/bigger terrain. See M-Free 112.
- **Line Bacon 108** — 141.4-107.6-137 (184); 17.4 m; 2194/2208 g; 61/6/55; 184; -4 / 88; 166-190; maple/aspen + fiberglass. Bacon playfulness with twists; easy to bend, symmetrical, engaging slow, balanced; fun on piste + good float/maneuverability; swing weight lighter than weight; weight reduces playfulness vs old light Bacons; damp but neither ultra-nimble nor very stable (soft flex). Lighter/stiffer → Season Nexus; firmer conditions → Line Chronic 101 (Park).
- **Moment Wildcat 108** (minor update: better turn initiation, smoother flex transitions) — 134.0-107.7-126.9 (184); 24 m; stated 2100 g; 74/5/71; 181.8; -6.1 / 84.8; 174-190; poplar/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Rewards quick reflexes and aggression from trees to bowls; drive shovels hard or centered; balanced; low weight, deep rocker — nimble and surfy; strong flex encourages aggressive skiing except firmest; not planted, not easy to bend slow. Long a reference ski for the class.
- **Majesty Vanguard 108 Ti** (new) — 134-108-124 (180); radii 20/22/20/18; 1930/1939 g; 61/2.5/48.5; 178.8; -5.2 / 84.2; 168-186; poplar/ash + 1 titanal + fiberglass. Practical daily-driver version of Vanguard 118 Ti; stiff, prefers upright centered stance; nimble for size, capable at speed in soft snow; not amazing suspension on firm but holds edge, aggressive GS turns. Dirty Bear XL more playful slow. Lighter no-metal Vanguard 108 Carbon for touring.
- **ON3P Jeffrey 108** — 138.5-109-132 (186); 21.3 m; 2168/2169 g; 76.5/5/58.5; 186.9; -4.8 / 88.7; 171-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Top pick for freestyle skiers prioritizing suspension and high-speed composure; above-average stability in soft chop/crud; heavy rocker = easy slashes; not soft but lots of rebound once you leverage rocker; current version a tad quicker. Others carve ice better; excellent big and fast in soft/variable.
- **Atomic Bent 110** (same specs). Purest for creativity/surfy soft-snow laps; light, loose, balanced; super low swing weight + stiff midsection great for tricks; among least stable in crud/dense chop — reserve for soft/consistent snow; amazing 50/50 or BC freestyle ski. Deeper → Bent Chetler 120; more stability/versatility → Reckoner 110.
- **K2 Reckoner 110** — 137.5-109.5-134.5 (184); 22 m; 2070/2103 g; 75.5/5.5/67.5; 183.7; -5.5 / 86.4; 170-191; aspen + carbon stringers + spectral braid + fiberglass. Very playful yet capable in poor conditions; deep splayed rocker, accessible flex = easy slash/bend/pop; respectable high-speed composure (big sweet spot); carves "way better than it has any right to." Very stable freestyle → Sender Free 110. No obvious weaknesses; more out-there → Reckoner KF.
- **Prior Northwest 110** — 138.5-110.0-131.2 (190); 23 m; 2188/2190 g; 64/4.5/59; 189.2; -3.1 / 91.5; 178, 184, 190 (custom); yellow cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). One of most confidence-inspiring for freestyle approach to natural features/challenging conditions; tested "quad-glass" build: high speed limit, great suspension; not quickest (lighter carbon version exists), not fun slow on mellow terrain (strong flex, heavy swing weight). Damper alternative to Wildcat 108/Professor 111 with stronger centered preference. Cliff/cornice hunters.
- **Rossignol Sender Free 110** — 140.3-110.4-132.6 (184); 20 m; 2196/2211 g; 69/6/50; 181.5; -3.4 / 87.4; 160-191; poplar + partial titanal + rubber inserts + Air Tip + carbon & fiberglass. Bigger tricks in bigger terrain: park design with serious freeride capability; one of best here for suspension and high-speed stability; centered symmetrical; holds edge better than previous version; looser in soft; stout/heavy — not for low-speed playfulness. Forward stance/surfier → M-Free 112, Sender Free 118.
- **RMU Professor 111** — 138-110.5-131 (188); 19.8 m; 1948/1957 g; 70/3/38.5; 186.3; -4.8 / 88.3; 168, 178, 188; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Few ~110s as quick, playful, well-rounded; low weight, middle flex — practical in shallow conditions; knocked around more in deep chop than heavier skis but much lower swing weight; more support than Bent 110/Bacon 108. More directional → RMU Apostle 114.
- **Dynastar M-Free 112** (freestyle listing; same specs). Standout for directional and park reviewers; big sweet spot, stance-tolerant; surfy with phenomenal suspension/stability; vs Sender Free 110: more intuitive for directional skiers, trades some firm precision for maneuverability and more forgiving flex; heavier swing weight but less than paper suggests (size up).
- **DPS Koala 111** (now made in DPS's Utah factory) — 140-111.1-127.6 (184); 18 m (all); 2180/2195 g; 68/4/60; 181.8; -6.9 / 84; 168-189; poplar + carbon stringers + ... (cont.). Outlier in DPS's light directional line; more composed in soft chop than light skis (Professor 111, Bent 110) but easier to bend/livelier than stout Sender Free 110/Northwest 110; forward or centered; soft-snow focused but not specialist — carves well, nice suspension...
  (Koala 111 cont.) core ends "+ fiberglass laminate"; for those caught between traditional directional and freestyle skis. Quicker (less damp) similarly versatile → Moment Deathwish 112.
- **Faction Studio 3** — 139.5-111.5-133.5 (184); 21 m; 2078/2091 g; 62/9/61.5; 182; mounts Newschool -5.6/85.4, Progressive -7.1/83.9, Classic -8.6/82.4; 165-190; poplar + carbon/rubber edge reinforcement + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Stiff freestyle shape for aggressive creative skiers; light, stout, symmetrical, minimal taper — high-speed tricks in soft snow; not exciting slow or very damp; pushable hard even with forward directional style; balanced, skis switch, energetic; carves very well for width. Studio 2 for shallower/firmer; Studio 3 for pow days and chop.
- **Moment Deathwish 112** — 137.6-111.9-129.3 (184); 25 m; stated 2050 g; 72/4/69; 182.2; -6.1 / 85; 174-190; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. 10+ years and still distinctive; Triple Camber = deep-rocker maneuverability + excellent edge hold from camber pockets; strong — blasts soft chop and carves firm better than weight/width suggest when driven; balanced/agile centered; not surfiest/most forgiving. Wide ski capable in pow and on firm. More float → Wildcat 118.
- **K2 Reckoner KF** (Karl Fostvedt pro model) — 137-113.5-133 (184); 23 m; 2176/2185 g; 68/3/64; 184.1; -4.2 / 87.9; 172-191; aspen + spectral braid + flax + carbon & fiberglass. Ultra-soft flex, radical rocker, extremely energetic core — easy to bend yet serious rebound; after adjustment, more versatile/capable at speed than nearly any similarly soft/surfy ski; not for driving through chop. Remarkably playful yet practical. More conventional → Reckoner 110.

**Blister Spectrums — All-Mountain Freestyle** (top → bottom):
- Most Stable in Crud → Least: Dynastar M-Free 112; Rossignol Sender Free 110 (191); M-Free 108 (192); Prior Northwest 110; ON3P Jeffrey 108; M-Free 108 (185); Moment Wildcat 108 (190); DPS Koala 111; Sender Free 110 (184); Armada ARV 106; Whitedot Altum 104; Faction Studio 3; Wildcat 108 (184); K2 Reckoner 110; Nordica Unleashed 108; FullSend Max K 106; Moment Deathwish 104; Deathwish 112; Faction Studio 2; Season Nexus; RMU Professor 111; Reckoner KF; Dynastar M-Free 100; Jeffrey 100; Fischer Nightstick 104; Line Bacon 108; Majesty Vanguard 108 Ti; Nightstick 97; Unleashed 98; Professor 101; Majesty Dirty Bear XL; 4FRNT Devastator; Sender Free 100; J Skis Vacation; Völkl Revolt 104; Reckoner 102; J Allplay; Atomic Bent 110; Season Kin.
- Most Park-Friendly → Least: Reckoner 102; Allplay; Revolt 104; Nightstick 97; Professor 101; Jeffrey 100; Sender Free 100; Studio 2; Kin; Vacation; Dirty Bear XL; Unleashed 98; Nexus; Nightstick 104; ARV 106; M-Free 100; Reckoner KF; Reckoner 110; Max K 106; Deathwish 104; Wildcat 108 (184); Professor 111; Devastator; Bacon 108; Bent 110; Vanguard 108 Ti; Jeffrey 108; Altum 104; Unleashed 108; Studio 3; Deathwish 112; M-Free 108 (185); Sender Free 110 (184); Wildcat 108 (190); Northwest 110; Koala 111; M-Free 112; Sender Free 110 (191); M-Free 108 (192).
- Most Surfy/Loose → Least: Devastator; Bent 110; Reckoner KF; M-Free 112; Reckoner 110; Koala 111; Professor 111; Max K 106; Jeffrey 108; Bacon 108; ARV 106; Wildcat 108 (184); Wildcat 108 (190); M-Free 108 (185); Vanguard 108 Ti; Studio 3; Deathwish 112; Vacation; Jeffrey 100; Professor 101; Reckoner 102; Deathwish 104; Nightstick 104; Nexus; Altum 104; M-Free 100; Northwest 110; M-Free 108 (192); Sender Free 110 (184); Dirty Bear XL; Allplay; Nightstick 97; Kin; Sender Free 100; Studio 2; Revolt 104; Unleashed 108; Sender Free 110 (191); Unleashed 98.

### Park Skis (src ~9030-)
Class overview (Dylan Wood, photos Taylor Ahearn): trend toward overall playfulness and versatility over max support/precision; ultra-stiff comp skis "an endangered species"; more options for slow-speed/small-feature creative skiers; more skis now cover both ends; many park skis perform well all over the mountain (see All-Mountain Freestyle for more).

- **K2 Omen 90** — 119-89.5-112.5 (179); 18 m; 1956/1978 g; 40.5/6/41; 180; -2 / 87.3; 149-185; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. More traditional: mostly cambered, strong flex, substantial core — more precise/supportive than most here; great on big features on firm snow; less maneuverable/more demanding slow vs Joyride or Jeffrey 92. Omen Team looser and more versatile. For those finding modern park skis lacking composure big and fast.
- **J Skis Joyride** — 117-89.5-113 (182); 21 m; 1825/1858 g; 59/4/47; 178.9; -4 / 85.5; 140-182; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Among most immediately accessible/playful ~90 mm park skis; soft, low swing weight, easy to whip around; intuitive and forgiving but little inherent stability — needs balanced takeoffs/landings on big features; fun in bumps/trees; good carver, tighter than stated radius. For agile forgiving ski on firm/shallow snow.
- **ON3P Jeffrey 92** — 125.5-93-119 (181); 17.9 m; 1792/1806 g; 65/7.5/50; 182; -4.6 / 86.4; 171-186; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Narrowest Jeffrey; strong lively flex in compact precise shape; shallower "Ripper" rocker but still more than most similar widths — maneuverable in soft snow; more firm precision/big-jump stability than wider Jeffreys; little energy at slow speed but explosive when flexed; low swing weight vs composure. A much stronger Joyride or more maneuverable/versatile Omen 90.
- **Armada BDOG 94** — 120.7-93.7-116.5 (180); 15 m; 1970/1990 g; 58/5/56; 178.2; 0 / 89.1 (true center); 164, 172, 180; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Embodies playfulness: very soft tips/tails transition smoothly to stiffer middle — no hinge points; tapered ends reduce catching on rails/butters; tight radius for quick carves; less stable on big jumps/very firm (short effective edge). Cohesive despite radical elements.
- **Head Oblivion 94** — 128.1-93.9-117.5 (184); 22.8 m; 1846/1857 g; 64/7/44; 181.7; mounts SC 0/90.5, All-Mountain -5/85.5; 163-184; poplar + elastomer reinforcements + fiberglass. Increasingly rare precision/agility/support mix; minimal taper/rocker = solid firm edge hold yet easy to release (more than Omen 90); low swing weight + stout flex — less damp in crud but reliable on big landings. Joyride better for butters; Omen 90 more planted. All-mountain → Oblivion 102.
- **K2 Omen Team** (replaces K2 Poacher) — 125-95.5-118.5 (184); 20 m; 2100/2115 g; 50/8.5/47; 185; -2 / 89.7; 163-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Competition roots but intuitive/adaptable; damp, stiff, capable on natural features; less precise/agile than Omen 90 but stiff, strong edge hold; enough rocker/taper/flex at ends for butters. Softer/lighter → Allplay. Stable platform for pushing pace.
- **J Skis Allplay** (park listing; same specs). Easy and fun to experiment, learning butters or creative tricks; soft, ideal for presses/jibs; less support on big features (land centered); respectable all-mountain stability for its flex; Joyride for hardpack; Allplay handles soft and firm, fine in slush/boot-top pow; capable forgiving all-mountain option.
- **Nordica Unleashed 98** (park listing). Viable wider park ski on precise/supportive side; strong midsection, little rocker/taper — one of more stable here; mounted a few cm forward = dependable all-round park ski between Omen Team and Allplay; can be hooky in inconsistent snow before detuning; not easy to bend; rarely out of place.
- **Prior Northwest 100** — 128.1-100.2-123.3 (184); 21 m; 1910/1919 g; 54/4.5/45; 182.8; -2.9 / 88.5; 158-190 (custom); yellow cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Customizable, Canadian-made; tested "traditional camber" version: narrow-park-ski traits on wider platform — impressive edge hold, excellent pop, stable on heavy landings; less maneuverable in variable, harder butters; low swing weight. "Hybrid Rocker" option should be more playful (like ON3P Mango 102). Variable snow → Northwest 110.
- **Line Chronic 101** — 129.1-100.4-124.2 (186); 16.6 m (179); 2126/2129 g (186); 59/4/50; 186; -4.5 / 89; 165-186; aspen + fiberglass. Soft flex + surprisingly substantial build — not twitchy in variable; energetic, balanced, swing weight lighter than weight; stands out outside park: excellent maneuverability, respectable composure in rough snow, fun on piste. Allplay more playful slow; Studio 2 more stable fast. Rare soft+calm combo.
- **K2 Reckoner 102** (park listing). Reviewer favorite: extremely playful yet cohesive; consistently soft — easy butters, forgiving off piste; not most stable/supportive but handles moderate jumps with centered landings; deep low rocker easy to throw sideways, carves non-icy snow; not lowest swing weight but nice suspension and burly build — optimistic on durability vs original Reckoner 102. Intuitive for new twin-tip converts and park vets.
- **Faction Studio 2** (park listing). Strong energetic flex in wider package for aggressive high-speed freestyle; lots of pop/amplitude; effort to bend at slow speed; narrower skis better firm precision/lower swing weight, Studio 2 more float and surfier in slush/variable; Reckoner 102/Chronic 101 more engaging slow; impressive composure/adaptability for moderate weight.
- **Head Oblivion 102** — 135.3-102.2-125.3 (189); 25.1 m; 1806/1884 g; 65/5/57; 186.7; mounts SC 0/93, All-Mountain -5/88; 173, 181, 189; poplar + recycled PET stringers + elastomer + carbon & fiberglass. Light build + fairly strong flex + minimal rocker + long radius = very low swing weight yet precise/stable for weight carving and on big landings; locked-in on firm, harder to pivot; solid edge, impressive high-speed composure vs light peers; rewards aggressive dynamic style like Studio 2 (Studio easier to slash/shuffle out of landings; Oblivion more agility/grip on firm).
- **ON3P Mango 102** (Jake 'Mango' Mageau collab; one of newest) — 130.5-103-127 (181); 18.6 m; 1970/2014 g; 76/5/56.5; 181.8; -1.6 / 89.3; 161-186; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Like Jeffrey 100 but more balanced/freestyle-focused; more agile/precise (more tapered ends, tighter radius); stiffer, more composed, and surfier than average; explosive pop; not best on ice. Uncommon stability/maneuverability/agility/energy. More accessible/directional-friendly → Jeffrey 100.
- **Salomon Départ 1.0** — 139.5-106-127 (180); 15 m; 1763/1783 g; 62/5/38; 178.4; -5.3 / 83.9; 170, 180; karuba/poplar + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Unusual: light, big taper, very deep rocker, stout flex, somewhat directional shape/mount — yet extremely playful; loves going sideways, very low swing weight; stiff midsection, excellent suspension-to-weight = respectable stability in soft-ish snow. Agile super-surfy supportive freestyle ski, or lighter freestyle-friendly QST 106.

**Blister Spectrums — Park** (top → bottom):
- Most Playful Overall → Least: Armada BDOG 94; J Skis Joyride; Salomon Départ 1.0; K2 Reckoner 102; J Allplay; Line Chronic 101; ON3P Mango 102; ON3P Jeffrey 92; Faction Studio 2; Head Oblivion 102; Nordica Unleashed 98; Prior Northwest 100; K2 Omen Team; K2 Omen 90; Head Oblivion 94.
- Most Stable on Jumps → Least: Omen 90; Omen Team; Northwest 100; Oblivion 94; Unleashed 98; Oblivion 102; Jeffrey 92; Studio 2; Mango 102; Départ 1.0; Reckoner 102; Chronic 101; Allplay; Joyride; BDOG 94.
- Most Versatile → Most Specialized: Northwest 100; Omen Team; Allplay; Reckoner 102; Jeffrey 92; Mango 102; Départ 1.0; Oblivion 102; Unleashed 98; Chronic 101; Studio 2; Joyride; Oblivion 94; Omen 90; BDOG 94.

### Powder Skis — More Directional (src ~9453-)
Class overview (Dylan Wood / Luke Koppa photos): line between playful and directional pow skis keeps blurring; not simply every fat ski with ~-9 cm or farther back mount; these aren't necessarily heavier, more stable, or straighter than More Playful — look at both sections. Generally for traditional forward stance, not spinning/switch.

- **Head Kore 112 Ti** (new; "biggest makeover" contender) — 139.5-111.5-127.5 (184); 21 m; 2224/2285 g; 64/6.5/33.5; 182.8; -9.1 / 82.3; 170-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Fans of old Kore 111's super-low swing weight should look elsewhere (50/50 section); now a different beast — better suspension/stability in chop/crud; hefty, not knocked off track; best forward but fairly surfy, less demanding than Revolt 114; easier to ski than most heavier stable skis here; doesn't plane readily in really deep snow but easy to turn submerged. More playful → Wildcat; more float → Kore 118 Ti.
- **Völkl Revolt 114** — 146-113.8-128 (184); radii 25/19/22; 2288/2291 g; 72/4/35; 182; -8 / 83; 177, 184, 191; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Strong, heavy, very stable but not old-school directional; deep rocker = easy release/pivot/slash; mount allows somewhat centered (not backseat); floats extremely well for size; loves bashing soft chop. Kore 112 Ti more forgiving/practical on firmer; Revolt 114 more float, surfier in deep.
- **Blizzard Rustler 11** — 142.3-114-132.6 (186); 20.5 m; 2082/2089 g; 81/3/49; 183.7; -7.6 / 84.2; 168-192; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. More a fat all-mountain ski: precision, energy, quickness over flotation/stability; easy to ski, respectable stability for weight; not out of place on firm — impressive carver, nimble in bumps/trees; heavier skis better in chop; others float better at moderate speed. Versatile for few inches to ~1 ft. More float, similar agility → Blaze 114.
- **Völkl Blaze 114** (pow listing; same specs). Different from Revolt 114 despite similar shape; shovels don't submerge easily even when very deep; centered or forward, easy carve/slash; low swing weight; carves groomers, fine in shallow/firm; main drawback doesn't blow through resort chop (heavier skis much better). Easygoing, intuitive, not sluggish on low-snow days.
- **Atomic Maverick 115 CTI** — 140-114.5-129 (185); 18 m; 2117/2132 g; 61.5/6.5/29.5; 184; -9.5 / 82.5; 177, 185, 193; ash/poplar + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Well-rounded for maneuverability/agility on directional platform; easygoing/forgiving like Bent cousins; lots of taper — one of looser here, rivaling More Playful skis, excellent in tight technical terrain; soft shovels + directional mount = impressive float even for shovel-drivers; not most stable or quickest; works mounted 1-2 cm forward. Damper surfy → ON3P Billy Goat 118.
- **Heritage Lab RC116** — 140-117-130 (190); 31.5 m; 2497/2583 g; 72/4/35; 189.5; -9.7 / 85; 184, 190; poplar/bamboo + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Inspired by cult classics (first-gen Blizzard Bodacious, Rossignol RC112): big, stiff, heavy, directional; extremely stable/powerful, shines in heavy maritime snow; needs physical effort and forward stance, but breaks into drifts fairly easily; powerful on edge though suspension not as plush as original Bodacious or Heritage Lab's heavier/softer R120 ST. For strong directional skiers at high speed in big terrain.
- **Moment Wildcat 118** (minor tweaks) — 142-117.5-133 (190); 27 m; 2371/2375 g; ... (cont.) Deservedly popular "playful charger"; width now standardized at 118 mm for all lengths, dual-radius sidecut tweaked for intuitive initiation, flex subtly smoothed...
  (Wildcat 118 cont.) 63.5/6/68; 187.8; -5.9 / 88.1; 174-196; poplar/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Blister needs time on the latest but expects fans to like it. Rare combo of directional stability and freestyle playfulness; very composed in chop/crud yet looser/quicker than heavy rearward-mounted skis. Billy Goat 118 and Folsom Rapture float better in really deep snow. For those between directional and playful classes.
- **Head Kore 118 Ti** — 144.5-117.5-129.5 (184); 24.3 m; 2238/2257 g; 63/5.5/33.5; 183.1; -7 / 84.5; 177, 184, 191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Widest new Kore; arguably most stable and most playful of the collection; like 112 Ti (deeper rocker, more taper, less set-back mount than narrower Kore Ti) but looser, more balanced in air, more forgiving backseat; both impressive suspension; 112 more powerful on shallower/firm, 118 easier to pivot in deep. For directional skiers wanting suspension without demands, and playful skiers prioritizing high-speed composure.
- **ON3P Billy Goat 118** — 146-119-128.5 (186); 29 m; 2334/2353 g; 79.5/3.5/32.5; 186; -9.8 / 83.2; 176-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Longstanding: damp sturdy build with very surfy yet directional shape; deep rocker, short effective edge, long radius — easy drifts from forward stance; not for slow tight carves on firm; calm in big open-face turns; somewhat pin-tailed, rearward mount — tips float, tails sink; planes with little speed/pitch; skis hard once pow skied out. More balanced/centered → Folsom Rapture.
- **Folsom Rapture** — 149.4-120.9-138.4 (192); 28 m; 2288/2301 g; 74/0/34.5; 190.5; -7.7 / 87.5; 178-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Still one of Paul Forward's (AK heli guide) all-time favorites; "Shallow Reverse Camber," fairly heavy: excellent stability everywhere, very good float except deepest/lightest snow, easy smooth drifts; composure in high-speed runouts over avy debris as good or better than most; narrower skis more versatile in resorts but one of best big-mountain pow skis.
- **Heritage Lab R120 ST** — 144-121-139 (193); 27 m; 2689/2717 g; 60/0/35; 193; -8 / 88.5; 186, 193; poplar/bamboo + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. One of heaviest skis reviewed; top-shelf suspension; progressive mount, forgiving flex — less old-school than RC116; good float, soft tips plane at low speed; exceptionally damp at speed, but pushed around a bit more than stiffer heavy skis; intuitive carving yet easy drifts; stance-tolerant. Vs Rapture: Rapture lower swing weight and more composed in big fast turns; R120 ST plusher.
- **4FRNT Renegade** (Hoji's signature pow ski) — 136.7-121.2-130.4 (191); 30 m; 2173/2204 g; 95/0/35.5; 188.9; -5.3 / 89.1; 177, 184, 191; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Playful dynamic in tight terrain and high-speed arcs in open; long radius, reverse camber — calm in long carves in clean snow; easy to feather/release/throw sideways; deflected more in chop than Wildcat 118/Chipotle Banana; tips plane in any snow; predictable in breakable crust. Surfier, more centered alternative to Maverick 115 CTI; lighter touring → UT122.
- **Moment Chipotle Banana** (limited-edition runs) — 140-122-134 (193); 35.5 m; 2323/2352 g; 71/0/71; 190; -5.8 / 89.2; 178, 186, 193; poplar/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Fully rockered fat waist suggests pow tool, but weight, straight shape, stout flex = high-speed charger in all conditions; not impressive flotation for width, but blown away by chop/crud handling — plows rough inbounds snow better than almost any >120 mm ski; easy to break into quick skids. R120 ST better float and more planted in firm crud; Chipotle quicker, slightly better through deep chop. Agility → Renegade.
- **Heritage Lab UT122** (pow listing; same specs). Nod to endangered reverse-camber/reverse-sidecut pow skis; best comparison Renegade (more stable in chop) — UT122 looser, more stance-adaptable, much lighter; flotation comparable to other ~120s; standout ease of pivoting/drifting in steep or mellow terrain; excellent light yet damp touring construction. Not for resort chop. Intuitive surfy touring pow ski.
- **DPS Lotus 124** — 146-124.6-134 (191); 23 m (all); stated 2200 g; 76/6/35; 189; -11 / 84; 178, 185, 191; poplar + carbon. Directional pow ski emphasizing easygoing maneuverability/agility; not stable/supportive in big turns in deep/variable; easy slash/pivot/carve at moderate speed; like Kaizen 112 / old Wailer 112 (low swing weight, slarvy), longer radius aids extended drifts/stability. High-speed big terrain → Rapture, R120 ST; max quickness → UT122. More engaging at slower speeds/mellower terrain.
- **Faction La Machine 5** — 147.5-125.5-138.5 (192); 27 m; 1947/1981 g; 72.5/7/34; 190.2; mounts Newschool -10.6/84.5, Progressive -12.1/83, Classic -13.6/81.5; 178, 186, 192; paulownia + carbon & fiberglass. Original (subtle reverse camber) was a favorite pow touring ski; current version has significant underfoot camber — harder to drift, more prone to hooking, needs more weighting/unweighting; less intuitive across turn styles (UT122 much surfier). Still worthwhile light touring pow ski with impressive float for directional skiers.
- **Folsom Rotor** — 157.5-131-147.5 (193); 25 m; 2343/2376 g; 73/0/58.5; 190.3; -7.2 / 88; 184-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). ~132 mm, based on Rapture (well-rounded AK heli ski); ~10 mm wider, modified shape/rocker for deepest days; nearly all Rapture traits carry over, planes even quicker in bottomless snow, slightly shorter radius eases initiation. Consensus: excellent heli ski, esp. open AK terrain; works best slightly softer than a comparable Rapture (tail softer than tip). Rapture more practical for most.
- **K2 Crescendo** — stated 155-132-144 (186); 22 m; 2177/2200 g; rocker/camber/rocker; 185.5; -12.2 / 80.5; 159-186; aspen/paulownia + carbon spectral braid + fiberglass. One of few big brands still making a really wide dedicated pow ski; bigger folks may want longer length; stable, excellent tip flotation; not as loose in drifts (esp. steep) as similarly wide skis; easy initiation, near-limitless buoyancy on moderate slopes; more intuitive on firm than old K2 Pontoon. Narrower heavier skis better for chop; Crescendo more buoyant/easygoing in untracked laps.

**Blister Spectrums — Powder More Directional** (top → bottom):
- Most Flotation → Least: Folsom Rotor; K2 Crescendo; Faction La Machine 5; Folsom Rapture; Heritage Lab UT122; ON3P Billy Goat 118; Heritage Lab R120 ST; 4FRNT Renegade (191); DPS Lotus 124; Heritage Lab RC116; Moment Chipotle Banana; Moment Wildcat 118 (190); Völkl Blaze 114; Renegade (184); Völkl Revolt 114; Atomic Maverick 115 CTI; Head Kore 118 Ti; Wildcat 118 (184); Head Kore 112 Ti; Blizzard Rustler 11.
- Best in Deep Chop → Worst: Chipotle Banana; Wildcat 118 (190); RC116; Rapture; R120 ST; Revolt 114; Wildcat 118 (184); Kore 118 Ti; Rotor; Billy Goat 118; Kore 112 Ti; Maverick 115 CTI; Renegade (191); Rustler 11; Blaze 114; Renegade (184); Lotus 124; Crescendo; La Machine 5; UT122.
- Best in Refrozen Crap → Worst: R120 ST; Kore 112 Ti; RC116; Wildcat 118 (190); Kore 118 Ti; Revolt 114; Wildcat 118 (184); Chipotle Banana; Rapture; Billy Goat 118; Rustler 11; Renegade (191); Maverick 115 CTI; Blaze 114; Renegade (184); Rotor; Lotus 124; Crescendo; La Machine 5; UT122.

### Powder Skis — More Playful (src ~9976-)
Class overview (Luke Koppa / Paul Forward at Chugach Powder Guides photos): increasingly interesting and hard to describe; less about driving shovels, more about surfing/smearing/tricking with upright centered stance; includes some of the most playful and also some of the most stable skis in guide; reviewers with no interest in airs/switch still love several here.

- **Armada ARV 112** (pow listing; 185: 134.5-111.5-129.5; 21 m; 1998/2026 g; 55/7/54; 183.4; Freestyle -2.8/88.9, Factory -5.2/86.5; 165-192). Agile freestyle pow ski with backbone; playful end of section, not very stable in variable but more well-rounded than ARV 116 JJ UL; for freestyle skiers prioritizing quickness/pivoting; can be skied aggressively in soft snow centered and light on feet; ends easy to bend, solid middle. More directional agility → Nomad 112, Apostle 114; surfier/stabler deep → Völkl Revolt 121. Practical with just a few inches.
- **Icelantic Nomad 112** — stated 147-112-139 (188); 20 m; 2026/2118 g; 67.5/4/58; 185.2; mounts PC -5.2/87.4, BC -7.2/85.4; 171-194; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Standout adaptability: moderate flex, deep rocker, minimal taper = accessible, maneuverable, pretty stable in chop; stance-tolerant; few obvious weaknesses; freestyle skiers confident at speed, directional skiers like easygoing maneuverability; big rockered shovels float very well; damp/composed for not-heavy ski.
- **Salomon S/Lab QST Blank** (unisex 186: 139-112-128.5; 19 m; 2187/2215 g; 69/5.5/37; 184.5; -7.5 / 84.8; 170-192). Updated: more playful/maneuverable, looser, lighter, stiffer tail; very easy to slash; stance-tolerant but less forgiving of backseat than ARV 112/Bacon 115 — upside more supportive landings in deep snow; original was damper; still respectable stability vs equally easygoing skis. Planted → Wildcat 118, Sender Free 118; surfier/lighter → QST X.
- **Whitedot Altum 114** — 142.5-113-131.3 (187); 24 m; 2122/2151 g; 63/3/58.5; 185.5; -5.9 / 86.8; 179, 187; poplar/ash + carbon/kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Not a specialized pow tool; fairly light, moderate taper + strong flex = very good stability in forgiving conditions; deep but low-slung rocker easy to throw sideways yet engages edge in carves; not highly surfy; damp chargers → Sender Free 118. Like Nomad 112 (Nomad more forgiving/playful; Altum stronger/more precise). Middle-of-preferences ski.
- **Line Optic 114** — 138.3-113.4-130.9 (186); 24 m; 2318/2322 g; 65.5/1.5/47.5; 185.2; -7.8 / 85.2; 178, 186, 192; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. More charger than most here with freestyle DNA; one of best for high-speed composure in soft chop, much less deflected than lighter skis; easy to throw sideways, natural in air, skis switch; not super forgiving esp. on firm; drive hard in variable, drifty in fresh. More forgiving symmetrical slow-speed playful → Bacon 115.
- **Meier Leeper** — 146-113.5-138 (185); 19 m; 2216/2246 g; 65/4/38; 183.7; -4.5 / 87.5; 171-192; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Built for big airs, friendly for everyday; hefty, strong, good suspension, fine at speed but fun without Mach 10; moderately centered mount, deep-ish rocker = easy slash/pivot, also bigger turns. Sender Free 118/Optic 114 better high-speed composure; Leeper lower swing weight, more practical on firm; directional reviewers liked it; not class-leading deep-snow float/maneuverability but needs less fresh snow.
- **Nordica Unleashed 114** — 147-113.6-135.7 (186); 20.4 m; 2086/2088 g; 78/6/57; 183.7; -8 / 83.5; 174-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Uncommon: playful not soft, directional not demanding, light not flimsy; mount closer to center for freestyle; very impressive float for 114; looser in soft than firm; great carver for size. Not super loose, ultra-playful, or very damp in chop. Like a softer/floatier Altum 114 or stiffer/lighter Nomad 112.
- **RMU Apostle 114** — 142.5-114-131.5 (184); 22 m; 2086/2125 g; 66.5/4.5/35; 182.5; mounts FR -4.7/86.5, TR -6.7/84.5; 176, 184; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Quick, playful with freeride-inspired stiffness; more directional than Professor 121 but stands out for nimble, surfy, playful ride; not damp — pop and slash through chop rather than plow; centered or directional; needs little fresh. For freestyle backgrounds wanting nimble + supportive, or directional skiers wanting surfy without super-centered stance.
- **Line Bacon 115** — 147.6-114.2-143.2 (188); 18.4 m; 2260/2290 g; 61/4/54; 188.2; -4.1 / 90; 168, 178, 188; maple/aspen + fiberglass. Center-mounted, soft, minimally tapered, tighter radius — freestyle-focused, playful; but hefty — notably better suspension/stability in crud than lighter ARV 112 / Bent Chetler 120; not a charger. Rare combo for freestyle-design fans wanting damp ride. Directional/composure → Optic 114.
- **Black Crows Anima** — 146.2-114.8-135.5 (189); 19 m; 2259/2279 g; 64/6/55; 187.4; -6.3 / 87.4; 176.6-194.4; poplar + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Compromise: capable at speed without always being on the gas; fairly heavy, composed in chop/crud, easy to edge, not punishing (more forgiving than Wildcat 118); balanced in air, can land switch in most snow; not low swing weight or easy butters; moderate rocker carves/handles firm well, looser in deep. One of more well-rounded here; heavier alternative to Unleashed 114/Nomad 112.
- **Head Oblivion 116** — 141.5-116-131.5 (189); 30.8 m; 1998/2024 g; 66/7/55; 187.2; mounts SC 0/93.6, All-Mountain -5/88.6; 181, 189; poplar + recycled PET stringers + elastomer + carbon & fiberglass. Different recipe: stiff, light, long radius; easy to slash in fresh, calm in big fast fall-line turns (more than its low swing weight suggests); poor suspension — soft-snow-specific tool; mount range for stances; excellent slashing/airing feature-filled pow lines; few as agile, balanced, and stable in fresh snow. Chop/crud → Prior Northwest 116, ON3P Jeffrey 118.
- **Salomon S/Lab QST X** (pow listing; same as 50/50). Excellent deep-snow maneuverability at touring weight; vs QST Blank: much lighter, surfier, more centered-stance biased; extremely easy to slash; light + short effective edge = not composed in dense chop (ski cautiously/dynamically); neutral stance in deep, can drive shovels in shallow; fairly stiff, no true twin — not very freestyle-focused; versatile turn shapes in untracked.
- **4FRNT Inthayne** (Thayne Rich) — 137.3-116.3-128.6 (188); 25 m (all); 2043/2046 g; 79.5/0/75; 185.4; -5.8 / 86.9; 181, 188; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Blurs freestyle playfulness and freeride stability: straight-ish shape, strong flex, low weight, progressive mount, reverse camber; far from loosest — can drive hard on edge in shallow/firm; heavier skis better in crud, but more composed in soft chop/pow than similarly light skis. Looser/more stable alternative to Oblivion 116. Quick + supportive, for high-speed runouts more than low-angle wiggling.
- **Prior CBC** — 139.6-116.5-137.5 (184); 16.5 m; 2147/2286 g; 64/3/61; 182.3; -2.1 / 89; 178-193 (custom); yellow cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Unapologetically focused: very balanced freestyle ski, stout flex, damp, surfy shape — exceptional for playful approach to steep, techy, feature-filled terrain; at home in air, composed in variable vs lighter skis, very solid landings; quicker/surfier than weight suggests but less big-turn stable than Wildcat 118/Sender Free 118. Shallower/firm → Northwest 116.
- **Prior Northwest 116** — 141.8-116.7-133.8 (190); 23 m; 2318/2377 g; 62/3/51; 188.9; -2.9 / 91.5; 178, 184, 190 (custom); yellow cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). More stable, well-rounded CBC counterpart: less taper, longer radius — floats better, blasts chop better; heavier swing weight, less surfy; for freestyle skiers skiing fast in fresh and chop; strong, hefty, unflustered by bad snow or big features. Carbon version for quick/touring; tested "Quad-Glass" one of dampest, most stable yet balanced freestyle skis here. Surfier similar → Jeffrey 118; directional → Wildcat 118, Optic 114.
- **J Skis Best Friend** (cover ski) — 140.5-117-130 (191); 21 m; 2259/2326 g; 63/3/49.5; 189.6; ... (cont.) Asymmetrical shape to make pow "easier for everyone" — succeeds; intuitive, balanced, drifts with very little effort from resort inches to bottomless BC; best in tighter, moderate-angle terrain rather than steep open faces; soft tips plane easily...
  (Best Friend cont.) -6.5 / 88.3; 177, 184, 191; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Surfy turns from centered or slightly forward; bigger/aggressive skiers may want more front support in big fast turns in heavy pow/dense chop; shape takes getting used to. Very playful, super surfy, easygoing, surprisingly intuitive.
- **Rossignol Sender Free 118** — 145.5-117.5-140.5 (186); 25 m; 2457/2533 g; 64/7/60; 184.2; -2.7 / 89.3; 176, 186; poplar + AirTip + fiberglass. All-time favorite of several reviewers: one of dampest/most stable in resort soft chop yet balanced, surfy, forgiving; absurdly calm and surefooted, not quick to punish; not nimble/light, not best-in-class float; centered stance (directional skiers mount ~-5/-6 cm). Narrower → M-Free 112.
- **Moment Wildcat 118** (playful listing). Longtime "playful charger"; stiffer/heavier than many here — one of best mobbing through soft chop; versatile post-storm; for very directional and very playful skiers; more energy than Sender Free 118 when flexed (needs effort). Minor tweaks to width/flex/sidecut this year; reviewers will report back. Go-to for not slowing down when tracked, without being a one-dimensional charger.
- **ON3P Jeffrey 118** — 146.5-119-138.5 (186); 23.5 m; 2377/2394 g; 81/3.5/56.5; 186.5; -4.7 / 88.6; 181, 186, 191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Freestyle shape + burly build: butter, slash, or power through pow and chop; deep rocker surfy, easier to press than Wildcat 118 though similarly stiff; smooth planted ride, a tad more agile/loose/lively than Sender Free 118/Northwest 116. Directional → Woodsman; more nimble/surfier → Revolt 121.
- **Atomic Bent Chetler 120** (playful listing; same specs). Top pick for max playfulness; very surfy, light, touring-friendly; less damp/stable in chop than most here, but loose, agile, forgiving; more capable than weight suggests in soft (even cut-up) snow. Damper similar loose feel → Revolt 121; directional/forward → Best Friend. For features on pow days.
- **RMU Professor 121** — 146.3-120.3-139.6 (188); 21 m; 2127/2161 g; 72/3/41; 186.5; -5 / 88.5; 178, 188; poplar + beech underfoot + fiberglass. Apostle 114 personality, wider with more rocker; soft deeply rockered ends — surfy, forgiving; strong midsection/wide footprint support off-center landings (more than Bacon 115); versatile deep-snow terrain; less intuitive for directional skiers than Apostle 114 but drivable; middle weight balances agility/suspension. More stable in rough → Revolt 121.
- **Völkl Revolt 121** — 142.5-120.4-134.2 (184); radii 20/19/20; 2240/2250 g; 87/4.5/53; 182.2; -2.8 / 88.3; 177, 184, 191; poplar/beech + fiberglass. For surfy-ski fans who dislike light sketchy feel in rough snow; deep rocker and very tapered — extremely easy to pivot/slarve; really nice suspension, strong middle — ski hard in chop and soft crud; few do both as well (Bent Chetler 120 looser; Sender Free 118 more stable). Directional/high-speed → Revolt 114, Wildcat 118.
- **Whitedot Ragnarok ASYM** — 144.8-120.9-132.9 (190); 30 m; 2062/2080 g; 72/3/37; 187.8; -7.4 / 86.5; 190; poplar/ash + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Funky asymmetric effective edge (more tip taper on outside edge) for balanced weighting and easier skids; dedicated pow tool (122 underfoot) — fun slarvy ride in consistent soft snow (blower to slush); skis short, not easy to carve firm; nice suspension for low weight; compelling 50/50 to haul uphill. Softer/damper → J Best Friend.
- **Black Crows Nocta** — 147-121-136.5 (190.6); 19 m (all); 2118/2124 g; 64/3.4/45; 187.5; -5.5 / 88; 177.6, 185.5, 190.6; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. More specialized deep-snow tool than Anima; easy to break into slarves/drifts despite not super-deep rocker; prefers centered/upright but drivable more than Revolt 121; fairly light, not a charger, but above its weight in chop/crud/crust; predictable on steep AK faces and low-angle runs; reasonable on skin track. New edition releasing this season.
- **K2 Reckoner 124** — 149-123.5-147 (189); 25 m; 2423/2446 g; 85/3/68; 188.3; -5.3 / 88.8; 159-189; aspen + spectral braid + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Big freestyle pow ski for BC booters but less specialized than its girth suggests; very deep splayed rocker = loose/surfy; substantial weight (189) adds suspension/composure in cut-up pow; more effort at slow speed — momentum is your friend. Chop focus → Sender Free 118, Jeffrey 118; max playfulness → Reckoner KF.
- **Line Pescado** — 157.5-124-144.5 (180); 19.5 m; 1808/1809 g; 66/5/22; 178.9; -9.8 / 79.6; 180; paulownia/maple + fiberglass. Technically very directional (swallowtail-style) but very playful; blast in soft snow where maneuverability matters; perfect for deep days in moderate terrain/tight trees thanks to big soft shovels and low weight; fun in slush, carves soft groomers, pops off features, very quick; not balanced in air or intuitive switch; rearward mount suits forward-stance skiers wanting lively surfy ski (slashes/carves over flips). Not a chop-buster; great for BC untracked in less extreme terrain.

**Blister Spectrums — Powder More Playful** (top → bottom):
- Most Surfy/Loose → Least: Whitedot Ragnarok ASYM; J Skis Best Friend; Salomon QST X; Völkl Revolt 121; Atomic Bent Chetler 120; Black Crows Nocta; Salomon QST Blank; Prior CBC; K2 Reckoner 124; ON3P Jeffrey 118; RMU Professor 121; Armada ARV 112; RMU Apostle 114; Line Optic 114; Moment Wildcat 118 (184); Black Crows Anima; Line Bacon 115; Icelantic Nomad 112; 4FRNT Inthayne; Meier Leeper; Rossignol Sender Free 118; Wildcat 118 (190); Whitedot Altum 114; Nordica Unleashed 114; Prior Northwest 116; Head Oblivion 116; Line Pescado.
- Lightest in the Air → Heaviest: Pescado; QST X; Bent Chetler 120; ARV 112; Oblivion 116; Apostle 114; Nocta; Unleashed 114; CBC; Ragnarok ASYM; Altum 114; Inthayne; QST Blank; Nomad 112; Revolt 121; Wildcat 118 (184); Professor 121; Leeper; Best Friend; Jeffrey 118; Bacon 115; Optic 114; Anima; Wildcat 118 (190); Northwest 116; Sender Free 118; Reckoner 124.
- Most Stable in Chop → Least: Wildcat 118 (190); Sender Free 118; Reckoner 124; Jeffrey 118; Optic 114; Anima; Northwest 116; Wildcat 118 (184); Revolt 121; Leeper; Altum 114; Nomad 112; Inthayne; Nocta; CBC; Bacon 115; Best Friend; Professor 121; QST Blank; Apostle 114; Oblivion 116; Unleashed 114; Bent Chetler 120; ARV 112; QST X; Ragnarok ASYM; Pescado.

### Ski Boots (src ~10852-)
Section overview (Ellsworth photo; Kristin Sinnott / Luke Koppa): best way to find boots is an experienced bootfitter — foot shape and biomechanics matter most. Then be clear on how/where you ski. Three classes: (1) Alpine (fixed cuff, no walk mode), (2) Alpine/Touring "AT" (alpine boots with walk mode — some good for touring, others just easier walking), (3) Touring (lighter, better ROM, all tech-binding compatible, less ideal for constant lift laps). Terminology: "progressive" flex = gets stiffer as you flex forward; "linear" = resistance stays roughly constant. Many reviewed boots are stiffest models (e.g., 130) of their series — fit/performance notes generally apply to softer (e.g., 120) versions.

**Alpine Boots**
- **Head Raptor WCR 140S PV** — flex 140 (spine bolt → stated 150); last 96 mm; Alpine sole; 2391 g (26.5). Updated; one of best for race-boot-like suspension/support with slightly more approachable fit; lower volume than most 98 "LV" but not as low as Atomic Remedy 130 / (esp.) Nordica Dobermann 5 S; new plastics a tad damper/smoother. For experts prioritizing precision, stiffness, smooth ride. Roomier/lighter → Head Formula 130 LV.
- **Nordica Dobermann 5 Stiff** — 140; 96 mm; Alpine GripWalk w/ tech toe inserts; 2304 g. Race-boot precision/responsiveness without World Cup ultra-slim fit; stiffest of 96-last Dobermanns; lowest-volume of race-inspired 96s here, esp. midfoot/toe box; can soften by removing spine bolts; stiffer/damper than Promachine 130; comparable to Remedy 130 and Raptor WCR 140, with most precise last.
- **Atomic Remedy 130** (new spinoff of Redster) — 130; 96 mm; GripWalk; 2272 g. Nearly identical to new Redster TX 130 but adds vibration-damping bootboard/spoiler and GripWalk soles — more off-piste-friendly race-inspired boot; vs old Redster CS: more consistent stiffness across temperatures but not quite as ultra-smooth in rough snow (nor as smooth as Raptor WCR 140). Comparable to Raptor/Dobermann 5 S. Roomier/lighter → Atomic Hawx.
- **Fischer RC4 Carbon Pro LV BOA** — 130+ (strong 130 / slightly soft 140); 98 mm; GripWalk; 2171 g. Below dedicated race boots: premium plastics, carbon-reinforced cuffs, big power straps, ZipFit cork liners (moldable, add/remove cork for years); lower BOA closure this season (LV and MV). Not as ultra-smooth or low-volume as heavier boots above but similar stiffness; more stiffness/suspension than typical 98-last 130s; ZipFit will outlast other stock liners by seasons (pricey).
- **Fischer RC4 Carbon Pro MV BOA** — 130+ (spine bolt → near 140); 100 mm; GripWalk; 2170 g. Strongest MV boot here; roomy midfoot/toe box; ankle volume adjustable with cork; very stiff for a 130, smoother than lighter boots; BOA gives fine-tunable, uniform lower-shell pressure. High price justified by ZipFit long-term fit.
- **K2 Cortex 140 Zonal BOA** (new) — 140; 96-98 mm; GripWalk w/ tech toe inserts; 2423 g (27.5). Massive departure from K2's light boots; dual-BOA works well; heavy, beefy — excellent power transmission/suspension comparable to Raptor WCR 140, far better than Recon; many tuning options (bolt-on shell stiffeners, front/rear spoilers, cuff alignment, "Ski Edge Sensitivity" shims, firm vs shock-absorbing boot boards); lower volume than Recon LV but may fit wider range than the 96 race boots.
- **Lange Shadow 140 Pro LV** — 140; 97 mm; GripWalk; stated 1986 g. Stiffer sibling of reviewer-loved Shadow 130 LV; extra cuff pivot and unique spine = excellent energy transmission without harshness despite modest weight; less stiff than some above, livelier/less damp; LV last far roomier than 96s, esp. instep/midfoot. Powerful boot for those who can't fit race-inspired models.
- **Salomon S/Pro Race 140** — 140; 96 mm; Alpine; 1933 g. Builds on S/Pro Alpha: stiffer, snugger, but room for higher insteps; not as low-volume as Remedy 130/Dobermann 5 S; "strong 130 / soft 140"; lighter, less damp but not a big outlier. Accessible → S/Pro Alpha 130.
- **Fischer RC4 Free Pro MV BOA** — 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech toe inserts; 2219 g (27.5). Like RC4 Pro MV but toe tech inserts only, more moldable "Vacuum" plastic, lighter parts — for very downhill-oriented BC skiers using hybrid bindings (Marker Duke PT, Salomon Shift, CAST Freetour) with fixed cuff; same roomy fit and ZipFit liner; slightly softer but legit 130. Narrow use case.
- **K2 Recon 140 BOA** — 140; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech toe inserts; 1653 g (25.5). Light Recon with more support; fixed-cuff with tech toes for pin-uphill bindings (Duke PT, Shift, Freetour) — not comfortable as a pivoting cuff but useful; one of lightest fixed-cuff boots here; feels softer than any 140 tested and several stiffer 130s. Minimal weight, moderately snug, skin-track dabbling.
- **Kästle K130P LV** — 130; 97 mm; Alpine; 1996 g. Newcomer competes with race-inspired designs; one of lowest-volume boots here, powerful; also 100 mm K130P and race "R" boots; flex/energy like RC4 Pro LV; heavier Remedy/Raptor/Dobermann better suspension on firm rough snow. Lighter, slightly less damp race precision. Roomier instep → S/Pro Race 140, Shadow 140 Pro LV.
- **Tecnica Mach1 LV 130** (updated) — 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1842 g. Low-volume fit in light package; mid-pack 130 flex, lighter and livelier; not muted, but not harsh; snugger than average 98s esp. instep, but more people can fit than prior even-lower-volume gen; MV and HV variants.
- **Lange Shadow 130 LV** — 130; 97 mm; GripWalk; 1977 g. Go-to boot for a couple of reviewers (fits them); unique pivot layout — noticeably easier ski bending vs traditional 130 in side-by-side test; different flex takes getting used to but addictive; nice suspension; LV considerably higher volume than most LVs (MV also). See full review.
- **Head Formula 130 LV** — 130 (extra spine bolt → "140"); 98 mm; GripWalk; 2052 g. Bridges Raptors and comfort boots; easier to flex than Raptor WCR 140; easier initiation than Mach1 LV 130 then nice progressive ramp; plush liner + moderately heavy shell = smooth damp ride vs K2 Recon 140, S/Pro Alpha 130, Hawx Ultra 130; legit low volume but slightly longer toe box. Balanced middle option.
- **Rossignol Hi-Speed Elite 130 Carbon LV** — 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2053 g. Versatile support/comfort/customization in low-volume package; nicely progressive — softer off the top than Mach1 130 but stiffens faster than K2 Recon 140 and Nordica Speedmachine 130; a touch roomier over instep/toe box than most LVs; liner adds customization... (cont.)
  (Hi-Speed Elite cont.) liner customization at ankle, 1st/5th metatarsals, instep; competitive support/suspension/comfort among 130s — not as stiff/damp as heavy race boots but mutes vibrations better than most lighter boots; MV and HV versions.
- **Nordica Promachine 130** — 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1890 g. Low-volume precision and strong support without bulk; flex comparable to Mach1 130/Formula 130; less smooth than heavier boots; more supportive than light K2 Recon 130 / Head Kaliber 130; good if Mach1 LV doesn't fit; consider Promachine 120 if unsure about 130. Instep too low → S/Pro Alpha.
- **Salomon S/Pro Alpha 130** — 130; 98 mm; Alpine; 1812 g. Twists on LV formula: roomier instep (vs old S/Max) with heel hold via buckle layout, low-volume elsewhere; nicely progressive; Mach1 LV/Promachine slightly more support/smoother; highly customizable (adjustable tongue, heat-moldable shell and liner). More support/precise → S/Pro Race 140.
- **Tecnica Mach1 MV 130** — 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; 2037 g. Flex/ride as stout as many LVs — one of stiffest MV boots here esp. early flex; smoother than lighter boots, better suspension than Mach1 LV 130; Blister experienced shell durability issues after 100+ days (see full review), but a reviewer's daily driver for seasons. Between MV and LV → Lange Shadow 130 LV.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra 130 S BOA** — 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1861 g. Atomic kicked off lightweight-alpine trend; latest Hawx Ultra the best yet; less supportive/damp than Remedy 130 but smoother, more progressive than similar light boots, not soft for a 130; distinctive but predictable rebound; heat-moldable shell, excellent Mimic liner (GEAR:30 #88, #132); one of lowest-volume 98-last boots before customization. Dual-BOA and "XTD" walk versions.
- **Armada AR ONE 130 MV** (new brand to boots) — 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech toe inserts; 2094 g. "Hybrid" shell (neither 2-piece overlap nor 3-piece cabrio); softer first half of flex than 2-piece 130s (Mach1 MV), ramps more than linear 3-piece (K2 FL3X Revolve KF); middle MV last, open-throated lower shell roomy over instep. Blend of both.
- **K2 Recon 130 BOA** — 130; 97-100 mm; GripWalk; 1847 g. Light, fit straddles low/medium volume; lower BOA very effective, easy micro-adjust; flex slightly soft, more linear, less ramp. Stiffer → Recon 140; touring → K2 Mindbender (Recon with walk mode).
- **Head Kaliber 130 MV BOA2** (flagship of Head's new all-mountain line) — 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; 2137 g. No traditional buckles (dual BOA); Blister: BOA shouldn't be deciding factor but is better at fine-tuning lower shell, no significant downside yet for upper-cuff BOA; nice suspension, more accessible flex than average 130; much roomier/forgiving than Mach1 MV/Formula 130; add spine bolt for more support.
- **Rossignol Vizion 4B Elite 130 LV** — 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2053 g. Solves on/off hassle: lever unlocks cuff from lower — step in/out hands-free; substantially softer than most 130s, linear initial travel then ramps late. Strong-flex fans → Hi-Speed. For frustration-free entry, OK with softer flex.
- **Phaenom FS 01 120** (young brand) — 120; 102 mm; GripWalk; 2055 g. Hybrid 2-piece/3-piece shell; linear off top then progressive; notably smooth ride for moderate weight; stiffer than soft 130s (Kaliber, Vizion) yet more forgiving initially than legit 2-piece 130s; wide last but simple buckle layout locks heel well; big elastic power strap, replaceable components. Vs AR ONE 130: AR ONE a touch narrower and stiffer deep; FS 01 more progressive, lower instep, wider midfoot.
- **Salomon S/Pro Supra Dual BOA 120** — 120; 100-106 mm; GripWalk; 1850 g. Many brands debut dual-BOA boots this season; light medium-volume; works for medium-high volume feet; suspension like S/Pro Alpha but more forgiving; slightly soft 120 but not an outlier like Vizion/Kaliber. Reviewers somewhat prefer lower BOA over buckles; less consensus on upper BOA; Supra offered with all three closure options.
- **Rossignol Pure Elite 120** (women's) — 120; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1707 g (24.5). One of stiffest women's boots tested without a "brick wall" — progressive, easy start then ramps; damp/smooth; low-volume but roomier instep/toe box than Promachine and Mach1 LV; Shadow 115 LV fits similarly but ramps earlier; Pure Elite stiffer deep; removable spine bolts soften flex. For intermediates growing and experienced skiers.
- **Nordica Promachine 115 W** — 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1690 g (24.5). Precise, powerful; consistently low-volume — snugger ankle/instep than Shadow LV, K2 Anthem BOA, Dalbello Cabrio LV; flex true to rating; energetic; stronger than K2 Anthem 115 and Fischer RC4 105. For stiff/low-volume fans who can't fit race boots and find unisex 130s too stiff. Alternative last → Mach1 LV 115.
- **Tecnica Mach1 LV 115 W** (updated) — 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1663 g (24.5). Tecnica's strongest/narrowest women's all-mountain; fit comparable to Hawx Ultra 115 and Promachine 115; maybe stiffest of the three; choose by fit. Wider → Mach1 MV 115 W.
- **Lange Shadow 115 W LV** — 115; 97 mm; GripWalk; 1769 g (24.5). Shadow pivot design in accessible flex and smaller sizes; exceptional power transmission without harshness; fits like Lange RS LV with snugger heel; not nearly as low-volume as Mach1 LV/Promachine/Hawx Ultra (esp. instep), similar to Pure Elite; efficient power transfer, smooth, snappy; LV and MV 115 women's versions.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra 115 BOA W** — 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1840 g (26.5). Very low-volume but easily customized (Mimic liner, heat-moldable shell — can create notable midfoot room); one of lowest-volume women's boots; slightly softer than Mach1 LV 115/Promachine 115 but progressive and supportive; a tad damper. Roomier → Hawx Prime 115 BOA W.
- **Dalbello Cabrio LV 115 W** — 115; 99 mm; GripWalk; 1727 g (24.5). 3-piece, low-volume except much more accommodating instep than LV 2-piece boots; heat-moldable 3DWrap liner eliminates shin slop; pivoting tongue easy entry; more linear flex — stiffer off top, less stiff deep vs strong 2-piece 115s; one of stiffest women's 3-piece options.
- **K2 Anthem 115 BOA** — 115; 97-100 mm; GripWalk; 1730 g (24.5). Recon construction in smaller sizes; BOA + wrapping lower adapts 97-100; low-volume reviewer got snug fit, also could suit medium-volume; predictable smooth flex, not stiffest 115 but close. Stiffer → Anthem 125 BOA.
- **Salomon S/Pro Race 110** (unisex, down to 22.5) — 110; 96 mm; Alpine; 1787 g (24.5). Notably low-volume — for LV fans needing more precision; not drastically narrower than Promachine 115/Mach1 LV 115; heat-moldable shell/liner; stiffness like some 115s (Hawx Ultra 115), more supportive than Anthem 115.
- **Fischer RC4 105 MV BOA** — 105; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1686 g (24.5). Adjustability and more power than rating suggests; "Vacuum" moldable plastic; roomy toe box but BOA snugs enough for 98-last reviewers; roomier than LVs, less than next two MVs; flex comparable to Promachine 115 W / Mach1 LV 115 W.
- **Salomon S/Pro Supra BOA 105 W** — 105; 100-106 mm; GripWalk; 1772 g (25.5). Adaptable shape for wider feet; better chance for high-volume feet; BOA gives even snug lower fit; RC4 105 MV lower-volume and stiffer; decent suspension, heat-moldable shell/liner; adjustable spine "direct" vs "progressive." 115-like → new S/Pro Supra Dual BOA 115 W or S/Pro Alpha 130 (to 22.5).
- **Head Kaliber 105 W MV BOA2** — 105; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1979 g (24.5). Stiffest women's Kaliber; no buckles; Kalibers softer than rating (feels ~95) but engaging without speed; roomy for MV, may suit near-high-volume feet; dual BOA (also buckle and lower-BOA-only versions). More support → RC4 105 MV; low-volume/support → Head Formula 105 W LV.

**Alpine/Touring (AT) Boots**
- **Scarpa 4-Quattro Pro** — 130+; 100 mm (fits like others' LV, esp. instep/midfoot); GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1531 g. Very stiff, surprisingly light; blurs AT/Touring; stiffer than 4-Quattro XT — one of stiffest walk-mode boots tested; walks very well for stiffness (a bit less ROM than XT); not as smooth as heavier boots on firm inbounds. Very stiff touring or light precise AT setup.
- **Dalbello Cabrio LV Free 130** — 130; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1885 g. Well-rounded AT for 3-piece fans; like old Lupo but more solid cuff-to-shell connection in ski mode, dual-density tongue adds ROM; skis like fixed-cuff 3-piece; linear flex absorbs mid-travel, less ramp; wrap liner limits ROM but snug high cuff. Lighter → Roxa R3 130.
- **Tecnica Cochise 130** — 130; 99 mm (HV 102 also); GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1832 g. Latest of the AT-defining line; generous ROM (stated 50°) yet skis like a light fixed-cuff alpine boot even in demanding inbounds conditions (PU shell, burly liner); Zero G Tour Pro far lighter/walks better; one of best 1-boot quivers for aggressive skiers.
- **Lange XT3 Free 130 LV** — 130; 97 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1777 g. Balanced; light/mobile enough for fairly long tours but skis much better than light touring boots (PU shell, thick liner, tall cuff); fits like RS LV/Shadow LV (roomier instep); MV version; too roomy → Hawx Ultra XTD. More uphill → XT3 Tour 2.0 (similar downhill).
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra XTD 130 BOA** — 130; 98 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1791 g. Top pick for low-volume mostly-resort boot with occasional skinning; heavier than touring boots but much better downhill/suspension; shockingly small difference from fixed-cuff Hawx Ultra 130; stated 54° ROM but not for long tours (→ Backland XTD); adaptable fit. Wider → Hawx Prime XTD.
- **Salomon Shift Alpha BOA 130** — 130; 98-104 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1799 g. One boot for everything; S/Pro Alpha-like low-volume last but BOA, higher instep, heat-moldable shell = medium-volume possible; walk mechanism/instep strap like S/Lab MTN Summit — impressive heel hold, respectable ROM; slightly softer off top, quicker rebound than Hawx Ultra XTD/Cochise (subtle). Down to size 22.5.
- **Atomic Hawx Prime XTD 130 BOA** — 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1873 g. Between Ultra (low) and Magna (high) lasts; mirrors fixed-cuff Hawx Prime 130 — legit 130; roomier than Ultra; not quite as plush as dedicated alpine boots but close; much damper/stiffer than Backland XTD 120. Light medium-volume → Tigard 130, Roxa R3 130 (Prime more comfortable in firm crud with similar ROM).
- **Dynafit Tigard 130** — 130; 101 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1702 g (27.5). Downhill-stability-first Dynafit with touring tech; a bit lighter than rivals; substantial flex/power, slightly softer/less progressive than Cochise 130; Hoji-Lock loosens entire upper cuff in one motion (fit-dependent); excellent fore-aft ROM for 4-buckle; one of more comfortable AT boots to skin in. Mostly BC → Dynafit Radical Pro.
- **Roxa R3 130 T.I.** — 130; 99-101 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1580 g. Uncommon mix of low weight, stout flex, mobility; 3-piece for linear-but-strong flex and upright stance fans; one of lightest AT boots; remove tongue for long uphills = tons of ROM; consistently stiff (two settings), less (adjustable) forward lean — polarizing; tongue or wrap Intuition liner (wrap better for resort), heat-moldable. More suspension → Cabrio LV Free 130.
- **K2 Mindbender 130 BOA** — 130; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1961 g. Recon/Anthem downhill experience with uphill capability; low-volume out of box but BOA/heat-moldable for medium feet; flex nearly identical to Recon 130 — softer, more linear than Cochise 130/Hawx Ultra XTD; average ROM; thick liner + PU lower less harsh than light boots. Lighter/uphill → K2 Dispatch.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra XTD 115 BOA W** — 115; 98 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1616 g (24.5). Excellent low-volume women's AT; skis like fixed-cuff Hawx Ultra 115 W; not lightest but respectable ROM, less harsh than touring boots on firm; easily customized. Higher volume → Cochise 115 W, Hawx Prime XTD 115 W; lighter → Scarpa 4-Quattro XT W.
- **Tecnica Cochise 115 W** — 115; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1643 g (24.5). Reviewer go-to travel setup; excellent 1-boot quiver for hard inbounds skiing plus some touring; more medium-volume over instep/midfoot/toe box than Mindbender/Hawx Ultra XTD but slim ankle; stiffer than several fixed-cuff women's alpine boots. Lower volume → Hawx Ultra XTD 115; mostly skinning → Zero G Tour Pro W.
- **Scarpa 4-Quattro XT W** — 115; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1323 g (24.5). Outlier: walks like Touring boots (Zero G Tour Pro W) with AT-like supportive flex (slightly poorer suspension in rough); low-volume instep/midfoot. Suspension priority → Hawx Ultra XTD 115 W, Cochise 115 W.
- **Salomon Shift Alpha BOA 115 W** — 115; 98-104 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1665 g (24.5). Strong downhill, occasional skinning; not quite as supportive/damp as Cochise 115 W/Hawx Ultra XTD 115 W but near women's 115 alpine boots; walks a bit better than those; customizable for low/medium volume; similar to K2 Mindbender 115 W but notably lighter, ramps quicker. Stiffer → Shift Alpha BOA 130 (to 22.5).
- **K2 Mindbender 115 BOA W** — 115; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1858 g (24.5). Nice suspension, occasional climbs, adaptable fit — can get very snug heel/ankle; not light, average ROM; may fit medium-volume more than 4-Quattro XT W/Hawx Ultra XTD 115 W; a touch softer than other 115 AT boots but more plush. Stiffer → Mindbender 125 BOA W.
- (Ad: BLISTER+ testimonial — tree collision in Taos covered; $395/year.)

**Touring Boots**
- **Lange XT3 Tour 2.0 130** — 130; 99 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; stated 1450 g. Lighter XT3 Free with impressively similar downhill; nearly identical walk mechanism; ROM below Zero G/Maestrale but pretty good, with excellent rearward support; standout predictable progressive flex that rebounds like heavier non-Grilamid boots; full-rubber Vibram sole better for scrambling. For those who dislike how touring boots flex differently from alpine boots.
- **Tecnica Zero G Tour Pro** — 130; 99 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1354 g (27.5). Main reference for "freeride touring": exceptional up/down combo; less progressive/smooth than heavy boots but seriously impressive descent for weight — enough support for big skiers on big touring skis; walks far better than nearly every AT boot at weight rivaling softer touring boots. Alternatives: Scarpa 4-Quattro XT, Maestrale.
- **La Sportiva Sender** (new, most downhill-oriented Sportiva) — 130; 102.5 mm (fits like ~100); GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1504 g. Better ROM than most 130 touring boots; softer, more linear forward flex than most 130s (considerably softer than previous two) — forgiving in tricky snow at slow speeds; notably stiffer laterally and rearward. Not for alpine-like 2-piece 130 feel.
- **K2 Dispatch Pro** — 130; 100 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1568 g (27.5). Stiffest Dispatch; elements of Mindbender with much less weight; light breathable BOA-equipped liner for on-the-go adjustment; Pebax shell/cuff progressive and respectably stiff; softer than Zero G Tour Pro / XT3 Tour 2.0 but more supportive than <1300 g boots; no notable shortcomings. Lighter → Maestrale RS, Radical Pro.
- **Scarpa Maestrale RS** — 125; 102 mm (feels smaller esp. ankle/instep); Touring w/ tech inserts; 1380 g. Freeride touring mainstay 10+ years; not quite as stiff as Zero G Tour Pro/XT3 Tour 2.0 but plenty to drive big skis; progressive flex/lateral support familiar from alpine boots vs uphill boots like Scarpa F1 LT; roomier fit; slightly above-average uphill. Roomier → Radical Pro; lower-volume/more support → 4-Quattro.
- **Dynafit Radical Pro** — 120; 103.5 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1483 g. Downhill-oriented, roomier fit, still uphill efficient; more supportive/progressive than many <1300 g boots; ROM a bit better than average for 120-130 touring boots; Hoji Lock (walk mechanism integrated with upper buckle/power strap tension; fit-dependent)... (cont.)
  (Radical Pro cont.) forward flex doesn't match previous few boots but notably stiffer laterally than Backland XTD 120 / Dalbello Quantum Free Pro; nicely progressive like many 110-120 alpine boots.
- **Tecnica Zero G Tour Pro W** — 125; 99 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1165 g (24.5). Stiffest women-specific touring boot tested; same features as men's; one of first to try for alpine-like flex; similar or slightly better ROM than Scarpa Gea RS / K2 Dispatch W but lighter and stiffer; fairly low-volume ankle/toe box (some high-instep reviewers fine). Remarkable up/down combo.
- **Scarpa Gea RS** (women's Maestrale RS) — 120; 102 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1270 g (25.0). Generous ROM, supportive; walks well, smooth; comparable uphill to Zero G Tour Pro W but forward flex softer, less progressive; slightly wider last — good for medium-volume feet; ratchet ankle strap and minimal lower buckle cable lock down foot (after heat-molding Intuition liner).
- **K2 Dispatch W** — 100; 100 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1351 g (24.5). Mirrors Dispatch Pro, softer, smaller sizes; stated 60° ROM; forgiving progressive flex; balanced; medium-volume (roomier than Zero G Tour Pro W, ~Gea RS); heat-moldable shell/liner for wide range incl. higher volume. For those not wanting a very stiff touring boot.
- **Atomic Backland XTD Carbon 120** — 120; 100 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1372 g. Bridges uphill Backland and resort Hawx XTD; forward flex fairly rated but notably less rigid laterally, less precise than prior 120-130 boots; middle ground; "nearly endless ROM" feels nicer uphill than heavier boots. For those wanting more mobility than freeride-touring boots.
- **Dalbello Quantum Free Pro** — 130; 100 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1367 g (27.5). Paper promises more than delivered: feels like boots of its weight, not its rating — softer, more linear forward flex than Zero G Tour Pro, Ridge Pro, even lighter S/Lab MTN Summit; considerably less powerful but respectable lateral; plenty of ROM; very low instep, moderately wide heel/toe box. More support → Ridge Pro, MTN Summit.
- **Dynafit Ridge Pro** (Hoji collab) — 120; 101 mm; Touring w/ tech inserts; 1376 g (27.5). Excellent fore-aft ROM, competitive with <1300 g boots; quick transitions with Hoji Lock (still some buckle/strap adjusting); stiffer forward/lateral/rearward than nearly all similarly efficient boots (Quantum Free Pro, Kilo XTR, Crux Pro); lower volume than Tigard, esp. instep. Impressive support for uphill feel; not very progressive.
- **La Sportiva Kilo XTR** (new) — 120; 100.5 mm; Tech-binding only; 1352 g (28.5). More descent-oriented Kilo; keeps Kilo's excellent ROM; adds downhill performance, esp. lateral stiffness when carving; fore/aft like Quantum Free / Crux Pro; less supportive/progressive than Zero G Tour Pro.
- **Head Crux Pro** — 130 (two upper buckles); 99 mm; Tech-binding only; 1273 g. Seems to compete with freeride touring boots but fore/aft power closer to high-performance 2-buckle boots (Quantum Free Pro, Kilo XTR); softer/more linear than MTN Summit/Ridge Pro; excellent ROM; excellent, padded liner; lower-volume with roomy toe box.
- **Salomon S/Lab MTN Summit** — 120; 99 mm; Tech-binding only; 1166 g. Outlier in 1000-1300 g class: ~75° ROM, sub-1200 g, walks incredibly well and skis quite well; progressive forward flex, rating fairly accurate (unlike Quantum Free Pro/Crux Pro); best in smoother snow; medium-volume. More uphill → Zero G Peak Carbon, Scarpa F1 LT; comparable downhill → Backland XTD 120, Ridge Pro.
- **Tecnica Zero G Peak Carbon** — flex not listed; 99 mm; Tech-binding only; 1077 g (27.5). Favorite in ~1 kg class; far lighter/more efficient than Zero G Tour Pro, low-friction stride; stiff enough to drive fat skis in soft snow (for those used to this class); not drastically better than F1 LT but lighter and competitive. Stiffer with wider toe box → MTN Summit.
- **Atomic Backland Carbon** — 110; 98 mm; Tech-binding only; 1112 g (28). Not full skimo but uphill-biased vs >1200 g boots; quick closure/walk mode, walks very well (open-throat lower shell); enough power from neutral stance; surprisingly progressive; gives up forward/lateral support — aggressive skiers go heavier; heat-moldable shell/liner — very fit-versatile.
- **Scarpa F1 LT** — 100; 100 mm; Tech-binding only; 1104 g (28). Still a standout ~1 kg boot for those sacrificing descent for efficiency; adequate for a 200-lb skier on 120 mm skis in soft/consistent big terrain; harder fast on firm; learning curve from stiff alpine boots; downhill as good as boots 100-200 g heavier; nearly effortless ROM.
- **La Sportiva Kilo** — 110; 100.5 mm; Tech-binding only; 1194 g (28.5) (stated ~1000 g @26.5). Long tours; very nice to walk, quick transitions; cam power strap helps; still softer end of ~1 kg boots (softer than F1 LT, Zero G Peak); best with <100 mm skis in soft snow; roomier heel/ankle, runs short per size. Try Kilo XTR too (skis better, similar mobility; Kilo saves 100-200 g/foot).
- **Dalbello Quantum Evo** — flex not listed; 101 mm; Tech-binding only; 1126 g (27.5). Lightest, most uphill Dalbello; extremely low instep, moderately wide heel/toe; good ROM, quick-release power strap/upper buckle; among least powerful — nearly too soft to drive >100 mm skis in soft snow at moderate speed. Most should consider other light boots; low-instep fans → Ridge Pro.
- (Ad: BLISTER+ testimonial — PT and X-ray covered.)

### Touring Bindings & Climbing Skins (src ~12156-)
Section overview (Paul Forward photos): BC world confusing; besides skis/boots, decision-making, risk assessment and trusted partners matter; bindings and skins matter more because most BC time is uphill. Tested bindings back-to-back on same ski models; many seasons comparing skins. First five binding options match alpine bindings downhill; others far lighter for long days; several in between. "A very good time to be a backcountry skier."

**Touring Bindings** (MSRP and measured weight given in guide)
- **CAST Freetour 2.0 Pivot 15** — $779; total system 1428 g (115 mm brakes); on ski while skinning 1016 g. Pioneer of "hybrid" concept: skin in pin toe, ski down in Look Pivot 15/18 alpine binding on CAST hardware (brake lock, two heel risers, swap-on touring toe); most refined, competitive weight, toe-plate release with few icing risk areas; not lightest or quickest to transition but ski down in one of most trusted alpine bindings; extremely durable.
- **Marker Duke PT 16** — $749; total 1383 g (125 mm brakes); 1074 g skinning. Hybrid in 11/13/16 DIN; flip alpine toe forward and lock, or remove it; transitions more involved than Shift2, a tad finickier than Freetour when removing toe; good up/down experience; Duke PT 11 much lighter (~850 g uphill mode) for BC-only; 13/16 preferred for frequent inbounds; reliable over many days. Kingpin if no lifts.
- **Tyrolia Attack Hybrid 14 MN/PT** (new) — $629; total 1256 g (110 mm brakes); 883 g skinning. Tyrolia's first hybrid: Attack alpine + Almonte touring; swap toe pieces on quick-release plate like Attack Demo (thousands of days of reliability); a bit more prone to icing (pole whacks fix); lightest on-ski weight of hybrids; "emergency mode" to cautiously ski down in uphill toes as last resort; performs similarly to competitors.
- **Salomon / Atomic / Armada Shift2 13 MN** — $679; 926 g (120 mm brakes). Single toe converts pin ↔ alpine (no swapping pieces); second season: largely fixed original Shift's issues — brakes stay up while skinning, no pre-releases, AFD stays set, taller single riser; toe lever can be damaged if forced (rare). 10 and 13 DIN.
- **Daymaker Classic Adapters & Tekdapters** — $279 each; Classic 738 g, Tekdapters 540 g per adapter. Not bindings — adapters to skin uphill in regular alpine bindings/skis; no drilling, cheaper; heavy, worse on firm sidehill skin tracks; Classic works with almost any boot/binding; Tekdapters need tech-toe boots, lighter, near-frictionless pivot, more compact/torsionally rigid. Easiest/most affordable entry to uphill.
- **Marker Kingpin 13** — $769; 775 g (75-100 mm brakes).
